The l l -adic bifiltered El Zein-Steenbrink-Zucker complex of a proper SNCL scheme with a relative SNCD
Yukiyoshi Nakkajima

Abstract




Abstract.—

The aim of this paper is to give the log
l
-adic
relative monodromy-weight conjecture and
to prove that this conjecture is true in certain cases.
This conjecture is a generalization of the
famous
l
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture due to P. Deligne and K. Kato.
To give our conjecture, we use our theory of the derived category of bifiltered complexes
which is a generalization of theory of the derived category of bifiltered complexes in

[
Il1
]
,
[
D2
]
,
[
D4
]
and the derived category of filtered complexes in

[
B2
]
(and
[
NS
]
).

References

1
Introduction

Let
V
be an object of an abelian category
with a finite increasing filtration
W
and
let
N\colon(V,W)\longrightarrow(V,W)
be a nilpotent filtered endomorphism.
In
[
D5
]
Deligne has proved that
there exists at most one monodromy filtration
relative to
W
. That is, there exists at most
one finite increasing filtration
M
on
V

such that
N(M_{k}V)\subset M_{k-2}V

(\forall k\in{\mathbb{Z}})
and
such that
N^{e}\colon V\longrightarrow V
induces an isomorphism

N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}^{M}_{k+e}{\rm gr}^{W}_{k}V\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}^{M}_{k-e}{\rm gr}^{W}_{k}V

for any
e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
.
Let
U
be the complement of a smooth divisor
D

defined by an equation
t=0

in a smooth scheme
X
over a finite field
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.
Let
{\cal F}
be a smooth
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-sheaf on
U
tamely ramified along
D
.
Let
{\cal F}[D]
be the restriction to
D

of a smooth extension
\overline{\cal F}
on
X[t^{1/n}]

((\exists n,q)=1)

of the pull-back of
{\cal F}
to
X[t^{1/n}]
(Abyankar’s lemma).
Let
\iota\colon\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}
be
an isomorphism of fields.
Assume that
{\cal F}
has a finite increasing filtration

W
of smooth
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-subsheaves such that

{\rm gr}_{k}^{W}{\cal F}
is exactly
\iota
-pure of weight
k
.
In [loc. cit.] Deligne has proved that there exists
the monodromy filtration
M
on
{\cal F}[D]

relative to
W
and that
{\rm gr}_{k}^{M}{\cal F}[D]
is
exactly
\iota
-pure of weight
k
.
Pursuing an
\infty
-adic analogue of this, he has defined
the variation of mixed Hodge structures
(V,W,F)

over the punctured unit disk
\Delta^{*}
over the complex number field
and he has posed a problem defining a class

(V,W,F)
with the logarithm
N

of a unipotent monodromy of
V
such
that there exists a monodromy filtration

M
on
V
relative to
W

(See
[
SZ
]
,
[
Kas
]
and
[
As
]

for a more expanded statement:
the admissibility of variations of mixed Hodge structures.).

Let
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
be a projective strict semistable family
with horizontal SNCD(=simple normal crossing divisor)
\overset{\circ}{\cal D}
over
the unit disk
\overset{\circ}{\Delta}
.
In
[
SZ
]
(resp.
[
E
]
)
Steenbrink and Zucker (resp. El Zein)
have given an answer for Deligne’s problem
in this good geometric case.
Let
\overset{\circ}{X}
and
\overset{\circ}{D}
be the special fibers of
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}

and
\overset{\circ}{\cal D}
, respectively.
To construct the monodromy filtration on the
suitable log cohomology of

(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{D})
with coefficient
{\mathbb{Q}}

relative to the weight filtration arising from
\overset{\circ}{D}
,
they have used the weight filtration arising from
both
\overset{\circ}{X}
and
\overset{\circ}{D}
.
One can prove that the weight filtration
has two characterizing properties of the relative monodromy filtration by using
a certain double complex and using M. Saito’s result
which tells us that the monodromy filtration coincides with
the weight filtration in the case
D=\emptyset
(
[
SaM1
]
).
Consequently the relative monodromy filtration exists in this geometric case.
This main result in
[
SZ
]
and
[
E
]
is a generalization of
that in
[
St
]
(whose complete proof has been given by M. Saito (
[
SaM1
]
)).
Influenced by their work,
M. Saito has constructed the category of mixed Hodge modules
(cf.
[
SaM2
, Introduction]
).

In this paper we give an
l
-adic version of
(a generalization of) El Zein-Steenbrink-Zucker’s work
in the equal characteristic case
p>0
(without no conjecture)
and in the mixed characteristics case
under the assumption of the log
l
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture
in the mixed characteristics case.
In order to give the
l
-adic version,
we use theory of the derived categories of bifiltered complexes in this paper,
theory of the log geometry of Fontaine-Illusie-Kato in
[
Ka1
]
and
theory of log étale cohomologies in
[
Nak1
]
.
Our theory of the derived categories of bifiltered complexes
can be considered as a generalization of the theory of filtered derived categories in

[
B2
]
(and
[
NS
]
) and the theory of bifiltered derived categories in
[
D4
]
.

In order to give relations between our work and preceding results in the
l
-adic geometric case,
let us recall the case where
W
is the trivial filtration, i.e.,
W_{k-1}V=0
and
W_{k}V=V
for
some
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
in the
l
-adic geometric case.

Let
\overset{\circ}{S}
be the spectrum of
a henselian discrete valuation ring
{\cal V}
.
Let
\overline{K}
be a separable closure of
K
and
let
\overline{\cal V}
be the integral closure of
{\cal V}
in
K
.
Set
\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}:={\rm Spec}(\overline{\cal V})
.
Let
\overline{\eta}
be the generic point of
\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}

and
let
\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}
be the closed point of
\overset{\circ}{\overline{S}}
.
Let
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
be a proper strict semistable family
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Let
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}}

(resp.
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}}
)
be the geometric special fiber (resp. the geometric generic fiber) of

\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
For a nonnegative integer
j
, let

(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}})^{(j)}
be the disjoint union of
the
(j+1)
-fold intersections of the irreducible components of

\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}}
.
Let
a^{(j)}\colon(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}})^{(j)}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}}
be the natural morphism.
Let
l
be a fixed prime number which is different from the characteristic of
the residue field. In
[
RZ
]

Rapoport and Zink have constructed the projective system

\{(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}},P)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
of
filtered complexes of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}
-modules in
the étale topos

(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}})_{\rm et}
of
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}}

such that

{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}\simeq\underset{j\geq{\rm max}\{-k,0\}}{\bigoplus}a^{(2j+k)}_{*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}})^{(2j+k)}})(-j-k)[-2j-k]

in the derived category

{\rm D}^{\rm b}_{\rm ctf}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}},{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

and such that there exists a compatible family of isomorphisms

\theta_{n}\colon R\Psi({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}
’s
in

{\rm D}^{\rm b}_{\rm ctf}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}},{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
’s,
where
R\Psi({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
is the nearby cycle sheaf
of
{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}
on
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}/\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Consequently we have the following
l
-adic weight spectral sequence of

{\rm Gal}(\overline{\eta}/\eta)
-modules:

(1.0.1)

E^{-k,q+k}_{1}:=\underset{j\geq{\rm max}\{-k,0\}}{\bigoplus}H^{q-2j-k}_{\rm et}((\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}})^{(2j+k)},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})(-j-k)\Longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{N}}).

(See also some remarks in
[
Nakk2
]

about this spectral sequence.)
However, unfortunately they have not defined their filtered complexes
as well-defined objects of what category.
One should consider them as objects of
the derived categories

{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}_{\rm ctf}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}},{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
’s of
bounded filtered complexes of
{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}
’s-modules
whose graded complexes have
finite tor-dimension in
the étale topos
(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}})_{\rm et}
such that
the cohomological sheaves of the graded complexes are constructible.
(See
[
Il1
]
,
[
D4
]
,
[
B2
]
and
[
NS
]

for the definition of the filtered derived category.)
In fact, one should show that

\{(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}},P)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
defines
a well-defined object of the projective 2-limit

{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}_{\rm ctf}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})

of
{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}_{\rm ctf}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{s}}},{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
’s.
(If one ignores torsion, the induced filtration on

R\Psi({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})[d]
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
on
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}/\overset{\circ}{S}

(twisted by
d
) by
P
is the well-defined monodromy filtration
by the
l
-adic monodromy operator on the perverse sheaf

R\Psi({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})[d]:=(R\varprojlim_{n}R\Psi({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}))\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
,
where
d=\dim\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{s}}
(
[
Il2
]
).)
In the log crystalline case one has to determine the analogous filtered complex to

\{(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}},P)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

as an object of what category
because the analogous filtered complex depends on the affine covering of

\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{s}}
and the local log smooth embedding a priori
(see
[
Nakk5
]
and
[
Nakk6
]
for details).

Let
P
be the induced filtration on

H^{q}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
by (
1.0.1
).
By
[
SGA 7-I
]
(cf.
[
RZ
]
) the action of
{\rm Gal}(\overline{\eta}/\eta)

on
H^{q}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
is tame.
Let
N\colon H^{q}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-1)

be the
l
-adic monodromy operator.
P. Deligne has conjectured (what is called,
the
l
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture in the case where

\overset{\circ}{S}
is of mixed characteristics)
that
N
induces an isomorphism

(1.0.2)

N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}_{q+e}^{P}H^{q}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}_{q-e}^{P}H^{q}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-e)\quad(e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}),

where
P
on
H^{q}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
is
the induced filtration by (
1.0.1
) (cf.
[
D1
]
).
In the case where
\overset{\circ}{S}
is of equal characteristic
p>0
, this conjecture is true by

[
D5
]
and
[
It1
]
. In the case where
\overset{\circ}{S}
is of mixed characteristics,
this conjecture has not yet been solved, though this has been proved to be true in special cases
(e.g.,
[
RZ
]
,
[
It2
]
,
[
Scho
]
).

In
[
Nak3
]
C. Nakayama has generalized Rapoport-Zink’s result above as follows.

Let
s
be the log point of a field
\kappa
,
i.e.,
s=({\rm Spec}(\kappa),{\mathbb{N}}\oplus\kappa^{*}\longrightarrow\kappa)
,
where the morphism
{\mathbb{N}}\oplus\kappa^{*}\longrightarrow\kappa
is defined by

(n,a)\longmapsto 0^{n}a

(n\in{\mathbb{N}},a\in\kappa^{*})
, where
0^{0}=1\in\kappa
and
0^{n}=0
for
n\not=0
.
Let
\kappa_{\rm sep}
be a separable closure of
\kappa
and
set
\overline{s}:=s\otimes_{\kappa}{\kappa}_{\rm sep}
.
Let
X
be a proper SNCL(=simple normal crossing log) scheme over
s
defined in
[
Nakk1
]
.
Set
X_{\overline{s}}:=X\times_{s}\overline{s}
.
For a log scheme
Y
, let
\overset{\circ}{Y}
be the underlying scheme of
Y
.
For a nonnegative integer
j
, let

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(j)}_{\overline{s}}
be the disjoint union of
the
(j+1)
-fold intersections of the irreducible components of

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\overline{s}}
.
Let
l
be a prime number which is different from the characteristic of
\kappa
.
For a commutative monoid
Q
with unit element, denote
by
{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[Q])
a log scheme
whose underlying scheme is

{\rm Spec}({\mathbb{Z}}[Q])

and whose log structure is the association of
a natural inclusion
Q\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{Z}}[Q]
.
Set
\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}}:=\overline{s}\times_{{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[{\mathbb{N}}])}{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-m}{\mathbb{N}}])

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})

and
\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}:=\varprojlim_{m}\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}}
.
For an fs(=fine and saturated) log scheme
Y
over
s
,
set
\overline{Y}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}:=Y\times_{s}\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}
.
In
[
Nak3
]
Nakayama has constructed
the projective system of filtered complexes producing
the following
l
-adic weight spectral sequence whose
convergent term is the Kummer log étale cohomology

H^{q}_{\rm ket}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})
(
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
)
defined in
[
FK
]
and
[
Nak1
]
:

(1.0.3)

E^{-k,q+k}_{1}:=\underset{j\geq{\rm max}\{-k,0\}}{\bigoplus}H^{q-2j-k}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}{}^{(2j+k)},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})(-j-k)\Longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm ket}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}).

However he has not defined his filtered complexes as objects of what category;
one should define them as objects of
the derived categories
{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}_{\rm ctf}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
’s of
bounded filtered complexes of
{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}
’s-modules
whose graded complexes have finite tor-dimension in
the étale topos
\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}_{\rm et}
such that
the cohomological sheaves of the graded complexes are constructible.
If
X
is the special fiber of
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}

with canonical log structure, then
K. Fujiwara and K. Kato have proved that

(1.0.4)

H^{q}_{\rm ket}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})=H^{q}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})

(
[
FK
]
) (See also
[
Nak2
]
,
[
Nak3
]
and
[
Il3
]
.).
Moreover we see that
Nakayama has essentially proved that his filtered complexes
are isomorphic to
\{(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}},P)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

in
{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}_{\rm ctf}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})

in
[
Nak3
]
.
In
[
Nakk2
]
we have proved that
the weight spectral sequence (
1.0.1
)
is isomorphic to
the weight spectral sequence (
1.0.3
).
As a result, (
1.0.3
) turns out to be
a generalization of (
1.0.1
).
In [loc. cit.] he has also proved
the degeneration at
E_{2}
modulo torsion of (
1.0.3
).
Let

N\colon H^{q}_{\rm ket}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm ket}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-1)

be the
l
-adic monodromy operator.
In
[
Kat
]
(see also
[
Nak3
]
,
[
Nakk2
]
)
Kato has conjectured the following,
which we call
the
log
l
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture
:

Conjecture 1.1

(
Log
l
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture (
[
Kat
]
)
)
.

If
\overset{\circ}{X}
is projective over
\kappa
,
then
N
induces an isomorphism

(1.1.1)

N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}_{q+e}^{P}H^{q}_{\rm ket}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}_{q-e}^{P}H^{q}_{\rm ket}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-e)\quad(e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}),

where
P
on
H^{q}_{\rm ket}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
is
the induced filtration by (
1.0.3
).

The aim of this paper is to give a generalized
l
-adic version of
El Zein-Steenbrink-Zucker’s work by using Nakayama’s results in
[
Nak3
]
and
general results for the derived category of
bifiltered complexes developed in this paper.
Our work is a generalization of Nakayama’s work.
Our generalization is technically
more difficult than the generalization
[
SZ
]
and
[
E
]
of

[
St
]
because torsion sheaves appear in our case
(the existence of the derived tensor product of two bounded above
(bi)filtered complexes
is necessary in our case).
The main result in this paper is to give
the log
l
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture (
1.2
) below;
we construct an expected
l
-adic relative monodromy filtration
on the
l
-adic Kummer log étale cohomology in a geometric way.

In the rest of this introduction,
we give a quick explanation for
our key bifiltered complex and we state
the log
l
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture.

Let
D
be an SNCD on
X/s
defined in
[
NY
]
.
Let
M(D)
be the sheaf of invertible functions of
\overset{\circ}{X}

outside
\overset{\circ}{D}

in the étale topos
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et}
.
For a positive integer
j
,
let
\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(j)}
be the disjoint union of
the
j
-fold intersections of the irreducible components of

\overset{\circ}{D}
. Endow
\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(j)}
with the pull-back of
the log structure of
X
and let
D^{(j)}
be
the resulting log scheme. Set
D^{(0)}:=X
.
Set
(X,D):=X\times_{\overset{\circ}{X}}(\overset{\circ}{X},M(D))
and

(X_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}}):=X_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}}\times_{\overset{\circ}{X}}(\overset{\circ}{X},M(D))

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
Set
\overline{D}:=D\times_{s}\overline{s}
and
(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}):=\varprojlim_{m}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}})
.
Let
{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{2}_{\rm ctf}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})
be the derived category
of bounded bifiltered complexes of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}
-modules
whose graded complexes have
finite tor-dimension in
the étale topos
\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}_{\rm et}
such that
the cohomological sheaves of the graded complexes are constructible.
(In the text we give the definition of

{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{2}_{\rm ctf}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})
.)
We construct a bifiltered complex

(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P):=\{(A_{l^{n}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\in{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{2}_{\rm ctf}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})

which plays a key role in this paper.
We call
(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)

the
l
-adic bifiltered El Zein-Steenbrink-Zucker complex
of
(\overline{X},\overline{D})/\overline{s}
.
Let
\pi_{(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})}\colon(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})\longrightarrow(\overline{X},\overline{D})
be a natural morphism and
let
\epsilon_{(\overline{X},\overline{D})}\colon(\overline{X},\overline{D})\longrightarrow(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}},\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}})
be
a morphism forgetting the log structure of
(\overline{X},\overline{D})
.
Then we prove that there exists a natural isomorphism

(1.1.2)

\displaystyle\theta\colon R(\epsilon_{(\overline{X},\overline{D})}\pi_{(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})})_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}A_{l^{n}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}).

The bifiltered complex

(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)

produces the following new spectral sequences of

{\rm Gal}(\overline{s}/s)
-modules,
respectively:

(1.1.3)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=H^{q-k}_{\rm ket}(\overline{D}{}^{(k)}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})(-k)\Longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{N}}),

(1.1.4)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{k^{\prime}\leq k}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k^{\prime},0\}}H^{q-2j-k}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})(-j-k)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{N}}).

((
1.1.4
) is a generalization of (
1.0.3
).)
Using the specialization argument as in
[
Nak3
]
,
we prove that (
1.1.4
) degenerates at
E_{2}
modulo torsion.
We also prove that (
1.1.3
) degenerates at
E_{2}
modulo torsion if

(X,D)
is the log special fiber of
a proper strict semistable family with a horizontal SNCD over a
henselian discrete valuation ring of any characteristic.
Though we have not yet proved that
(
1.1.3
) degenerates at
E_{2}
modulo torsion in the general case,
we prove that the edge morphisms
\{d_{r}^{-k,q+k}\}_{k,q}
of the spectral sequence
(
1.1.3
) are strictly compatible with the induced filtration by the weight filtrations
on
H^{q-k}_{\rm ket}(\overline{D}{}^{(k)}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})(-k)
’s.
Furthermore, by pursuing the analogue of the log crystalline case in
[
Nakk6
]
,
we prove the contravariant functoriality of

(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)

with respect to
the following commutative diagram of log schemes

\begin{CD}(X,D)@>{g}>{}>(Y,E)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
s@>{v}>{}>s^{\prime},\end{CD}

where
v
is a certain morphism of log points such that
{\rm deg}(v)

is not divisible by
l
(see
[
Nakk5
]
for the definition of
{\rm deg}(v)
) and

g\colon(X,D)/s\longrightarrow(Y,E)/s^{\prime}
is a morphism of SNCL log schemes with SNCD’s.
As a corollary of this contravariant functoriality, if
for each irreducible component
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}
of
\overset{\circ}{X}
,
there exists a smooth component
\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
of
\overset{\circ}{Y}

such that

\overset{\circ}{g}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda})\subset\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\lambda^{\prime}}

and if,
for each irreducible component
\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu}
of
\overset{\circ}{D}
,
there exists a smooth component
\overset{\circ}{E}_{\mu^{\prime}}
of
\overset{\circ}{E}

such that

\overset{\circ}{g}(\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu})\subset\overset{\circ}{E}_{\mu^{\prime}}
,
then we obtain the contravariant functorialities of the spectral sequences (
1.1.3
) and
(
1.1.4
).
That is, we obtain the following spectral sequences

(1.1.5)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=H^{q-k}_{\rm ket}(\overline{D}{}^{(k)}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})(-k;v)\Longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{N}}),

(1.1.6)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{k^{\prime}\leq k}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k^{\prime},0\}}H^{q-2j-k}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})(-j-k^{\prime};v)(-(k-k^{\prime});g,\Delta,\Delta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{N}}),

where
(-k;v)
is the
l
-adic analogue of the
D
-twist defined in
[
Nakk5
]
and
[
Nakk4
]
and

(-(k-k^{\prime});g,\Delta,\Delta^{\prime})
is the
l
-adic
D
-twist defined in the text, where

\Delta=\{\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu}\}_{\mu}

and
\Delta^{\prime}=\{\overset{\circ}{E}_{\mu^{\prime}}\}_{\mu^{\prime}}
are the irreducible components of
\overset{\circ}{D}

and
\overset{\circ}{E}
, respectively.
These contravariant functorialities on (
1.1.5
) and (
1.1.6
) have
not been (able) to be considered in
[
RZ
]
,
[
Nak3
]
,
[
It1
]
nor
[
SaT
]
even in
the case
D=\emptyset
.
We also prove the base change theorem of
(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)

under a mild condition.

To construct
(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)
,
we use a bifiltered version of Berthelot’s theory of
the filtered derived category in
[
B2
]
and
[
NS
]
.
To give a bifiltered version of Berthelot’s theory is a nontrivial work.
We have to give appropriate definitions and appropriate formulations
about bifiltered complexes and
we have to make quite complicated calculations about them patiently
for the construction of our theory.
The fundamental machines in
[
B2
]
,
[
NS
]
and
the bifiltered version of Berthelot’s theory in the first part of this paper
with fundamental facts for Kummer log étale cohomologies in

[
Nak1
]
,
[
KN
]
,
[
Nak4
]

give us several properties of

(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)
.
For example, by virtue of the adjunction formula for the derived homomorphism functor for
bifiltered complexes proved in the first part,
we obtain the contravariant functoriality and the base change theorem of

(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)
((
11.3
), (
12.1
)).

Let

N\colon H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-1)

be the
l
-adic monodromy operator which will be defined in the text.
We denote by
P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
and
P

the induced filtrations on

H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
by
(
1.1.3
) and (
1.1.4
), respectively.
It is not difficult to prove that

N\colon H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-1)

induces a morphism

N\colon P_{k}H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow P_{k-2}H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}})
by using (
1.1.2
) and
the filtered complex

(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P)
.
We conjecture the following, which we call
the
log
l
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture
:

Conjecture 1.2

(
Log
l
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture
)
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is projective over
\kappa
.
Then the
l
-adic relative monodromy filtration
on
H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

relative to
P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
exists and that it is equal to
the induced filtration
P
on

H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
.
This is equivalent to the following
:

the morphism

N\colon H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-1)

induces the following isomorphism

(1.2.1)

N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}_{q+k+e}^{P}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}}H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}_{q+k-e}^{P}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}}H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-e)

for
k,q\in{\mathbb{N}},e\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
.

Here note that the index
q+k+e
of
{\rm gr}_{q+k+e}^{P}
is different from
the index
a+b
of
{\rm gr}_{a+b}^{W}
in
[
E
, (1.10.1)]
if
q\not=0
;
the index
a+b
in [loc. cit.] is incorrect.
If this conjecture is true, then the filtration
P
on

H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

is equal to the relative monodromy filtration of
N
with respect to the filtration

P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
on

H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
.
Especially the relative monodromy filtration of
N
with respect to the filtration

P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
on

H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
exists.
By showing a key lemma and the strict compatibility
of the edge morphisms of (
1.1.5
) with respect to
the weight filtration which has been already stated,
we prove that the conjecture (
1.2
) is true if
the log
l
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture
(
1.1
) for
D^{(k)}
for any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
is true.
Because (
1.1
) has been proved
in the proper strict semistable case in the equal characteristic
p>0
(
[
D2
]
,
[
It1
]
),
we see that the conjecture (
1.2
) is true
if

(X,D)/s

is the log special fiber
of a proper strict semistable family over a henselian discrete valuation ring
of equal characteristic
p>0
.
We also prove that the conjecture (
1.2
) is true
when
{\rm dim}\overset{\circ}{X}\leq 2
by using
Kajiwara-Achinger’s result (
[
Kaj
, (3.1)]
,
[
Ac
, Theorem 3.6]
)
and Rapoport-Zink-Mokrane’s calculation (
[
RZ
]
,
[
M
]
)
for
D^{(k)}

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
.

It is very natural to give
the
p
-adic version of this paper.
We have already given this in
[
Nakk6
]
.
Especially we have constructed the log crystalline analogue of

(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)
.
In the future we would like to discuss the
\infty
-adic version
of this paper, which is a generalization of
[
SZ
]
.

This paper consists of three parts.
The aim of the first part
(
{\rm\lx@sectionsign\ref{sec:nfil}}\sim{\rm\lx@sectionsign\ref{sec:rksfdc}}
)
is to construct a general theory of the derived category of bifiltered complexes
which will be used in the second part.
This part is the bifiltered version of Berthelot’s theory (
[
B2
]
,
[
NS
]
).
For a morphism
f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})

of ringed topoi, we define four functors
Rf_{*}
,
Lf^{*}
,
RHom^{\bullet}
,
\otimes^{L}

in the derived categories of bifiltered complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules among
so called Grothendieck’s six functors.
The aim of the second part (
{\rm\lx@sectionsign\ref{sec:lbc}}\sim{\rm\lx@sectionsign\ref{sec:ssf}}
)
is to give the log
l
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture
and to prove that this conjecture is true in the cases already stated.
This part is a generalization of Nakayama’s work.
The third part (§
15
) is an appendix,
in which we give the explicit descriptions of
the edge morphisms of the
E_{1}
-terms of the spectral sequences
(
1.1.3
) and (
1.1.4
).

The content of each section of this paper is as follows.

In §
2
we give the definition of the derived category of bifiltered complexes.
The notion of the strictly exactness of a bifiltered complex is a key notion for the definition.
Our definition of
the derived category of bifiltered complexes
is a generalization of
the derived category of bounded below biregular bifiltered complexes in
[
D4
]
.

In §
3
we give the notion of a strictly injective module
and we prove the existence of the strictly injective resolution of
a bounded below bifiltered complex and the existence of
Rf_{*}
.

In §
4
we give the notion of a strictly flat module
and we prove the existence of
the strictly flat resolution of a bounded above bifiltered complex
and the existence of
Lf^{*}
.

In §
5
we prove
the existence of the derived homomorphism functor
{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}

from a bounded above bifiltered complex
to a bounded below bifiltered complex.
We also prove
the adjunction formula of the derived homomorphism functor.

In §
6
we prove the existence of the derived tensor product
\otimes^{L}

of two bounded above bifiltered complexes.
To prove the existence of
\otimes^{L}
is a much more nontrivial work than
to prove the existence of
{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}
.
The derived tensor product
\otimes^{L}
in
[
NS
]
and
the bifiltered derived category in this paper
is necessary for the second part of this paper.

In §
7
we give some complements.

In §
8

we give a simple remark related to the general theory of quasi-abelian categories of
Schneiders (
[
Schn
]
,
[
SS
]
).

In §
9
we construct the
l
-adic bifiltered El Zein-Steenbrink-Zucker complex

(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)\in{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{2}_{\rm ctf}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}).

This section is a main part of this paper.

In §
10
we construct the spectral sequences (
1.1.3
) and (
1.1.4
).

In §
11
we prove the contravariant functoriality of

(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)
and
we construct the spectral sequences (
1.1.5
) and (
1.1.6
).

In §
12
we investigate fundamental properties of
the induced filtrations
P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
and
P
on

H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

by the spectral sequences (
1.1.3
) and (
1.1.4
), respectively.

In §
13
we give the conjecture (
1.2.1
)
and we prove that this is true in the case

\dim\overset{\circ}{X}\leq 2
.

In §
14
we construct a similar bifiltered complex to

(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)
in the proper strictly semistable case with a relative SNCD
over
{\cal V}
and we give a comparison theorem
between the similar complex and

(A_{l^{\infty}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)
.
We also prove that the conjecture (
1.2
)
is true if
D^{(k)}/s
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
is the log special fiber of
a proper strict semistable family over
a henselian discrete valuation ring
of equal characteristic.

In §
15

we give the explicit expressions of the edge morphisms between
the
E_{1}
-terms of
l
-adic weight spectral sequences (
1.1.3
) and
(
1.1.4
).

Acknowledgment.

I would like to express my sincere thanks to
K. Kato for sending me a letter
[
Kat
]
, K. Fujiwara
for informing me of a technique in the proof of
[
It1
, (6.1)]
before
the publication of the paper and C. Nakayama
for giving me a suggestion in (
14.15
) (2) in the text, respectively.

Notations.

(1) For a log scheme
X
,
\overset{\circ}{X}
denotes the underlying scheme of
X
.
For a morphism

\varphi\colon X\longrightarrow Y
,
\overset{\circ}{\varphi}
denotes
the underlying morphism

\overset{\circ}{X}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Y}
of
\varphi
.

(2) SNC(L)=simple normal crossing (log),
SNCD=simple normal crossing divisor.

(3) For a complex
(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})

of objects in an exact additive category
{\cal A}
,
we often denote
(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
only by

E^{\bullet}
as usual.

(4) For a complex
(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
in (3) and
for an integer
n
,
(E^{\bullet}\{n\},d^{\bullet}\{n\})

denotes the following complex:

\cdots\longrightarrow\underset{q-1}{E^{q-1+n}}\overset{d^{q-1+n}}{\longrightarrow}\underset{q}{E^{q+n}}\overset{d^{q+n}}{\longrightarrow}\underset{q+1}{E^{q+1+n}}\overset{d^{q+1+n}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots.

Here the numbers under the objects
above in
{\cal A}
mean the degrees.

(5) For a complex
(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
in (3),

\tau=\{\tau_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
denotes
the canonical filtration on
(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
:

\tau_{k}(E^{\bullet}):=(\cdots\longrightarrow E^{k-2}\longrightarrow E^{k-1}\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(d^{k})\longrightarrow 0\longrightarrow 0\longrightarrow\cdots).

We fix the following isomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{\tau}E^{\bullet}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{k}(E^{\bullet})[-k]

induced by the projection
{\rm Ker}(E^{k}\longrightarrow E^{k+1})\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{k}(E^{\bullet})
.

(6) For a morphism

f\colon(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet}_{E})\longrightarrow(F^{\bullet},d^{\bullet}_{F})

of complexes,
let
{\rm MF}(f)
(resp. MC(f))
be the mapping fiber (resp. the mapping cone)
of
f
:
{\rm MF}(f):=E^{\bullet}\oplus F^{\bullet}[-1]

with boundary morphism “
(x,y)\longmapsto(d_{E}(x),-d_{F}(y)+f(x))
”
(resp.
{\rm MC}(f):=E^{\bullet}[1]\oplus F^{\bullet}

with boundary morphism “
(x,y)\longmapsto(-d_{E}(x),d_{F}(y)+f(x))
”).

(7) Let
({\cal T},{\cal A})
be a ringed topos.

(a)
C({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
{\rm C}^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

C^{\rm b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
): the category of
(resp. bounded below, bounded above,
bounded) complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules,

(b)
K({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
K^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

K^{\rm b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
): the category of
(resp. bounded below, bounded above,
bounded) complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules modulo homotopy,

(b)
D({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
D^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

D^{\rm b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
): the derived category of

K({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
K^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

K^{\rm b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
).
For an object
E^{\bullet}
of

C({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
C^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

C^{\rm b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
), we denote
simply by
E^{\bullet}

the corresponding object to
E^{\bullet}
in

D({\cal T},{\cal A})
(resp.
D^{\pm}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

D^{\rm b}({\cal T},{\cal A})
).

(d) The additional notation
{\rm F}

to the categories above means “the filtered ”.
Here the filtration is
an increasing filtration indexed by

{\mathbb{Z}}
.
For example,

{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal T},{\cal A})
is
the category
of bounded below filtered complexes
modulo filtered homotopy.

(e)
{\rm DF}^{2}({\cal T},{\cal A})

(resp.
{\rm D}^{\pm}{\rm F}^{2}({\cal T},{\cal A})
,

{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{2}({\cal T},{\cal A})
):
the derived category of (resp. bounded below, bounded above, bounded)
bifiltered complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules which will be defined.

(8) For an fs log scheme
X
,
X_{\rm ket}
denotes
the Kummer log étale topos of
X
by imitating
“
(X/S)_{\rm cris}
” as in the classical crystalline case (
[
B1
]
).
(We do not use the notation

\widetilde{X}_{\rm ket}
which was used in references.)
For a scheme
Y
,

Y_{\rm et}
denotes the étale topos of
Y

(
Y_{\rm et}
is not the étale site of
Y
in this paper).

Conventions.

We make the following conventions about signs
(cf.
[
BBM
]
,
[
Co
]
).

Let
{\cal A}
be an exact additive category.

(1) (cf.
[
BBM
, 0.3.2]
,
[
Co
, (1.3.2)]
)
For a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet}_{E})\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}(F^{\bullet},d^{\bullet}_{F})\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}(G^{\bullet},d^{\bullet}_{G})\longrightarrow 0

of complexes of objects in
{\cal A}
,
the mapping fiber of
g
.
We fix an isomorphism
“
E^{\bullet}\owns x\longmapsto(f(x),0)\in{\rm MF}(g)=F^{\bullet}\oplus G^{\bullet}[-1]
”
in the derived category
D({\cal A})
.

(2) (
[
BBM
, 0.3.2]
,
[
Co
, (1.3.3)]
)
In the situation (1), the boundary morphism

(G^{\bullet},d^{\bullet}_{G})\longrightarrow(E^{\bullet}[1],d^{\bullet}_{E}[1])

in
D^{+}({\cal A})

is the following composite morphism

(G^{\bullet},d^{\bullet}_{G})\overset{\sim}{\longleftarrow}{\rm MC}(f)\overset{{\rm proj.}}{\longrightarrow}(E^{\bullet}[1],d^{\bullet}_{E}[1])\overset{(-1)\times}{\longrightarrow}(E^{\bullet}[1],d^{\bullet}_{E}[1]).

More generally, we use only the similar boundary morphism
for a triangle in a derived category.

(3) For a complex
(E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
of objects in

{\cal A}
,
the identity
{\rm id}\colon E^{q}\longrightarrow E^{q}

(\forall q\in{\mathbb{Z}})
induces an isomorphism

{\cal H}^{q}((E^{\bullet},-d^{\bullet}))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{q}((E^{\bullet},d^{\bullet}))

(\forall q\in{\mathbb{Z}})
of cohomologies.

(4) For a ringed topos
({\cal T},{\cal A})
and
for two complexes
(A^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})
and
(B^{\bullet},d^{\bullet})

of
{\cal A}
-modules, the boundary morphism
d=\{d^{n}\}_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
of

A^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}B^{\bullet}
is defined as usual:

d^{n}=\sum_{p+q=n}(d^{p}\otimes{\rm id}+(-1)^{p}{\rm id}\otimes d^{q}).

Part I. Derived categories of bifiltered complexes

In the Part I of this paper we construct
theory of derived categories of bifiltered complexes.
We need new various ideas to construct it.

2
The definition of the derived category of bifiltered complexes

In this section we give the definition of the strictness of a morphism of
bifiltered modules and the definition of the strictly exactness of bifiltered complexes.
The latter notion is the most important one for
the definition of the derived category of bifiltered complexes
in this paper.
To give the definitions is not an obvious work (see (
2.3
) below);
the theory in
[
B2
]
and
[
NS
]
does not imply the theory in this paper.
Unfortunately the author has not yet given the appropriate definition of the strictly exactness of
a complex with
n
-pieces of filtrations for a general positive integer
n

because of the quite complicated description of the multi-graded complex of a complex with

n
-pieces of filtrations for the case
n\geq 3
.

Let
({\cal T},{\cal A})
be a ringed topos.
Let
E
be an

{\cal A}
-module in
{\cal T}
.
An increasing filtration on
E

is, by definition, a
family
P:=\{P_{k}\}:=\{P_{k}E\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
of

{\cal A}
-submodules of

E
such that
P_{k}E\subset P_{k+1}E

for any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
As in
[
B2
]
and
[
NS
]
,
filtrations are not necessarily
exhaustive nor separated unlike biregular filtrations in
[
D4
]
.
(In
[
Nakk4
]
we have already used results in
[
B2
]
and
[
NS
]
essentially
for non-biregular filtrations for the study of weight filtrations on
the log crystalline cohomological sheaves of proper SNCL schemes in characteristic
p>0
.)
In this paper we consider only increasing filtrations
and we call them filtrations shortly.
Let
n
be a positive integer and let

P^{(i)}

(i=1,\ldots,n)
be filtrations on
E
.
We denote by
(E,\{P^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

an
{\cal A}
-module
E

with
n
-pieces of filtrations
P^{(1)},\ldots,P^{(n)}
on
E
.
For simplicity of notation, we almost always denote

P^{(i)}
by
E^{(i)}
and

P^{(i)}_{k}E

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}})
by
E^{(i)}_{k}
, respectively.
For
1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{m}\leq n

(1\leq m\leq n)
and
k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
set

E^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{m})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{m}}:=E^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap E^{(i_{m})}_{k_{m}}.

For
1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq\cdots\leq i_{n}\leq n

and
k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we also use
the following convenient notation

E^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}:=E^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap E^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}.

For an
{\cal A}
-module
E
,
we mean by the trivial filtration on
E

a filtration
\{P_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

such that
P_{0}E=E
and
P_{-1}E=0
.
In
[
B2
]
,
{\cal A}
has
a nontrivial filtration and

{\cal A}
is not necessarily commutative;
in this paper, we consider only
the trivial filtration on

{\cal A}
and
{\cal A}
is assumed to be commutative.

A morphism
f\colon(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

of modules of
n
-pieces of filtrations is defined to be
a morphism
f\colon E\longrightarrow F
of
{\cal A}
-modules
such that
f(E^{(i)}_{k})\subset F^{(i)}_{k}
for any
1\leq i\leq n
and any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Let
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
be the category of
{\cal A}
-modules with

n
-pieces of filtrations.
Obviously
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
is an additive category.
We do not say that
f
is strict even if

f\colon(E,E^{(i)})\longrightarrow(F,F^{(i)})
is strict
(i.e.,
{\rm Im}(f)\cap F^{(i)}_{k}=f(E^{(i)}_{k})

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}})
)
for
1\leq\forall i\leq n
:

Definition 2.1
.

Let
f\colon(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

be a morphism in

{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
.
Then we say that
f
is
strict
if
{\rm Im}(f)\cap F^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}=f(E^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}})

for
1\leq i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{n}\leq n
and
k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

It is obvious that, if
f\colon(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is strict,
then
the underlying morphism
f^{(i)}\colon(E,E^{(i)})\longrightarrow(F,F^{(i)})
of filtered
{\cal A}
-modules
is strict for
1\leq\forall i\leq n
.

We often use the following simple criterion in key points in the proofs of
results in this paper:

Proposition 2.2
.

Let
f\colon(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

be a morphism in

{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
.
For
1\leq i\leq n
,
let
f^{(i)}\colon(E,E^{(i)})\longrightarrow(F,F^{(i)})

be the filtered morphism.
If
f
is injective, then
f
is strict if and only if

f^{(i)}\colon(E,E^{(i)})\longrightarrow(F,F^{(i)})

is strict for
1\leq\forall i\leq n
.

Proof.

We have only to prove that
{\rm Im}(f)\cap F^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}\subset f(E^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}})
.
Since
F^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}\subset F^{(i_{m})}_{k_{m}}

(1\leq\forall m\leq n)

and
since

f^{(i)}\colon(E,E^{(i)})\longrightarrow(F,F^{(i)})

(1\leq i\leq n)
is strict,

{\rm Im}(f)\cap F^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}\subset\bigcap_{m=1}^{n}f(E^{(i_{m})}_{k_{m}})
.
Since
f\colon E\longrightarrow F
is injective, it is very easy to check
that

\bigcap_{m=1}^{n}f(E^{(i_{m})}_{k_{m}})=f(E^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}})
.
∎

Remark 2.3
.

Even if
f^{(i)}
is strict for
1\leq\forall i\leq n
,

f
is not necessarily strict in general because

E^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}
may be too small compared with

F^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}
.
Indeed, let
A
be a nonzero commutative ring with unit element.
Let
M
be an
A
-module and let

0\subsetneq N\subsetneq M
be an
A
-submodule.
Consider a filtration
P
on
M
defined by

P_{k}M:=0

(k<0)
,
P_{0}M:=N

(k=0)
and
P_{k}M:=M

(k>0)
.
Set
E:=M\oplus M
and consider two filtrations

E^{(1)}
and
E^{(2)}
defined by

E^{(1)}_{k}=\begin{cases}0&(k<0),\\
N\oplus 0&(k=0),\\
E&(k>0)\end{cases}

and

E^{(2)}_{k}=\begin{cases}0&(k<0),\\
0\oplus N&(k=0),\\
E&(k>0).\end{cases}

Set
F:=M
and consider two filtrations

F^{(1)}
and
F^{(2)}
on
F
defined by

F^{(1)}:=P=:F^{(2)}
.
Then the summation
+\colon M\oplus M\longrightarrow M

induces a bifiltered morphism

f\colon(E,(E^{(1)},E^{(2)}))\longrightarrow(F,(F^{(1)},F^{(2)}))
of
A
-modules.
The morphism
f^{(i)}\colon(E,E^{(i)})\longrightarrow(F,F^{(i)})
for
i=1,2
is strict,
while
f
is not:

f(E^{(12)}_{0})=0\not=N={\rm Im}(f)\cap F^{(12)}_{00}
.
Because

{\rm Im}(f)=(f(E),\{f(E)\cap F^{(1)}_{k})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}},\{f(E)\cap F^{(2)}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})

and

\displaystyle{\rm Coim}(f)

\displaystyle=(E,P^{(1)},P^{(2)})/{\rm Ker}(f)

\displaystyle=(E/f^{-1}(0),\{E^{(1)}_{k}+f^{-1}(0)/f^{-1}(0)\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}},\{E^{(2)}_{k}+f^{-1}(0)/f^{-1}(0)\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})

\displaystyle=(f(E),\{f(E^{(1)}_{k})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}},\{f(E^{(2)}_{k})\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}),

{\rm Coim}(f)={\rm Im}(f)
.
Hence
f
is strict in the sense of
[
Schn
, §1]
.
(Consequently our definition of the strictness for a morphism
of bifiltered modules is not equal to the natural definition of the strictness for a morphism
of bifiltered modules in [loc. cit.].)
However the induced morphism

f^{\prime}\colon(E,E^{(1)},E^{(2)},E^{(12)})\longrightarrow(F,F^{(1)},F^{(2)},F^{(12)})

of trifiltered complexes by
f
is not strict in the sense of
[
Schn
, §1]
.
Here
the category of trifiltered complexes obtained by bifiltered complexes is defined suitably.
Note that the sets of indexes of the filtrations
E^{(12)}
and
F^{(12)}

are
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
.
More generally,

f
in (
2.1
) is strict if and only if
the induced morphism

f^{\prime}\colon(E,\{E^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}\}_{1\leq i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{n}\leq n,k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}})\longrightarrow(F,\{F^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}\}_{1\leq i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{n}\leq n,k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}})

is strict in the sense of [loc. cit.].

Note also that the morphism

M\owns x\longmapsto(x,-x)\in M\oplus M

does not induce a filtered morphism
(F,F^{(i)})\longrightarrow(E,E^{(i)})

(i=1,2)
if
N\not=0
.
Hence we cannot consider an exact sequence
“
0\longrightarrow(F,F^{(i)})\longrightarrow(E,E^{(i)})\overset{+}{\longrightarrow}(F,F^{(i)})\longrightarrow 0
”.

Let
f\colon(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

be a morphism in
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
.
We say that
f
is a
strict injective morphism

(resp.
strict surjective morphism
) if
f
is strict
and if the induced morphism
E\longrightarrow F
is injective (resp. if
f
is strict
and if the induced morphism
E\longrightarrow F
is surjective).

A complex with
n
-pieces of filtrations is, by definition, a complex

(E^{\bullet},d)
with
n
-pieces
\{P^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}
of filtrations
such that

d(P^{(i)}_{k}E^{q})\subset P^{(i)}_{k}E^{q+1}
.
A morphism of complexes with
n
-pieces of filtrations is defined in an obvious way.
Let
{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})
be
the category of complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations
and let

{\rm C}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
,

{\rm C}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
,

{\rm C}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

be the categories of bounded below,
bounded above and bounded complexes of

{\cal A}
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations, respectively.
We define the notion of the
n
-filtered homotopy
in an obvious way.

Let
{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})
be
the category of complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations
modulo
n
-filtered homotopies and
let
{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
,

{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
,

{\rm K}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
be
the categories of bounded below, bounded above and
bounded complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations
modulo
n
-filtered homotopies, respectively.
For an object of
{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})

or
{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})
, we define
the direct image and the inverse image
of an object of
{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})
or
{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})

by a morphism of ringed
topoi in an obvious way.
Since
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})

is an additive category,

{\rm K}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

(
\star=+
,
-
, b, nothing)
is a triangulated category.
For a complex

(E^{\bullet},\{P^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})

with
n
-pieces of filtrations
and for a sequence
\underline{l}:=(l_{1},\ldots,l_{n})
,
we define the shift

(E^{\bullet},\{P^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\langle\underline{l}\rangle

by
P^{(i)}\langle l_{i}\rangle_{k}E^{\bullet}:=P^{(i)}_{l_{i}+k}E^{\bullet}

(
[
D2
, (1.1)]
).
In the following we often denote

(E^{\bullet},\{P^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
by

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.

Proposition-Definition 2.4
.

(1)
For
1\leq i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{n}\leq n

and
k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
the following
intersection functor

(2.4.1)

\bigcap^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}\colon{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})\owns(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longmapsto E^{\bullet(i_{1}\cdots i_{n})}_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}=\bigcap_{j=1}^{n}E^{\bullet(i_{j})}_{k_{j}}\in K({\cal A})

is well-defined. Here
K({\cal A})
is the category of complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules
modulo homotopy.

(2)

For
1\leq i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{n}\leq n

and
k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
the following
gr functor

(2.4.2)

{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{1})}}_{k_{1}}\cdots,{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{n})}}_{k_{n}}\colon{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})\owns(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longmapsto{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{1})}}_{k_{1}}\cdots,{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{n})}}_{k_{n}}(E^{\bullet})\in K({\cal A})

is well-defined.

Proof.

This is easy to prove.
∎

The following is a generalization of the strictly exactness
defined in
[
B2
]
(and
[
NS
]
).

Definition 2.5
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
We say that a complex

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})

with
n
-pieces of filtrations is

strictly exact
if the complexes

E^{\bullet}
and
E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1}k_{2}}

(1\leq\forall i_{1}\leq\forall i_{2}\leq n,\forall k_{1},\forall k_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}})
are exact.

The following simple remark is very important:

Remark 2.6
.

For the case
n=1
, we have said that

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is strictly exact if

E^{\bullet}
and
E^{\bullet(1)}
is exact in
[
B2
]
and
[
NS
]
.
For the case
n=2
,
the following two conditions is
not
necessary equivalent
((2) does not imply (1) in general):

(1)
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{2})
is strictly exact.

(2)
(E^{\bullet},E^{\bullet(i)})
is strictly exact for
1\leq\forall i\leq 2
.

Indeed, let the notations be as in (
2.3
).
Let us consider the following sequence

0\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(f)\longrightarrow(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1,2})\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1,2})\longrightarrow 0.

Then it is easy to check that

0\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(f^{(i)})\longrightarrow(E,E^{(i)})\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}(F,F^{(i)})\longrightarrow 0

is exact for
i=1,2
. Indeed,

F_{-1}^{(i)}{\rm Ker}(f^{(i)})=M
,
E^{(i)}_{-1}=M\oplus M
,

F^{(i)}_{-1}=M
,

F_{0}^{(i)}{\rm Ker}(f^{(i)})=0
,
E^{(1)}_{0}=N\oplus 0
,

E^{(2)}_{0}=0\oplus N
and
F^{(i)}_{0}=N
.
However

0\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(f^{(12)})\longrightarrow(E,E^{(12)})\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}(F,F^{(12)})\longrightarrow 0

is not exact since
E^{(12)}_{0}=0
and
F^{(12)}_{0}=N\not=0
.

Proposition 2.7
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
If a complex
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

with
n
-pieces of filtrations
is strictly exact and if

(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\simeq(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

in
{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})
,
then
(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is
also strictly exact.

Proof.

This is easy to prove.
∎

Proposition 2.8
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
. Let
1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq n
be integers.
Let
k_{1}
and
k_{2}
be integers.
If
E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{l_{1}l_{2}}
is exact for
(l_{1},l_{2})=(k_{1},k_{2})
,
(k_{1}-1,k_{2})
,
(k_{1},k_{2}-1)

and
(k_{1}-1,k_{2}-1)
,
then

{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{1})}}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{2})}}_{k_{2}}E^{\bullet}
is exact.

Proof.

In the case
n=1
, this is obvious.
Because

(2.8.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{1})}}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{2})}}_{k_{2}}E^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1}k_{2}}/(E^{{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}}+E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1},k_{2}-1}),

we have only to prove that

E^{{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}}+E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1},k_{2}-1}
is exact.
This follows from the following exact sequence

(2.8.2)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow E^{{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}-1}\overset{{\rm inc}.\oplus-{\rm inc}.}{\longrightarrow}E^{{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}}\oplus E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1},k_{2}-1}\overset{+}{\longrightarrow}E^{{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}}+E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1},k_{2}-1}\longrightarrow 0.

∎

Remark 2.9
.

Consider the case
n=3
.
By the definition of the quotient filtration, we obtain the following formulas
for
1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq i_{3}\leq n
:

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{1})}}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{2})}}_{k_{2}}{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{3})}}_{k_{3}}E^{\bullet}=

\displaystyle E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}/\{E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}k_{3}}+E^{\bullet(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}\cap(E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2}-1,k_{3}}+E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2},k_{3}-1})\}.

Hence we would like to consider the following
{\cal A}
-module:

\displaystyle E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}k_{3}}\cap\{E^{\bullet(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}\cap(E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2}-1,k_{3}}+E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2},k_{3}-1})\}

\displaystyle=E^{\bullet(i_{1})}_{k_{1}-1}\cap E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2}k_{3}}\cap(E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2}-1,k_{3}}+E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2},k_{3}-1})

\displaystyle=E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}k_{3}}\cap(E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2}-1,k_{3}}+E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2},k_{3}-1}).

In the case where
n=3
, it is reasonable to say that

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is strictly exact if

{\cal H}^{q}(E^{\bullet})=0
,

{\cal H}^{q}(E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}})=0

and

{\cal H}^{q}(E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}k_{3}}\cap(E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2}-1,k_{3}}+E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2},k_{3}-1}))=0

(\forall q\in{\mathbb{Z}},1\leq\forall i_{1}\leq\forall i_{2}\leq\forall i_{3}\leq n=3,\forall k_{1},\forall k_{2},\forall k_{3}\in{\mathbb{Z}})
.
However I have not yet proved the existence of
the strictly injective resolution of a bounded below complex
with
3
-pieces of filtrations because of the complicated term

E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}k_{3}}\cap(E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2}-1,k_{3}}+E^{\bullet(i_{2}i_{3})}_{k_{2},k_{3}-1})
.
In this reason we discuss the derived category of complexes with
n
-pieces of filtrations
under the assumption
n\leq 2
in this paper.

Proposition 2.10
.

Let the notations
be as in
(
2.8
)
.
Let
p
be an integer.
If the boundary morphism

d^{p-1}\colon(E^{p-1},\{E^{p-1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(E^{p},\{E^{p(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is strict,
then

(2.10.1)

\displaystyle{\rm Im}({\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{1})}}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{2})}}_{k_{2}}E^{p-1}\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{1})}}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{2})}}_{k_{2}}E^{p})={\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{1})}}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{2})}}_{k_{2}}{\rm Im}(E^{p-1}\longrightarrow E^{p}).

Proof.

The left hand side of (
2.10.1
) is equal to

\displaystyle{\rm Im}(E^{{p-1(i_{1}i_{2})}}_{k_{1}k_{2}}/(E^{{p-1(i_{1}i_{2})}}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}}+E^{p-1(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1},k_{2}-1})\longrightarrow E^{{p(i_{1}i_{2})}}_{k_{1}k_{2}}/(E^{{p(i_{1}i_{2})}}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}}+E^{p(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1},k_{2}-1})).

The right hand side of (
2.10.1
) is equal to

\displaystyle{\rm Im}(E^{p-1}\longrightarrow E^{p})^{(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1}k_{2}}/({\rm Im}(E^{p-1}\longrightarrow E^{p})^{(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1}-1,k_{2}}+{\rm Im}(E^{p-1}\longrightarrow E^{p})^{(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1},k_{2}-1}).

Hence we have a natural morphism from the left hand side of (
2.10.1
)
to the right hand side of (
2.10.1
).
By the assumption of the strictness of
d^{p-1}
,
we see that this natural morphism is an isomorphism.
∎

Definition 2.11
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
We say that a filtered morphism

(2.11.1)

\displaystyle f\colon(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

in
{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})
is an
n
-
filtered quasi-isomorphism

(or simply a
filtered quasi-isomorphism
) if
the induced morphisms

(2.11.2)

\displaystyle f\colon E\longrightarrow F

and

(2.11.3)

\displaystyle f\colon E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}\longrightarrow F^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}

are quasi-isomorphisms for

1\leq\forall i_{1}\leq\forall i_{n}\leq n
and

\forall k_{1},\forall k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Remark 2.12
.

For the morphism (
2.11.1
) we can define the
n
-filtered complex

({\rm MC}(f),\{{\rm MC}(f)^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})
in a natural way.
It is obvious that
f
is an
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphism
if and only if
({\rm MC}(f),\{{\rm MC}(f)^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is strictly exact.

By the following proposition, we see that our definition above
is equivalent to the definition
in
[
D2
, (1.3.6) (i), (ii)]
in the case
n=1,2

if the filtrations are biregular.

Proposition 2.13
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
Let

f\colon(E^{\bullet},\{P^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(F^{\bullet},\{Q^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

be an
n
-filtered morphism in
{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})
.
Assume that the filtrations
P^{(i)}
and
Q^{(i)}

(1\leq i\leq n)
are biregular.
Then the following are equivalent
:

(1)
The morphism
f
is an
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

The morphism

(2.13.1)

{\rm gr}(f)\colon{\rm gr}^{P^{(1)}}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{P^{(m)}}_{k_{m}}E^{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{Q^{(1)}}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{Q^{(m)}}_{k_{m}}F^{\bullet}

for any
k_{1},k_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and for
m\leq n

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Furthermore, if
n=2
, then
(1)

is equivalent to the following
:

(3)

The morphism

(2.13.2)

{\rm gr}(f)\colon{\rm gr}^{P^{(1)}}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{P^{(2)}}_{k_{2}}E^{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{Q^{(1)}}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{Q^{(2)}}_{k_{2}}F^{\bullet}

for any
k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

Because (
2.13
) is easy to prove in the case
n=1
,
we prove (
2.13
) only in the case
n=2
.
First assume that
f
is a bifiltered quasi-isomorphism.
Then the morphism
{\rm gr}(f)
’s in (
2.13.1
) and (
2.13.2
)
are quasi-isomorphisms by
(
2.8.1
) and (
2.8.2
).

Because (2) implies (3), it suffices to prove that (3) implies (1).
Assume that (
2.13.2
) is a quasi-isomorphism.
Let
q
be an integer.
Let
\bullet
be
q
or
q\pm 1
.
Let
k_{1},k_{2},l_{1},l_{2}
be integers such that

E^{\bullet(1)}_{k_{1}}=0
,
E^{\bullet(1)}_{l_{1}}=E^{\bullet}
,
E^{\bullet(2)}_{k_{2}}=0

and
E^{\bullet(2)}_{l_{2}}=E^{\bullet}
.
Then

{\rm gr}^{P^{(1)}}_{k_{1}+1}{\rm gr}^{P^{(2)}}_{k_{2}+1}E^{\bullet}=E^{\bullet(12)}_{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}

and

{\rm gr}^{P^{(1)}}_{k_{1}+m}{\rm gr}^{P^{(2)}}_{k_{2}+1}E^{\bullet}=E^{\bullet(12)}_{k_{1}+m,k_{2}+1}/E^{\bullet}_{k_{1}+m-1,k_{2}+1}\quad(m\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Using the induction on
k_{1}+m
, we see that the morphism

E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{m_{1},k_{2}+1}\longrightarrow F^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{m_{1},k_{2}+1}

is a quasi-isomorphism for any
m_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Furthermore,

{\rm gr}^{P^{(1)}}_{k_{1}+1}{\rm gr}^{P^{(2)}}_{k_{2}+2}E^{\bullet}=E^{\bullet(12)}_{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+2}/E^{\bullet(12)}_{k_{1}+1,k_{2}+1}

and

{\rm gr}^{P^{(1)}}_{k_{1}+m}{\rm gr}^{P^{(2)}}_{k_{2}+2}E^{\bullet}=E^{\bullet(12)}_{k_{1}+m,k_{2}+2}/(E^{\bullet(12)}_{k_{1}+m-1,k_{2}+2}+E^{\bullet(12)}_{k_{1}+m,k_{2}+1})\quad(m\in{\mathbb{N}}).

By using (
2.8.2
), the induction on
k_{1}+m
tells us that the morphism

E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{m_{1},k_{2}+2}\longrightarrow F^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{m_{1},k_{2}+2}

is a quasi-isomorphism for any
m_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
More generally,

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{1})}}_{k_{1}+m}{\rm gr}^{P^{(i_{2})}}_{k_{2}+l}E^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1}+m,k_{2}+l}/(E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1}+m-1,k_{2}+l}+E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{k_{1}+m,k_{2}+l-1}).

By using (
2.8.2
) again, the induction tells us that
the morphism

E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{m_{1}m_{2}}\longrightarrow F^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{2})}_{m_{1}m_{2}}

is a quasi-isomorphism for any
m_{1},m_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Since the filtrations
P^{(1)}
and
P^{(2)}
(resp.
Q^{(1)}
and
Q^{(2)}
) on

E^{\bullet}
(resp.
F^{\bullet}
) for
\bullet=q,q\pm 1
are finite,
the morphism (
2.11.2
) is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
Let us consider the set of morphisms

({\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})
whose elements are
the
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphisms in

{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})
.
Then it is easy to see that

({\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})
forms a
saturated multiplicative system
which is compatible with the triangulation
in the sense of

[
V
, II SM1)
\sim
SM6) p. 112]
.
Set

{\rm D}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}):={\rm K}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})_{({\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})}

(
\star=+
,
-
, b, nothing).

Definition 2.14
.

We call
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
,
{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

and
{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
the
derived category of bounded below
complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations
,
the
derived category of bounded above
complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations

and the
derived category of bounded complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules
with
n
-pieces of filtrations
, respectively.

In the rest of this section, we give notations which will be necessary in later sections.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
Let
{\rm K}^{\star}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))

(
\star=+
,
-
, b, nothing) be the category of
complexes of
\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A})
-modules
modulo homotopies
with respect to
\star=+,-,{\rm b},{\rm nothing}
.
Let
{\rm D}^{\star}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A})):={\rm K}^{\star}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))_{({\rm Qis})}

be its derived category.
Let

{\rm MF}^{n}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))
and

{\rm C}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))

be the categories of

\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A})
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations
and that of complexes of

\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A})
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations, respectively,
with respect to
\star=+,-,{\rm b},{\rm nothing}
.
Let

{\rm K}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))

be the category of complexes of

\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A})
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations
modulo
n
-filtered homotopies with respect to
\star=+,-,{\rm b},{\rm nothing}
.
By abuse of notation, let
({\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})

be the set of morphisms whose elements are
the
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphisms in

{\rm K}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))
.
Let

{\rm D}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))

be the derived
category of
{\rm K}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))

localized by the set
({\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})
of
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphisms of
complexes of
\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A})
-modules.

3
Strictly injective resolutions and
Rf_{*}

In this section we give the definitions of the specially injective resolution and
the strictly injective resolution of a complex with
n
-pieces of filtrations,
which are generalizations of the definitions in
[
B2
]
and
[
NS
]
.
We also define the right derived functor

Rf_{*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})

(n=1,2)

for a morphism
f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})

of ringed topoi.

The
special filtered module
in
[
B2
]
(and
[
NS
]
)
can be generalized in the following way for a general positive integer
n
.
For
{\cal A}
-modules
F
,
F^{(1)}_{l_{1}},\ldots,F^{(n)}_{l_{n}}
(
l_{1},\ldots,l_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
),
we set

\prod_{\cal A}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}):=F\times\underset{l_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}F^{(1)}_{l_{1}}\times\cdots\times\underset{l_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}F^{(n)}_{l_{n}}

with
n
-pieces of filtrations
\prod^{(i)}_{\cal A}
’s on
\prod_{\cal A}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

defined by

(\prod^{(i)}_{\cal A}(F,\{F^{(j)}\}_{j=1}^{n}))_{k}:=F\times\prod_{m=1}^{i-1}\underset{l_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}F^{(m)}_{l_{m}}\times\underset{l_{i}\leq k}{\prod}F^{(i)}_{l_{i}}\times\prod_{m=i+1}^{n}\underset{l_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}F^{(m)}_{l_{m}},

where
F^{(i)}:=\{F^{(i)}_{l_{i}}\}_{l_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

(i=1,\ldots,n)
.
Then we have an
{\cal A}
-module with
n
-pieces of filtrations:

(\prod_{\cal A}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),\{\prod^{(i)}_{\cal A}\}_{i=1}^{n}).

By abuse of notation, we denote this filtered module
simply by
\prod_{\cal A}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
We call
\prod_{\cal A}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
the
special
n
-filtered module
of

F
,
F^{(1)}_{l_{1}},\ldots,F^{(n)}_{l_{n}}
.

The following formula is a generalization of the formula
in
[
B2
]
(and
[
NS
, (1.1.0.2)]
):

Proposition 3.1
.

The following formula holds
:

(3.1.1)

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})}((E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),\prod_{\cal A}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))=

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\cal A}(E,F)\times

\displaystyle{\prod}_{i=1}^{n}{\prod}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\rm Hom}_{\cal A}(E/E^{(i)}_{k-1},F^{(i)}_{k}).

Proof.

Assume that a morphism

f\colon(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow\prod_{\cal A}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

in
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
is given.
Then we have morphisms
E\longrightarrow F
and
E/E^{(i)}_{k-1}\longrightarrow F^{(i)}_{k}
of

{\cal A}
-modules by using the projections

\prod_{\cal A}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow F
and

\prod_{\cal A}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow F^{(i)}_{k}
, respectively.

Assume that morphisms

E\longrightarrow F
and
E/E^{(i)}_{k-1}\longrightarrow F^{(i)}_{k}
for any
1\leq i\leq n
and
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}

are given.
Then we have a morphism

E\overset{{\rm proj}.}{\longrightarrow}E/E^{(i)}_{k-1}\longrightarrow F^{(i)}_{k}
.
Hence we have a morphism

E\longrightarrow F\times\underset{l_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}F^{(1)}_{l_{1}}\times\cdots\times\underset{l_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}F^{(n)}_{l_{n}}
.
This morphism induces a filtered morphism

(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow\prod_{\cal A}(F,\{F^{(j)}\}_{j=1}^{n})

since the composite morphism
E/E^{(i)}_{k}\longrightarrow E/E^{(i)}_{l-1}\longrightarrow F^{(i)}_{l}

for
l>k
is a zero morphism.
∎

For two objects
(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
,

(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\in{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
,
we define

(3.1.2)

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\cal A}((E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})):=

\displaystyle({\rm Hom}_{\cal A}(E,F),\{{\rm Hom}^{(i)}_{\cal A}(E,F)\}_{i=1}^{n})

\displaystyle\in{\rm MF}^{n}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))

in a well-known way:

(3.1.3)

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}^{(i)}_{\cal A}(E,F)_{k}:=\{f\in{\rm Hom}_{\cal A}(E,F)~|~f(E^{(i)}_{l})\subset F^{(i)}_{l+k}~(\forall l\in{\mathbb{Z}})\}.

For
1\leq i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{n}\leq n
and
k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
it is obvious that the following formula holds:

(3.1.4)

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}^{(i_{1})}_{\cal A}(E,F)_{k_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap{\rm Hom}^{(i_{n})}_{\cal A}(E,F)_{k_{n}}={\rm Hom}_{{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})}((E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n},(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\langle k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}\rangle).

We obtain a similar object

{\cal H}{\it om}_{\cal A}((E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\in{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})

in a similar way.

In the following we assume that
n\leq 2
.
The following definition is a nontrivial generalization of
the definition due to Berthelot (
[
B2
]
):

Definition 3.2

(
[
B2
]
for the case
n=1
).

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
We say that an object

(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
of
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
is

strictly injective
if it satisfies the
following two conditions:

(1)
J
and
J^{(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}

are injective
{\cal A}
-modules
for
1\leq\forall i_{1}\leq\forall i_{n}\leq n,\forall k_{1},\forall k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

(2) For a strictly injective morphism

(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

in
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
,
the induced morphism

{\rm Hom}_{\cal A}((F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\cal A}((E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

is an epimorphism.

In the following we assume that
n\leq 2
.
Set

(3.2.1)

\displaystyle{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A}):=\{(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})~|

\displaystyle~J\text{ and }J^{(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}\text{ are flasque }{\cal A}{\textrm{-}}\text{modules}

\displaystyle(1\leq\forall i_{1}\leq\forall i_{n}\leq n,\forall k_{1},\forall k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}})\},

Remark 3.3
.

One need not assume that
J
is an injective
{\cal A}
-module
in the definition (
3.2
)
because one can prove that the property (2) in (
3.2
) implies that

J
is an injective
{\cal A}
-module.
Indeed, assume that

(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
enjoys the property (2).
Let
J\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}K
be an injective morphism into an injective
{\cal A}
-module.
Consider the identity morphism
{\rm id}\colon(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Endow
K
with the filtration
K^{(i)}:={\rm Im}(J^{(i)}\longrightarrow K)

(i=1,2)
.
The induced morphism

(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is strict by (
2.2
).
Hence there exists a projection

p\colon(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}\longrightarrow(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
of the strict injective morphism

(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Set
L:={\rm Ker}(p\colon K\longrightarrow J)
.
Now it is obvious that
K=J\oplus L
and it is easy to see that

J
is an injective
{\cal A}
-module.

(3.3.1)

\displaystyle{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm inj}({\cal A}):=\{(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})~|

\displaystyle~J\text{ and }J^{(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}\text{ are injective }{\cal A}{\textrm{-}}\text{modules}

\displaystyle(\forall k_{1},\forall k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}},~1\leq\forall i_{1}\leq\forall i_{n}\leq n)\},

(3.3.2)

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A}):=\{(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})~|~\text{$(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})$
is a strictly injective
${\cal A}${{-}}module$\}$},

(3.3.3)

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm spinj}({\cal A}):=\{\prod_{\cal A}(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})~|~\text{$I$ and $I^{(i)}_{k}$ are injective
${\cal A}${{-}}modules}~(\forall k\in{\mathbb{Z}},1\leq\forall i\leq n)\}.

Then
{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A})\subset{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm inj}({\cal A})\subset{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A})
.
The following is a generalization of a result of Berthelot

[
B2
]
(and
[
NS
, (1.1.2)]
):

Proposition 3.4

(
[
B2
]
for the case
n=1
).

Assume that
\prod_{\cal A}(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm spinj}({\cal A})
.
Then
\prod_{\cal A}(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A})
.

Proof.

Note that, for
1\leq i_{1}<i_{m}\leq n

(1\leq m\leq n)

and
k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

((\prod_{\cal A}(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})))^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}=I\times\underset{l_{1}\leq k_{1},l_{m}\leq k_{m}}{\prod}(I^{(i_{1})}_{l_{1}}\times I^{(i_{m})}_{l_{m}})\times\underset{j\not\in\{i_{1},i_{m}\}}{\prod}\underset{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}I^{(j)}_{k}.

Hence
(\prod_{\cal A}(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})))^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}
is
an injective
{\cal A}
-module.

Let
(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be a strictly injective morphism.
Then the induced morphism

E/E^{(i)}_{k-1}\longrightarrow F/F^{(i)}_{k-1}
is injective.
Now (
3.4
) is obvious by (
3.1.1
)
since

I
and
I^{(i)}_{k}
are injective.
∎

Definition 3.5
.

We say that an
{\cal A}
-module

(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})

with
n
-pieces of filtrations is

n
-
filteredly flasque
,
n
-
filteredly injective

and
n
-
specially injective

if
(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A})
,

\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm inj}({\cal A})

and

\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm spinj}({\cal A})
,
respectively.

The category
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
has
enough special injectives in the following sense
(the following is a generalization of a result in
[
B2
]

(and
[
NS
, (1.1.4)]
)):

Proposition 3.6

(
[
B2
]
for the case
n=1
).

For an object

(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
,
there exists a strictly injective morphism

(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\prod_{\cal A}(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

with

\prod_{\cal A}(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm spinj}({\cal A})
.

Proof.

Though the following proof is similar to that of
[
NS
, (1.1.4)]
,
we give the complete proof because we use (
2.2
)
and we use the following proof in the proof of (
3.9
) below.

Let
E\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}I
and

E/E^{(i)}_{l-1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}I^{(i)}_{l}

be injective morphisms into injective
{\cal A}
-modules.
Set
J:=I\times{\prod}_{i=1}^{n}\underset{l\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}I^{(i)}_{l}
and

J^{(i)}_{k}:=I\times\prod_{m=1}^{i-1}\underset{l_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}I^{(m)}_{l_{m}}\times\underset{l_{i}\leq k}{\prod}I^{(i)}_{l_{i}}\times\prod_{m=i+1}^{n}\underset{l_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}I^{(m)}_{l_{m}}
.
There are natural injective morphisms

J^{(i)}_{k}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}J^{(i)}_{k+1}
and
J^{(i)}_{k}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}J
. Since
I
and
I^{(i)}_{k}

are injective
{\cal A}
-modules,

(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is an object of

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm spinj}({\cal A})
.

Two morphisms
E\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}I
and

E\overset{\rm proj.}{\longrightarrow}E/E^{(i)}_{l-1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}I^{(i)}_{l}

induce an injective morphism

E\longrightarrow J
. Furthermore, two composite morphisms

E^{(i)}_{k}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}E\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}I
and

E^{(i)}_{k}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}E\overset{{\rm proj}.}{\longrightarrow}E/E^{(i)}_{l-1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}I^{(i)}_{l}
induce an injective morphism

E^{(i)}_{k}\longrightarrow J^{(i)}_{k}
.

It remains to prove that
the morphism
(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is strict.
By (
2.2
), it suffices to prove that
the morphism
(E,E^{(i)})\longrightarrow(J,J^{(i)})
is strict.
Set
N^{(i)}_{k}:={\rm Im}(E\longrightarrow J)\cap J^{(i)}_{k}
.
Then
N^{(i)}_{k}
is isomorphic to
the kernel of the following composite morphism

E\longrightarrow J\overset{\rm proj.}{\longrightarrow}\underset{l>k}{\prod}I^{(i)}_{l}.

This kernel is nothing but
E^{(i)}_{k}

by the definition of
I^{(i)}_{l}

(l>k)
.
Hence the morphism
(E,E^{(i)})\longrightarrow(J,J^{(i)})
is strict.
∎

Definition 3.7
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
. Let
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be an object of

{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
.

(1)

(
[
B2
]
for the case
n=1
)

We say that an object

(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

with an
n
-filtered morphism

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is a
strictly injective resolution
of

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
if

(J^{q},\{J^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A})

for any
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}

and if the morphism

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}){\longrightarrow}(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is an
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphism which
induces a strictly injective morphism

(E^{q},\{E^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}){\longrightarrow}(J^{q},\{J^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

for any
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

(2)
We say that an object

(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

with an
n
-filtered morphism

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is an
n
-
filtered flasque resolution
,
an
n
-
filtered injective resolution

and an
n
-
specially injective resolution

of

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

if
(J^{q},\{J^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A})
,

\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm inj}({\cal A})
and

\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm spinj}({\cal A})
, respectively,
for any
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}

and if the morphism

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}){\longrightarrow}(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is an
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphism which induces
a strictly injective morphism

(E^{q},\{E^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}){\longrightarrow}(J^{q},\{J^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

for any
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Remark 3.8
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
Let
({\cal T},{\cal A})
be
a ringed topos with enough points.
Let
(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be
an object of

{\rm C}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
.
For an integer
p
, let
