(I^{pq},I^{pq(i)}_{k},d^{pq})_{q\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}

be the Godement resolution of
(F^{p},F^{p(i)}_{k})
.
Then the sequence

0\longrightarrow(F^{p},F^{p(i)}_{k})\longrightarrow(I^{p0(i)},I^{p0(i)}_{k})\overset{(-1)^{p}d^{p0}}{\longrightarrow}(I^{p1(i)},I^{p1(i)}_{k})\overset{(-1)^{p}d^{p1}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\quad(p,k\in{\mathbb{Z}})

gives an
n
-filtered flasque resolution

s(I^{\bullet\bullet},\{I^{\bullet\bullet(i)}_{k}\}_{i=1}^{n})
of

(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
because
taking a point of
{\cal T}
and the direct image of abelian sheaves by
a morphism of ringed topoi are compatible
with the finite intersection of
{\cal A}
-modules.

The example in (
2.3
) and the remark (
2.12
) tell us that,
even if
f\colon(E^{\bullet},E^{\bullet(i)})\longrightarrow(F^{\bullet},F^{\bullet(i)})

is a filtered quasi-isomorphism for
1\leq\forall i\leq n
,

f\colon(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is not necessarily an
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphism in
{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})
.
The proposition
[
NS
, (1.1.7)]
(=the following proposition for the case
n=1
)
does not imply the following proposition for the case
n=2
.

Proposition 3.9
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
For an object

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
,
there exists a
specially injective resolution

(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
of

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.

Proof.

Because the following proof is not only an obvious imitation of
[
NS
, (1.1.7)]
,
we give the complete proof
(we have to use the simple argument in the proof (
2.2
)
and we need an additional argument in order to
obtain (
3.9.5
) below).

We may assume that
E^{q}=0
for
q<0
.
Assume that we are given

(J^{0},\{J^{0(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(J^{1},\{J^{1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),\ldots,(J^{q},\{J^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}_{\rm spinj}({\cal A}).

We consider
{\cal A}
-modules

J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1}
and

J^{q(i)}_{k}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i)}_{k}}E^{q+1(i)}_{k}

(i=1,\ldots,n)
.
Using the strictness of the morphism

(E^{q},E^{q(i)})\longrightarrow(J^{q},J^{q(i)})
, we can easily check that
the natural morphism

J^{q(i)}_{k}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i)}_{k}}E^{q+1(i)}_{k}\longrightarrow J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1}
is injective.
Hence

\{J^{q(i)}_{k}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i)}_{k}}E^{q+1(i)}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

defines a filtration on
J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1}
.
The natural morphism

E^{q+1}\owns s\longmapsto(0,s)\in J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1}
induces a
filtered morphism

(E^{q+1},\{E^{q+1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1},\{\{J^{q(i)}_{k}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i)}_{k}}E^{q+1(i)}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\}_{i=1}^{n}).

It is immediate to check
that, for each
i
, the filtered morphism

(E^{q+1},E^{q+1(i)})\longrightarrow(J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1},\{J^{q(i)}_{k}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i)}_{k}}E^{q+1(i)}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})

is strict.
Let
I^{q+1}
and
I^{q+1(i)}_{k}
be injective
{\cal A}
-modules such
that there exist the following injective morphisms of

{\cal A}
-modules:

(3.9.1)

J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}I^{q+1},\quad J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1}/J^{q(i)}_{k}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i)}_{k}}E^{q+1(i)}_{k}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}I^{q+1(i)}_{k+1}.

Set

J^{q+1}:=I^{q+1}\times{\prod}_{i=1}^{n}\underset{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}I^{q+1(i)}_{k}

and

J^{q+1(i)}_{k}:=I^{q+1}\times\prod_{1\leq m\not=i\leq n}\underset{l_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}I^{q+1(m)}_{l_{m}}\times\underset{l_{i}\leq k}{\prod}I^{q+1(i)}_{l_{i}}

for each
1\leq i\leq n
.
Then
(J^{q+1},\{J^{q+1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\allowbreak\in{\cal I}_{\rm spinj}({\cal A})
.
By (
3.1.1
) and (
3.9.1
),
we have a natural injective morphism

(3.9.2)

(J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1},\{\{J^{q(i)}_{k}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i)}_{k}}E^{q+1(i)}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\}_{i=1}^{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(J^{q+1},\{J^{q+1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}).

Since the morphism
E^{q}\longrightarrow J^{q}
is injective,
the morphism
E^{q+1}\longrightarrow J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1}
is injective,
and so is the following composite morphism

E^{q+1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}J^{q+1}.

In fact, we have a morphism

(E^{q+1},\{E^{q+1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(J^{q+1},\{J^{q+1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Using a morphism

J^{q}\owns s\longmapsto(s,0)\in J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1}
and the morphism
(
3.9.2
),
we have a morphism

(J^{q},\{J^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(J^{q+1},\{J^{q+1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
For a fixed
i
, by the proof of (
3.6
)
and by the strictness of the morphism

(E^{q+1},\{E^{q+1(i)}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})\longrightarrow(J^{q}{\oplus}_{E^{q}}E^{q+1},\{J^{q(i)}_{k}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i)}_{k}}\allowbreak E^{q+1(i)}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})
,
the morphism

(E^{q+1},\{E^{q+1(i)}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})\longrightarrow(J^{q+1},\{J^{q+1(i)}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})
is strict.
Hence we obtain
(J^{\bullet},\allowbreak\{J^{\bullet(i)}_{k}\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}})
inductively.

We claim that
(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is filteredly quasi-isomorphic to
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
To prove this, we first note that

(3.9.3)

\displaystyle{\rm Ker}(J^{q(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}\longrightarrow J^{q+1(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}})={\rm Ker}(J^{q(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}}E^{q+1(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}})\quad(q\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Indeed, the problem is local.
Because the morphism

\displaystyle J^{q(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}}E^{q+1(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}\longrightarrow J^{q+1(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}\quad(1\leq l\leq n)

is injective,

(3.9.4)

\displaystyle{\rm Ker}(J^{q(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}\longrightarrow J^{q+1(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}})=\bigcap_{l=1}^{n}{\rm Ker}(J^{q(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}\longrightarrow J^{q(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}}E^{q+1(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}).

Let
s
be a local section of the right hand side of (
3.9.4
).
Let
g^{q}\colon E^{q}\longrightarrow J^{q}
be the constructed morphism.
Then we may assume that there exists a local section
t_{l}
of
E^{q(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}

such that
s=g^{q}(t_{l})
and
d(t_{l})=0
for
1\leq\forall l\leq n
.
Hence
g^{q}(t_{l})=g^{q}(t_{m})
for
1\leq\forall l,\forall m\leq n
.
Because
g^{q}
is injective,
t_{l}=t_{m}\in E^{q(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}
.
Hence

(3.9.5)

\displaystyle{\rm Ker}(J^{q(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}\longrightarrow J^{q(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}}E^{q+1(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}})

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{l=1}^{n}{\rm Ker}(J^{q(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}\longrightarrow J^{q(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}{\oplus}_{E^{q(i_{l})}_{k_{l}}}E^{q+1(i_{l})}_{k_{l}})

\displaystyle={\rm Ker}(J^{q(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}\longrightarrow J^{q+1(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}).

Let
\triangle
be nothing or
(i_{1}i_{n})
for
1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{n}\leq n
and
let
\circ
be nothing or
n
-pieces of integers
k_{1}k_{n}
.
By (
3.9.5
) we see that
the morphism

{\rm Ker}(E^{q\triangle}_{\circ}\longrightarrow E^{q+1\triangle}_{\circ})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(J^{q\triangle}_{\circ}\longrightarrow J^{q+1\triangle}_{\circ})

is an epimorphism. In particular,
the morphism

{\cal H}^{q}(E^{\bullet}_{\circ})\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q}(J^{\bullet}_{\circ})

is an epimorphism.
Furthermore, the morphism

J^{q-1}_{\circ}\longrightarrow J^{q}_{\circ}
factors through

J^{q-1}_{\circ}\longrightarrow J^{q-1}_{\circ}{\oplus}_{E^{q-1}_{\circ}}E^{q}_{\circ}
by (
3.9.5
).
Note again that

J^{q-1}_{\circ}{\oplus}_{E^{q-1}_{\circ}}E^{q}_{\circ}\longrightarrow J^{q}_{\circ}
is an injective morphism.
Because the inverse image of

{\rm Im}(J^{q-1}_{\circ}\longrightarrow J^{q}_{\circ})
by the morphism

E^{q}_{\circ}\longrightarrow J^{q}_{\circ}

is equal to
the inverse image of

{\rm Im}(J^{q-1}_{\circ}\longrightarrow J^{q-1}_{\circ}{\oplus}_{E^{q-1}_{\circ}}E^{q}_{\circ})
,
the morphism

{\rm Ker}(E^{q}_{\circ}\longrightarrow E^{q+1}_{\circ})/{\rm Im}(E^{q-1}_{\circ}\longrightarrow E^{q}_{\circ})\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q}(J^{\bullet}_{\circ})

is an injective morphism. Consequently the morphism

{\cal H}^{q}(E^{\bullet}_{\circ}){\longrightarrow}{\cal H}^{q}(J^{\bullet}_{\circ})
is an isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 3.10
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
Let
f^{\bullet}\colon(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be a morphism in

{\rm C}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
. Then there exists a
morphism

g^{\bullet}\colon(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

in
{\rm C}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
such that

(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

(
resp.
(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

is a specially injective resolution of

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

(
resp.
(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

and such that the following diagram is commutative
:

\begin{CD}(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})@>{\subset}>{}>(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\\
@V{f^{\bullet}}V{}V@V{}V{g^{\bullet}}V\\
(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})@>{\subset}>{}>(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}).\end{CD}

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of

[
NS
, (1.1.8)]
by using the proof of (
3.9
).
∎

For an additive full subcategory

{\cal I}
of
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
,
let
{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I})

be the category of
the bounded below complexes with
n
-pieces of filtrations
whose components belong to

{\cal I}
.

Lemma 3.11
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
Let
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be
a complex of
{\cal A}
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations
and let

(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be an object of

{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A}))
.
Assume that
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is strictly exact.
Let
f\colon(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

be a morphism of complexes with
n
-pieces of filtrations.
Then
f
is
n
-filteredly homotopic to zero.

Proof.

By the definition of the strict injectivity,
the same argument as
that in the classical case works.
∎

Lemma 3.12
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
. Then the following hold
:

(1)
Let
(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be an object of

{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A}))
.
Let

s\colon(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

be an
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphism.
Then
s
induces an isomorphism

s^{*}\colon{\rm Hom}_{{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})}((F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})).

(2)
If a morphism
s\colon(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is
an
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphism from an object of

{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A}))
to
a complex of
{\cal A}
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations,
then
s
has an
n
-filtered homotopy inverse.

Proof.

By using (
3.11
),
the proof is the same as that of
[
NS
, (1.1.10)]
.

(2): The proof is the same as that of

[
H
, I (4.5)]
by using
(
3.11
), though
there is an error in signs in
the proof of
[
H
, I (4.5)]

(see
[
NS
, (1.1.11)]
for this).
∎

Corollary 3.13
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.

(1)
The following equalities hold
:

\displaystyle{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

\displaystyle={\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A}))_{({\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})}={\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n}_{\rm inj}({\cal A}))_{({\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})}

\displaystyle={\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A}))={\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n}_{\rm spinj}({\cal A})).

(2)
Set
{\cal I}:={\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A})
,

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm inj}({\cal A})
,

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A})
or
{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm spinj}({\cal A})
.
Let
f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})
be a morphism of ringed topoi.
Then there exists the right derived functor

Rf_{*}\colon{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})

of
f_{*}
such that

Rf_{*}([(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})])=[(f_{*}(I^{\bullet}),\{f_{*}(I^{\bullet(i)})\}_{i=1}^{n})]

for an object
(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I})
.
Here
[~]
is
the localization functor.

(3)
Let

f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})
and

g\colon({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime\prime})
be morphisms of ringed topoi.
Then
R(gf)_{*}=Rg_{*}Rf_{*}
.

Proof.

(1): The first
two equalities follow from
(
3.9
) and the proof of

[
H
, I (5.1)]
.
The last two equalities follow
from the proof of
[
H
, I (4.7)]
and
(
3.12
) (2).

(2): (2) follows from the argument in the proof of
[
H
, I (5.1)]
.

(3): (3) follows by setting

{\cal I}:={\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A})
in (2).
∎

Proposition 3.14
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
Let
\star
be
+
,
-
,
b
or nothing.
For
1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{n}\leq n

and
k_{1},k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
the intersection functor

(3.14.1)

\bigcap^{(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}\colon{\rm DF}^{\star n}({\cal A})\owns[(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})]\longmapsto[E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}]\in D^{\star}({\cal A})

is well-defined. Here
D^{\star}({\cal A}):=K^{\star}({\cal A})_{({\rm FQis})}
as usual.

Proof.

This follows from the definition of
{\rm DF}^{\star n}({\cal A})
.
∎

4
Strictly flat resolutions and
Lf^{*}

In this section we give the definitions of
the specially flat resolution and
the strictly flat resolution of
a bifiltered complex. These are generalizations of the definitions in

[
B2
]
and
[
NS
]
.
The definitions in this section are more complicated than those in the previous section
because the definitions which are dual to those in the previous section are not appropriate.
We also define the left derived functor

Lf^{*}\colon{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

(n=1,2)

for a morphism
f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})

of ringed topoi.

First we define another
special filtered module

(see
[
B2
]
and
[
NS
]
for the case
n=1
).

For two objects
(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
,

(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
,
we define the
n
-filtered tensor product

(E{\otimes}_{\cal A}F,\{(E\otimes_{\cal A}F)^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

of
(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
and

(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
as follows:

(E\otimes_{\cal A}F)^{(i)}_{k}:={\rm Im}(\bigoplus_{l+m=k}E^{(i)}_{l}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{(i)}_{m}\longrightarrow E\otimes_{\cal A}F).

Let
{\cal P}(n)
be the set of nonempty subsets of the set
\{1,\ldots,n\}

and set
{\cal P}(n)_{i}:=\{P\in{\cal P}(n)~|~i\in P\}
for
1\leq i\leq n
.
For
P\in{\cal P}(n)
,
set
m(P):=\#P
. Let

i_{1}<\ldots<i_{m(P)}
be the elements of
P
:
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{m(P)}\}=P
.
Assume that, for any
P\in{\cal P}(n)
, we are given an

{\cal A}
-module
E^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{m(P)})}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}}
.
We set
E^{P}:=\{E^{(i_{1}\cdots i_{m(P)})}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}}\}_{l_{1},\ldots,l_{m(P)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

and

\Sigma_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)}):=E\oplus\bigoplus_{P\in{\cal P}(n)}\bigoplus_{\{l_{1},\ldots,l_{m(P)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}}E^{P}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m_{(P)}}}

with
n
-pieces of filtrations
\Sigma^{(i)}_{\cal A}
’s defined by

\displaystyle(\Sigma^{(i)}_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)}))_{k}:=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{P=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{m(P)}\}\in{\cal P}(n)_{i}}\bigoplus_{\{l_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}},\ldots,l_{m(P)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}~|~l_{m}\leq k~{\rm for}~i_{m}=i~{\rm for}~1\leq m\leq m(P)\}}E^{P}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}}

for
1\leq i\leq n
.
Then
(\Sigma_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)}),\Sigma^{(i)}_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)}))
is
an object of
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
.
For simplicity of notation, we denote this filtered module by

\Sigma_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})
.
The filtered module
\Sigma_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})

is a highly nontrivial generalization of
the special filtered module defined in
[
B2
]
(and
[
NS
]
).
By the definition of the filtration
\Sigma^{(i)}_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})
,
we obtain the following formula:

(4.0.1)

\displaystyle(\Sigma^{(i_{1}\ldots i_{n})}_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)}))_{k_{1}\cdots k_{n}}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{j=1}^{n}(\Sigma^{(i_{j})}_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)}))_{k_{j}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{P=\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{m(P)}\}\in\cap_{j=i_{1}}^{i_{n}}{\cal P}(n)_{j}}\bigoplus_{\{l_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}},\ldots,l_{m(P)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}~|~l_{j}\leq k_{m}~{\rm for}~e_{j}=i_{m}~{\rm for}~{\rm some}~1\leq m\leq n\}}{\bigoplus}E^{P}_{l_{1},\ldots,l_{{m(P)}}}.

The following is a generalization of a formula
in
[
B2
]
and
[
NS
, (1.1.12.1)]
:

Proposition 4.1
.

The following formula holds
:

(4.1.1)

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})}({\Sigma}_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)}),(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))=

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\cal A}(E,F)\times{}{\prod}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)}\underset{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}{\rm Hom}_{\cal A}(E^{P}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}},F^{P}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}})

Proof.

Assume that we are given a filtered morphism

{\Sigma}_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})\longrightarrow(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Then we have morphisms

E\longrightarrow F
and
E^{P}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}}\longrightarrow F
.
By the definition of
\Sigma^{(i)}_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})
,
the latter morphism factors through
F^{P}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}}
.

Conversely assume that we are are given morphisms

E\longrightarrow F
and
E^{P}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}}\longrightarrow F^{P}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}}
.
Obviously we have the composite morphism

E^{P}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}}\longrightarrow F^{P}_{l_{1}\cdots l_{m(P)}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}F
.
This composite morphism and the morphism
E\longrightarrow F

induces a filtered morphism

{\Sigma}_{\cal A}(E,\{E^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})\longrightarrow(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
∎

The following is a highly nontrivial generalization of the definition of
the strictly flatness in
[
B2
]
(and
[
NS
]
).
This definition plays a central role in the definition of
the derived tensor product
\otimes^{L}_{\cal A}
for two complexes of
{\cal A}
-modules
with
n
-pieces of filtrations defined
in §
6
below.

Definition 4.2
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
We say that an object

(Q,\{Q^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
of
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
is

strictly flat
if it satisfies the
following two conditions:

(1)
Q
and

Q/\sum_{j=1}^{N}Q^{(i^{j}_{1}i^{j}_{n})}_{k^{j}_{1}k^{j}_{n}}

(N\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1},1\leq\forall i^{j}_{1}\leq\forall i^{j}_{n}\leq n,\forall k^{j}_{1},\forall k^{j}_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}})

are flat
{\cal A}
-modules.

(2) For a strictly injective morphism

(E,\{E^{(i)\bullet}\}_{i=1}^{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(F,\{F^{(i)\bullet}\}_{i=1}^{n})
,
the induced morphism

(Q{\otimes}_{\cal A}E,\{(Q\otimes_{\cal A}E)^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(Q{\otimes}_{\cal A}F,\{(Q\otimes_{\cal A}F)^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is a strictly injective morphism.

The following remark (1) is very important.

Remark 4.3
.

(1) The dual definition of the first property of
(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

in (
3.2
) (1) is the following statement:

“
Q
and
Q/Q^{(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}

(1\leq\forall i_{1}\leq\forall i_{n}\leq n,\forall k_{1},\forall k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}})

are flat
{\cal A}
-modules.”

However this notion is not appropriate in the definition of the derived tensor product

\otimes^{L}_{\cal A}
below.

(2) Let
(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be an object of

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A})
for any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}

Then, for any positive integer
N
,

\sum_{j=1}^{N}J^{(i^{j}_{1}\cdots i^{j}_{n})}_{k^{j}_{1}\cdots k^{j}_{n}}
is automatically flasque for

1\leq\forall i^{j}_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\forall i^{j}_{n}\leq n,\forall k^{j}_{1},\ldots,\forall k^{j}_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

In the following we assume that
n\leq 2
.
Let us consider
the following additive full subcategories
of
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
:

(4.3.1)

\displaystyle{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm fl}({\cal A}):=\{(Q,\{Q^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})~|~

\displaystyle Q\text{ and }Q/\sum_{j=1}^{N}Q^{(i^{j}_{1}i^{j}_{n})}_{k^{j}_{1}k^{j}_{n}}\text{ are flat }{\cal A}{\textrm{-}}\text{modules}

\displaystyle~(N\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1},1\leq\forall i^{j}_{1}\leq\forall i^{j}_{n}\leq n,\forall k^{j}_{1},\forall k^{j}_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}})\},

(4.3.2)

{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm stfl}({\cal A}):=\{(Q,\{Q^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})~|~\text{$(Q,\{Q^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})$ is a strictly
flat ${\cal A}${{-}}module$\}$},

(4.3.3)

\displaystyle{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm spfl}({\cal A}):=\{\Sigma_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})~|~

\displaystyle\text{$Q$ and $Q^{P}_{k_{1}k_{m(P)}}$
are flat ${\cal A}${{-}}modules}~{\rm for}~

\displaystyle\forall P\in{\cal P}(n)~{\rm and}~\forall k_{1},\forall k_{m(P)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}.

Then
{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm stfl}({\cal A})\subset{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm fl}({\cal A})
.

Definition 4.4
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
We say that an object

(Q,\{Q^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
is

n
-
filteredly flat

(resp.
n
-specially flat
)
if
(Q,\{Q^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm fl}({\cal A})

(resp.
(Q,\{Q^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm spfl}({\cal A})
).

Lemma 4.5

(
[
B2
]
for the case
n=1
).

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
Then

{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm spfl}({\cal A})\subset{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm stfl}({\cal A})
.

Proof.

Let
\Sigma_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})

be an object of
{\cal Q}_{\rm spfl}({\cal A})
.
It is easy to see that

\Sigma_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})
and

\Sigma_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})/\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\Sigma_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)}))^{(i^{j}_{1}i^{j}_{n})}_{k^{j}_{1}k^{j}_{n}}
are flat
{\cal A}
-modules.

Let
\iota\colon(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be a strictly injective morphism
and let
k
be an integer.
Denote by the same symbol
\iota
the induced injective
morphism

\Sigma_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})\otimes_{\cal A}E\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})\otimes_{\cal A}F
.
Let
s
be a local section of

\iota(\Sigma_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})\otimes_{\cal A}E)\cap(\Sigma^{(i)}_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})\otimes_{\cal A}F)_{k}
.
By the definition of the filtration on the filtered tensor product,

s
is a finite sum of local sections of

\bigoplus_{P=\{i_{1}i_{m(P)}\}\in{\cal P}(n)_{i}}\bigoplus_{\{l_{1},l_{m(P)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}~|~l_{m}\leq l~{\rm for}~i_{m}=i~{\rm for}~1\leq m\leq m(P)\}}Q^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}\otimes_{\cal A}F_{j}

(l+j\allowbreak\leq k)
. Because

\displaystyle(Q^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}\otimes_{\cal A}F_{j})\cap(Q^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}\otimes_{\cal A}\iota(E))

\displaystyle=Q^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}\otimes_{\cal A}(F_{j}\cap\iota(E))

\displaystyle=Q^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}\otimes_{\cal A}\iota(E_{j}),

s
is a local section of

\iota((\Sigma^{(i)}_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})\otimes_{\cal A}E)_{k})
.
Now we can complete the proof of (
4.5
)
by (
2.2
).
∎

Proposition 4.6

(
[
B2
]
for the case
n=1
).

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
For an
{\cal A}
-module

(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
with
n
-pieces of filtrations,
there exists a strict epimorphism

\Sigma_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})\longrightarrow(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

with

\Sigma_{\cal A}(Q,\{Q^{P}\}_{P\in{\cal P}(n)})\in{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm spfl}({\cal A})
.

Proof.

Recall the functor

L^{0}\colon\{{\cal A}\text{-modules}\}\longrightarrow\{\text{flat }{\cal A}\text{-modules}\}

(
[
BO
, §7]
):
for an
{\cal A}
-module,

L^{0}(E)
is, by definition, the sheafification of the presheaf

(U\longmapsto\text{a }\text{free }\Gamma(U,{\cal A})\text{-module
with basis }\Gamma(U,E)\setminus\{0\}).

The natural morphism
L^{0}(E)\longrightarrow E
is an epimorphism.

Let
Q\longrightarrow E
and

Q^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}\longrightarrow E^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}

(
P\in{\cal P}
)
be epimorphisms from flat
{\cal A}
-modules.
Set

R:=Q\oplus\bigoplus_{P\in{\cal P}}\underset{l_{1},l_{m(P)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\bigoplus}Q^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}

and

R^{(i)}_{k}:=\bigoplus_{P=\{i_{1},i_{m(P)}\}\in{\cal P}_{i}}\bigoplus_{\{l_{1},l_{m(P)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}~|~l_{j}\leq k~{\rm for}~i_{m}=i~{\rm for}~{\rm any}~1\leq m\leq m(P)\}}Q^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}.

Then

(R,\{R^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is an object of
{\cal Q}_{\rm spfl}({\cal A})
.
The morphisms
Q\longrightarrow E
and

Q^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}{\longrightarrow}E^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}E

induce an epimorphism

R\longrightarrow E
. The morphism

Q^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}\longrightarrow E^{P}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}E^{P}_{k_{1}k_{m(P)}}

(l_{j}\leq k_{j})
induces an epimorphism

R^{P}_{k_{1}k_{m(P)}}\longrightarrow E^{P}_{k_{1}k_{m(P)}}
.
Obviously the morphism
R\longrightarrow E
is strict.
Thus (
4.6
) follows.
∎

Definition 4.7
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
Let
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be an object of

{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
.

(1) We say that an object

(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

with an
n
-filtered morphism

(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is a
strictly flat resolution
of

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

if
(Q^{q},Q^{q(i)}_{k})\in{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm stfl}({\cal A})
for any

q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and if the morphism

(Q^{\bullet},Q^{\bullet(i)})\longrightarrow(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is
an
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphism which induces
a strict epimorphism

(Q^{q},Q^{q(i)})\longrightarrow(E^{q},\{E^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

for any
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

(2) We say that an object

(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

with an
n
-filtered morphism

(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is a
filtered flat resolution

(resp.
specially flat resolution
)
of
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

if
(Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm fl}({\cal A})

(resp.
(Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm spfl}({\cal A})
)
for any
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and if the morphism

(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is an
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphism
which induces a strict epimorphism

(Q^{q},Q^{q(i)})\longrightarrow(E^{q},\{E^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

for any
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

The following is a more nontrivial result than (
3.9
) at first glance
because we cannot use (
2.2
):

Proposition 4.8

(
[
B2
]
for the case
n=1
).

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
For an object

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
,
there exists a specially flat resolution

(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
of
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.

Proof.

Let
{\triangle}
and
\circ
be as in the proof of (
3.9
).
We may assume that
E^{q}=0
for
q>0
.
Assume that we are given

(Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),\ldots,(Q^{0},\{Q^{0(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
for
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{<0}
.
Let the notations be as in (
3.9
).
Consider the fiber product

(Q^{q}\times_{E^{q}}E^{q-1},\{Q^{q(i)}\times_{E^{q(i)}}E^{q-1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Obviously the morphism

{\rm Ker}(Q^{q\triangle}_{\circ}\times_{E^{q\triangle}_{\circ}}E^{q-1\triangle}_{\circ}\longrightarrow Q^{q\triangle}_{\circ})\longrightarrow{\rm Ker}(E^{q-1\triangle}_{\circ}\longrightarrow E^{q\triangle}_{\circ})

is surjective.
Consider the kernel
I^{q-1\triangle}_{\circ}
of the following morphism

\displaystyle{\rm Ker}(Q^{q\triangle}_{\circ}\times_{E^{q\triangle}_{\circ}}E^{q-1\triangle}_{\circ}\longrightarrow Q^{q\triangle}_{\circ})\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q-1}(E^{\bullet\triangle}_{\circ})

for the case where
\triangle
is nothing or
(i)
for
1\leq i\leq n
.
Set

\displaystyle Q^{q-1}:=L^{0}(I^{q-1})\bigoplus_{P\in{\cal P}}\bigoplus_{\{l_{1},l_{m(P)}\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}}L^{0}(I^{P,q-1}_{l_{1}l_{m(P)}})

and

\displaystyle Q^{q-1(i)}_{k}:=\bigoplus_{P=\{i_{1},i_{m(P)}\}\in{\cal P}_{i}}\bigoplus_{\{l_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}},\ldots,l_{m-1}\in{\mathbb{Z}},l_{m}\leq k,l_{m+1}\in{\mathbb{Z}},\ldots,l_{{m(P)}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}~|~i_{m}=i~{\rm for}~{\rm some}~1\leq m\leq m(P)\}}L^{0}(I^{P,q-1}_{l_{1},l_{{m(P)}}})

for
1\leq i\leq n
.
Then
(Q^{q-1},\{Q^{q-1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is an object of
{\cal Q}_{\rm spfl}({\cal A})
.
By the definition of
Q^{q-1(i)}_{k}
(cf. (
4.0.1
)),

\displaystyle Q^{q-1(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}=\bigoplus_{P=\{j_{1},j_{m(P)}\}\in{\cal P}_{i}}\bigoplus_{\{l_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}},\ldots,l_{m-1}\in{\mathbb{Z}},l_{p}\leq k_{p},l_{m+1}\in{\mathbb{Z}},\ldots,l_{{m(P)}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}~|~j_{p}=i_{p}~{\rm for}~p=1,n\}}L^{0}(I^{P,q-1}_{l_{1},l_{{m(P)}}}).

Hence we see that the morphism

(4.8.1)

\displaystyle{\cal H}^{q-1}(Q^{\bullet\triangle}_{\circ})\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{q-1}(E^{\bullet\triangle}_{\circ})

is an isomorphism for the case
\triangle
is nothing or
(i_{1}i_{n})
.
∎

For an additive full subcategory

{\cal Q}
of
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
,
let
{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal Q})

be the category of
the bounded above complexes with
n
-pieces of filtrations
whose components belong to

{\cal Q}
.

Corollary 4.9
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
. Then the following hold
:

(1)
The following equalities hold
:

{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})={\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm fl}({\cal A}))_{({\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})}={\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm stfl}({\cal A}))_{({\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})}={\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm spfl}({\cal A}))_{({\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})}.

(2)
Let
{\cal Q}^{\prime}{}^{n}:={\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm fl}({\cal A}^{\prime})
,

{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm stfl}({\cal A}^{\prime})

or
{\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm spfl}({\cal A}^{\prime})
.
Let
f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})
be
a morphism of ringed topoi. Then
there exists the left derived functor

Lf^{*}\colon{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
such that

Lf^{*}[(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})]=[(f^{*}(Q^{\bullet}),\{f^{*}(Q^{\bullet(i)})\}_{i=1}^{n})]

for an object

(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal Q}^{\prime}{}^{n})
.

(3)
Let

f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})

and
g\colon({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime\prime})

be morphisms of ringed topoi.
Then
L(gf)^{*}=Lf^{*}Lg^{*}
.

Proof.

(1) and (2) are obvious. (3) follows
by setting
{\cal Q}:={\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm fl}({\cal A})
in (2).
∎

5
{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}

In this section we define
the derived homomorphism functor
{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}

from bounded above complexes with
n
-pieces of filtrations
to bounded below complexes with
n
-pieces of filtrations for
n\leq 2
.
The results in this section are generalizations of results in
[
NS
, (1.2)]
.

As in
[
H
, p. 63]
, we set

{\rm Hom}^{m}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})):=

\underset{q\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}{\rm Hom}_{\cal A}((E^{q},\{E^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{q+m},\{F^{q+m(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

for

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})
.
Then we have an object

{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\in{\rm CF}^{n}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))

of
\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A})
-modules;
the boundary morphism

{\rm Hom}^{m}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\longrightarrow

{\rm Hom}^{m+1}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

is defined as in
[
BBM
, p. 4]
and
[
Co
, p. 10]
:

d^{m}:=\underset{q\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{\prod}((-1)^{m+1}d_{E}^{q}+d_{F}^{q+m}).

(Recall the filtration (
3.1.3
).)

For a sequence
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{n})
of integers,
an
m
-cocycle of

\bigcap_{j=1}^{n}{\rm Hom}^{\bullet(j)}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))_{k_{j}}

corresponds to an
n
-filtered morphism

E^{\bullet}\longrightarrow F^{\bullet}[m]\langle\underline{k}\rangle
.
An
m
-coboundary of

\bigcap_{j=1}^{n}{\rm Hom}^{\bullet(j)}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))_{k_{j}}

corresponds to a morphism

E^{\bullet}\longrightarrow F^{\bullet}[m]\langle\underline{k}\rangle

which is homotopic to zero.
Hence

(5.0.1)

H^{m}(\bigcap_{j=1}^{n}{\rm Hom}^{\bullet(j)}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))_{k_{j}})=

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})[m]\langle\underline{k}\rangle).

In particular,

(5.0.2)

H^{0}(\bigcap_{j=1}^{n}{\rm Hom}^{\bullet(j)}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))_{0})=

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})).

More generally,
for
1\leq i_{1}<i_{p}\leq n

(1\leq p\leq n)

and a sequence
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{p})
of integers,
we have

(5.0.3)

\displaystyle H^{m}(\bigcap_{k_{1}k_{p}}^{(i_{1}i_{p})}{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})))

\displaystyle={\rm Hom}_{{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i_{q})}\}_{q=1}^{p}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i_{q})}\}_{q=1}^{p})[m]\langle\underline{k}\rangle).

To define the derived functor of the functor

{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal A}(\bullet,\bullet)\colon{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})^{\circ}\times{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\rm A})\longrightarrow{\rm KF}^{n}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A})),

we have to check the following:

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be an object of
{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})

and let
(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be an object of

{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A}))
.
Assume that one of the following two
conditions holds.

(1)

(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is strictly exact.

(2)

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is strictly exact.

Then

{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

is strictly exact.

Proof.

(1): By the definition of the strict injectivity,
there exist
{\cal A}
-modules

J^{q}
and
J^{q(i)}_{k}

(i=1,n,q,k\in{\mathbb{Z}})

satisfying the following three conditions:

(i)
J^{q(i)}_{k-1}\subset J^{q(i)}_{k}\subset J^{q(i)}
,

(ii)
(I^{q(i)},\{I^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\simeq(J^{q-1},\{J^{q-1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\oplus(J^{q},\{J^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
,

(iii) the boundary morphism
d\colon(I^{q},\{I^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(I^{q+1},\{I^{q+1(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is identified with the induced morphism by the morphisms

J^{q-1}\longrightarrow 0
and
J^{q}\overset{\rm id}{\longrightarrow}J^{q}
.
By (
5.0.3
), we have only
to construct a filtered homotopy for a morphism

f\in{\rm Hom}_{{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))
,
which is easy.

(2): By (
5.0.3
) and by
the definition of the strict injectivity,
the same argument as
that in the classical case works.
∎

By (
5.1
) we obtain
the following derived functor

{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}_{\cal A}\colon{\rm DF}^{n}({\cal A})^{\circ}\times{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow{\rm DF}^{n}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A})).

By (
3.14
)
we obtain the following functor

\bigcap^{(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}_{\cal A}\colon{\rm DF}^{n}({\cal A})^{\circ}\times{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow D(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))

for
1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{n}\leq n

and
k_{1},k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

The following includes the adjunction formula in
[
B2
]
and

[
NS
, (1.2.2)]
:

Theorem 5.2

(
Adjunction formula).

Let

f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})
be
a morphism of ringed topoi.
Let
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

(
resp.
(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

be an object of

{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})

and
{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}_{\cal A}(Lf^{*}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\overset{=}{\longrightarrow}

{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}_{{\cal A}^{\prime}}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),Rf_{*}((F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})))

in
{\rm DF}^{n}(\Gamma({\cal T},{\cal A}))
.
The isomorphism above satisfies
the transitive condition
(cf.
[
B1
, V Proposition 3.3.1]
)
.

Proof.

The following proof is the bifiltered version of that of
[
NS
, (1.2.2)]
(cf.
[
B1
, V Proposition 3.3.1]
).

Let
(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

be a strictly injective resolution of
(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Let
(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be a filtered flat resolution
of
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Let
(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}({\cal I}_{\rm stinj})

be a strictly injective resolution of
f_{*}((I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))
.
Then we have the following composite morphism

(5.2.1)

{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal A}(f^{*}((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})),(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))={\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{{\cal A}^{\prime}}((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),f_{*}((I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})))

\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{{\cal A}^{\prime}}((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\overset{\sim}{\longleftarrow}{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{{\cal A}^{\prime}}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}),(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})).

Here the last quasi-isomorphism follows from (
5.1
) (2).

As in
[
B1
, V Proposition 3.3.1]
, by the transitive condition,
we have only to prove
that (
5.2
) holds
for a morphism

f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T},{\cal B})
of ringed topoi such that

f={\rm id}_{\cal T}
as
a morphism of topoi.
As in the trivial filtered case,
consider the following
functor
f^{!}
:

\displaystyle f^{!}\colon{\rm MF}({\cal B})\owns(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longmapsto

\displaystyle{\cal H}{\it om}_{\cal B}(f_{*}({\cal A}),(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

\displaystyle={\cal H}{\it om}_{\cal B}({\cal A},(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\in{\rm MF}({\cal A}).

Here we endow
f_{*}({\cal A})(={\cal A})

with the trivial filtration.
The functor
f^{!}
is the right adjoint functor of
f_{*}
:

(5.2.2)

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\cal A}((M,\{M^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),f^{!}((K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})))={\rm Hom}_{\cal B}(f_{*}((M,

\displaystyle\{M^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})),(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

\displaystyle(M,\{M^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm MF}({\cal A})).

By (
5.2.2
), we see that, if
(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm MF}({\cal B})
is
a strictly injective
{\cal B}
-module,
then
f^{!}((K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))
is a strictly injective
{\cal A}
-module.
Moreover, for a strictly injective morphism

f_{*}((M,\{M^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
of
{\cal B}
-modules,
the corresponding morphism

(M,\{M^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}f^{!}((K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is
a strictly injective morphism of

{\cal A}
-modules, which is easily checked.
Hence, by the same proof as that of
(
3.9
)
(especially, by noting that the functor
f^{!}

commutes with the direct product), we can take

f^{!}((K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))
as
(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
,
where
(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is a bounded below
complex of strictly injective
{\cal B}
-modules.

Let
R^{\bullet}
be a flat
resolution of
f_{*}({\cal A})

with the trivial
filtration. Since the filtration
on
R^{\bullet}
is trivial, it is obvious
that the morphism

(Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}){\otimes}_{\cal B}R^{\bullet}{\longrightarrow}\allowbreak(Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}){\otimes}_{\cal B}f_{*}({\cal A})

is a filtered quasi-isomorphism
(q\in{\mathbb{Z}})
.
By (
5.1
) (2) we have the following isomorphism

(5.2.3)

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal B}((Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}){\otimes}_{\cal B}f_{*}({\cal A}),(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal B}((Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}){\otimes}_{\cal B}R^{\bullet},(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})).

(
5.2.3
) is equal to the following:

(5.2.4)

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal B}((Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),{\cal H}{\it om}_{\cal B}(f_{*}({\cal A}),(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})))

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal B}((Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),{\cal H}{\it om}_{\cal B}^{\bullet}(R^{\bullet},(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))).

Here

{\cal H}{\it om}_{\cal B}(f_{*}({\cal A}),(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))
is
considered as a filtered
{\cal B}
-module, which is nothing but

f_{*}f^{!}(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
It is easy to check that

{\cal H}{\it om}_{\cal B}(R^{q},(K^{q+n},\{K^{q+n(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))
is a strictly
injective
{\cal B}
-module; so
is
{\cal H}{\it om}_{\cal B}^{n}(R^{\bullet},(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

(n\in{\mathbb{Z}})
. Therefore

{\cal H}{\it om}_{\cal B}^{\bullet}(R^{\bullet},(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

is a strictly injective resolution of
f_{*}f^{!}(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

by the sheafification of (
5.1
) (2).
Hence we can take

{\cal H}{\it om}_{\cal B}^{\bullet}(R^{\bullet},(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))
as

(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
, and
we have a filtered quasi-isomorphism

(5.2.5)

{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal B}((Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),f_{*}f^{!}(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal B}((Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

by (
5.2.4
).

Let
(C^{\bullet},\{C^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be
the mapping cone of the morphism

f_{*}f^{!}(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Then we have a triangle

{\rm Hom}_{\cal B}^{\bullet}((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),f_{*}f^{!}(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\cal B}^{\bullet}((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

{\longrightarrow}{\rm Hom}_{\cal B}^{\bullet}((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(C^{\bullet},\{C^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}\cdots.

By (
5.2.5
) the filtered complex

{\rm Hom}_{\cal B}^{\bullet}((Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(C^{\bullet},\{C^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))
is strictly exact.
As in
[
B1
, p. 327]
, by noting that

(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is
bounded above, one can easily check that

{\rm Hom}_{\cal B}^{\bullet}((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(C^{\bullet},\{C^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

is also strictly exact.
Therefore we obtain

{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal B}((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),f_{*}f^{!}(K^{\bullet},\{K^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm Hom}^{\bullet}_{\cal B}((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})),

which enables us to
finish the proof of (
5.2
).
∎

Let
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

(resp.
(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
) be
an object of
{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})

(resp.
{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
).
Set

{\rm Ext}^{q}_{{\cal A}}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})):={\rm Hom}_{{\rm DF}^{n}({\cal A})}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})[q]).

The following lemma is a

n
-filtered version of a classical lemma

[
H
, I (6.4)]
.

Lemma 5.3
.

The following formula holds
:

(5.3.1)

H^{q}(\bigcap^{(1,n)}_{0,0}{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})))=

{\rm Ext}^{q}_{{\cal A}}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})).

In particular,

(5.3.2)

H^{0}(\bigcap^{(1,n)}_{0,0}{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}_{\cal A}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})))=

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm DF}^{n}({\cal A})}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})).

Proof.

By using (
3.12
) (1), (2) and (
5.0.1
),
the proof is the same as that of
[
NS
, (1.2.3)]
.
∎

6
\otimes^{L}_{\cal A}

In this section we define the
n
-filtered derived tensor product
\otimes^{L}_{\cal A}
of
two bounded below complexes with
n
-pieces of filtrations.
As in the previous section, we assume that
n\leq 2
.
The results in this section are generalizations of results in
[
NS
, (1.2)]
.
The construction of
\otimes^{L}_{\cal A}
is more nontrivial than the construction of

{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}

in the previous section because the dual notion (
4.3
) (1) does not work
in this section.

The following (2) is a key lemma for the definition
\otimes^{L}_{\cal A}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
1\leq i_{1}<i_{m}\leq n

(1\leq m\leq n)
and
k_{1},k_{m}
be
integers. Then the following hold
:

(1)

Let
(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be an
{\cal A}
-module with
n
-pieces of filtrations.
Then

(6.1.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}E/{\rm Fil}^{(i_{1})}_{q_{1}}({\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}E)=E^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}/(E^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{q_{1}q_{m}}+E^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}-1}).

(2)

Let
(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

and
(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be
{\cal A}
-modules
with
n
-pieces of filtrations.
Assume that
(F,\{F^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal Q}_{\rm fl}({\cal A})

((\ref{eqn:qfla}))
.
Then the natural morphism

(6.1.2)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{p_{1}+q_{1}=k_{1},p_{m}+q_{m}=k_{m}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{p_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{p_{m}}E{\otimes}_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{q_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}F{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{k_{m}}(E\otimes_{\cal A}F)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

(1): We omit the proof.

(2):
We proceed on induction on
m
.
When
m=1
, (2) is nothing but
[
NS
, (1.2.4)]
.
Hence we have only to prove (2) for the case
m=n=2
.
Because
F/{\rm Fil}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}F
is a flat
{\cal A}
-module,
we have the following isomorphism:

(6.1.3)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{p_{m}+q_{m}=k_{m}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{p_{m}}E{\otimes}_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}F\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{k_{m}}(E\otimes_{\cal A}F)

by
[
NS
, (1.2.4)]
.
Hence we have the following isomorphism

(6.1.4)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{p_{m}+q_{m}=k_{m}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}({\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{p_{m}}E{\otimes}_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}F)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{k_{m}}(E\otimes_{\cal A}F).

Because

{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}F
and

{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}F/{\rm Fil}^{(i_{1})}_{q_{1}}({\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}F)=E^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}/(E^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{q_{1}q_{m}}+E^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}-1})

are flat
{\cal A}
-modules
by the definition of
{\cal Q}_{\rm fl}({\cal A})
,
we have the following isomorphism by
[
NS
, (1.2.4)]
:

(6.1.5)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}({\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{p_{m}}E{\otimes}_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}F)=\bigoplus_{p_{1}+q_{1}=k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{p_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{p_{m}}E{\otimes}_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{q_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}F.

(This is the point of this proof.)
By (
6.1.4
) and (
6.1.5
) we have the following isomorphism:

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{p_{1}+q_{1}=k_{1},p_{m}+q_{m}=k_{m}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{p_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{p_{m}}E{\otimes}_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{q_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{q_{m}}F\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{k_{m}}(E\otimes_{\cal A}F).

∎

Let
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
and

(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be objects of
{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})
.
Set

(6.1.6)

(E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet})_{k}^{r(i)}:={\rm Im}(\bigoplus_{l+m=k}\bigoplus_{p+q=r}E^{p(i)}_{l}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q(i)}_{m}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{p+q=r}E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q}).

Then we have a complex

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\otimes_{\cal A}(F^{\bullet},\{F^{(i)\bullet}\}_{i=1}^{n}):=(E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet},\{(E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet})_{k}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

of
{\cal A}
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations,
where the boundary morphism
is defined by the following formula

(6.1.7)

d|_{E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q}}=(d^{p}_{E}\otimes 1)+(-1)^{p}(1\otimes d^{q}_{F}).

The functor

\otimes\colon{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})\times{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow{\rm CF}^{n}({\cal A})

induces a functor

\otimes\colon{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})\times{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A}).

As in
[
H
, II (4.1)]
,
[
B2
]
and
[
NS
, (1.2.5)]
,
we need the following key theorem
to define the following
n
-filtered derived functor

\otimes^{L}_{\cal A}\colon{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\times{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}).

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

and
(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be two complexes of

{\cal A}
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations.
Assume that
(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm fl}({\cal A}))
.
Assume that either

({\rm a})

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is strictly exact

or

({\rm b})

(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is strictly exact

and assume also that
either

({\rm c})

E^{\bullet}
is bounded above

or

({\rm d})

F^{\bullet}
is bounded below.

Then

(E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet},\{(E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet})^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})

is strictly exact.

Proof.

By
[
H
, II (4.1)]
,

E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet}

is exact.
By
[
NS
, (1.2.5)]

(E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet})^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}
is exact for

k_{1}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Hence we have only to prove that

(E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}

is exact for
1\leq i_{1}<i_{m}\leq n

(1\leq m\leq n)
and

k_{1},k_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Let
G^{\bullet\bullet}
be a double complex defined by

G^{pq}:=E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q}
with
n
-pieces of filtrations

G^{pq(i)}_{k}:={\rm Im}(\underset{l+m=k}{\bigoplus}E^{p(i)}_{l}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q(i)}_{m}\longrightarrow G^{pq})

(1\leq i\leq n)
. Set

G^{pq(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}:=G^{pq(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}\cap G^{pq(i_{m})}_{k_{m}}
.
Then we have the following two spectral sequences

E_{2}^{pq}={\cal H}^{p}_{\rm II}{\cal H}^{q}_{\rm I}(G^{\bullet\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}})\Longrightarrow{\cal H}^{p+q}((E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}),

E_{2}^{pq}={\cal H}^{p}_{\rm I}{\cal H}^{q}_{\rm II}(G^{\bullet\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}})\Longrightarrow{\cal H}^{p+q}((E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}).

The assumption (c) or (d) implies
that the two spectral sequences above are
bounded and regular.

First, assume that (a) holds.
Set

E_{\infty}^{p(i)}:=\bigcup_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}E_{k}^{p(i)}

and
F_{\infty}^{q(i)}:=\bigcup_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}F_{k}^{q(i)}

(p,q\in{\mathbb{Z}})
.
Since
F^{q(i)}_{k}

(\forall k\in{\mathbb{Z}})
is a flat
{\cal A}
-module,
F_{\infty}^{q(i)}
is also so.
Because the complex
E^{\bullet(i)}_{\infty}
is exact by (a),

E^{\bullet(i)}_{\infty}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q(i)}_{\infty}
is also so.
We prove that
(E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}

for any
1\leq i_{1}<i_{m}\leq n

(1\leq m\leq n)

and
k_{1},k_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
is exact.
We have only to prove that

(6.2.1)

{\rm Im}((E^{p-1}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}\!\rightarrow\!(E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}})\supset{\rm Ker}((E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}\!\rightarrow\!(E^{p+1}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}).

Set

E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}:=\bigcap_{j=1}^{m}\bigcup_{k_{j}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}E^{\bullet(i_{j})}_{k_{j}}=\bigcup_{k_{1},k_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}

and

F^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}:=\bigcap_{j=1}^{m}\bigcup_{k_{j}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}F^{\bullet(i_{j})}_{k_{j}}=\bigcup_{k_{1},k_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}}F^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}.

Because

(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm fl}({\cal A}))
,

F^{q}/F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}
and
F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}
are flat
{\cal A}
-modules,

F^{q}/F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}
and

F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}
are also flat
{\cal A}
-modules.
Consequently the following natural composite morphism

E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}\longrightarrow E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}\longrightarrow E^{\bullet}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q}

is injective.
Because the lower horizontal sequence of
the following commutative diagram

(6.2.2)

\begin{CD}(E^{p-1}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}@>{}>{}>(E^{p}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}@>{}>{}>(E^{p+1}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
E^{p-1(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}@>{}>{}>E^{p(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}@>{}>{}>E^{p+1(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}\end{CD}

is exact by the assumption (a), we may assume that

E^{\bullet}=E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}
and

F^{q}=F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}
.
Set
B^{p(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\circ}:={\rm Im}(E^{p-1(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\circ}\longrightarrow E^{p(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\circ})

(\circ=k_{1}k_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{m}
or nothing).
Then the sequence

0\longrightarrow B^{p-1(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\circ}\longrightarrow E^{p(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\circ}\longrightarrow B^{p+1(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\circ}\longrightarrow 0

is exact by the assumption (a).
Moreover we have the following commutative diagram
with lower exact row:

(6.2.3)

\begin{CD}(B^{p}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}@>{}>{}>(E^{p}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}@>{}>{}>(B^{p+1}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}\\
@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{\bigcap}V{}V@V{\bigcap}V{}V\\
0@>{}>{}>\!\!B^{p}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q}\!@>{}>{}>\!E^{p}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q}\!@>{}>{}>\!B^{p+1}{\otimes}_{\cal A}F^{q}\!\!@>{}>{}>0.\end{CD}

We claim that, to prove (
6.2.1
),
it suffices to prove that the following sequence

(6.2.4)

0\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{k_{m}}(B^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{k_{m}}(E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{k_{m}}(B^{p+1}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})

is exact. Indeed, let
s
be a local
section of the sheaf on the right hand
side of (
6.2.1
).
Then, by the lower exact sequence of (
6.2.3
)
and by the
assumptions
E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}=E^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty}

and
F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}=F^{q(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty}
,
there exist integers
k^{\prime}_{1}\geq k_{1},k^{\prime}_{m}\geq k_{m}
such that

s\in(B^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k^{\prime}_{1}k^{\prime}_{m}}
.
If
k^{\prime}_{j}=k_{j}
for any
1\leq j\leq m
, there is nothing to prove.
If there exists
k^{\prime}_{j}>k_{j}
for some
1\leq j\leq m
,
then (
6.2.4
) for
k^{\prime}_{j}
and either of
k^{\prime}_{j+1}
or
k^{\prime}_{j-1}
implies
that

s\in(B^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k^{\prime}_{1}k^{\prime}_{j-1}k^{\prime}_{j}-1k^{\prime}_{j+1}k^{\prime}_{m}}

by the injectivity of the morphism

{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{k^{\prime}_{m}}(B^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{k^{\prime}_{m}}(E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})
in
(
6.2.4
) with the replacement of
k_{1}
and
k_{m}
by
k^{\prime}_{1}
and
k^{\prime}_{m}
, respectively,
since the image of
s
in

{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{k^{\prime}_{m}}(E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})
is zero.
Repeating this process, we see that

s\in(B^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}
.
This means that

s\in{\rm Im}((E^{p-1}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}}\!\rightarrow\!(E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{q})^{(i_{1}i_{m})}_{k_{1}k_{m}})
.

Now let us prove the exactness of (
6.2.4
).

By (
6.1
) (2) we have only to
prove that the following sequence

(6.2.5)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{l_{1}+l^{\prime}_{1}=k_{1},l_{m}+l^{\prime}_{m}=k_{m}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l_{m}}B^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l^{\prime}_{m}}(F^{q})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{l_{1}+l^{\prime}_{1}=k_{1},l_{m}+l^{\prime}_{m}=k_{m}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l_{m}}E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l^{\prime}_{m}}(F^{q})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{l_{1}+l^{\prime}_{1}=k_{1},l_{m}+l^{\prime}_{m}=k_{m}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l_{m}}B^{p+1}\otimes_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l^{\prime}_{m}}(F^{q})

is exact.
Because
(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal Q}^{n}_{\rm fl}({\cal A}))
,

{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l^{\prime}_{m}}(F^{q})
is a
flat
{\cal A}
-module by (
2.8.1
).
Hence (
6.2.5
) is
exact by the assumption (a), (
2.8
) and (
2.10
).

Next assume that (b) holds.
Since
F_{\infty\infty}^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}
is bounded above,

E^{p}_{\infty\infty}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty}
is exact.
We prove that
(E^{p}_{\infty\infty}\otimes_{\cal A}F^{\bullet(i_{1}i_{m})}_{\infty\infty})_{k_{1}k_{m}}
is exact.
Set
K^{q}_{\circ}:={\rm Im}(F^{q-1}_{\circ}\longrightarrow F^{q}_{\circ})

(\circ=k_{1}k_{m}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{m}
or
\infty\infty
).
Then
K^{q}_{\circ}
is a flat
{\cal A}
-module
since
F^{\bullet}_{\circ}
is bounded above.
As in the case (a), we have only
to prove that the following sequence

(6.2.6)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{l_{1}+l^{\prime}_{1}=k_{1},l_{m}+l^{\prime}_{m}=k_{m}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l_{m}}E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l^{\prime}_{m}}K^{q}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{l_{1}+l^{\prime}_{1}=k_{1},l_{m}+l^{\prime}_{m}=k_{m}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l_{m}}E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l^{\prime}_{m}}F^{q}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{l_{1}+l^{\prime}_{1}=k_{1},l_{m}+l^{\prime}_{m}=k_{m}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l_{m}}E^{p}\otimes_{\cal A}{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l^{\prime}_{m}}K^{q+1}\longrightarrow 0

is exact.
By the assumption (b), (
2.8
) and (
2.10
), the following sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l^{\prime}_{m}}K^{q}\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l^{\prime}_{m}}F^{q}\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l^{\prime}_{m}}K^{q+1}\longrightarrow 0

is exact. Since
F^{\bullet}
is bounded above, we see that

{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{l^{\prime}_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{m})}_{l^{\prime}_{m}}K^{q}
is a flat
{\cal A}
-module
by descending induction on
q
.
Hence (
6.2.6
) is exact.

We finish the proof.
∎

By using (
6.2
) (b),
we have the following derived functor:

(6.2.7)

\otimes^{L}_{\cal A}\colon{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\times{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}).

By (
6.2
) (a)
the functor above induces the following derived functor
(cf.
[
H
, II §4]
)

(6.2.8)

\otimes^{L}_{\cal A}\colon{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\times{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}).

Remark 6.3
.

The derived tensor product
\otimes^{L}_{\cal A}
for the case
n=1

(resp.
n=2
) has
a key role for the construction of the
l
-adic weight spectral sequence
(resp. the construction of a fundamental bifiltered complex) of
a proper SNCL scheme
over a family of log points with a relative horizontal SNCD
in the second part of this paper.

7
Complements

This section is a complement of §
3
and §
4
.
Let the notations be as in §
2
.

Assume that
n\leq 2
.
Let
f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})

be a morphism of ringed topoi.
As in
[
NS
, §3]
we consider the following larger
full subcategory category
{\cal I}^{n}_{f_{*}{\textrm{-}}{\rm acyc}}({\cal A})
than

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A})
in

{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
(cf.
[
D2
, (1.4.5)]
):

\displaystyle{\cal I}^{n}_{f_{*}{\textrm{-}}{\rm acyc}}({\cal A}):=\{(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})~|~

\displaystyle J~\text{and}~J^{(i_{1}i_{n})}_{k_{1}k_{n}}~\text{are}~f_{*}\text{-acyclic}~\text{for}~1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{n}\leq n

\displaystyle~\text{and}~k_{1},k_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}

Then the following holds by
(
3.9
):

Proposition 7.1
.

The canonical morphism

{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n}_{f_{*}{\textrm{-}}{\rm acyc}}({\cal A}))\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})
induces an equivalence

{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n}_{f_{*}{\textrm{-}}{\rm acyc}}({\cal A}))_{{(\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

of categories
and the right derived functor

Rf_{*}
is calculated by the following formula

Rf_{*}([(J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})])=[f_{*}((J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))]

((J^{\bullet},\{J^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n}_{f_{*}{\textrm{-}}{\rm acyc}}({\cal A})))
.

We can consider the dual notion of the above as follows.
Set

\displaystyle{\cal Q}^{n}_{f^{*}{\textrm{-}}{\rm acyc}}({\cal A}):=\{(Q,\{Q^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})~|~

\displaystyle Q\text{ and }Q/\sum_{j=1}^{N}Q^{(i^{j}_{1}i^{j}_{n})}_{k^{j}_{1}k^{j}_{n}}\text{ are }f^{*}{\textrm{-}}\text{ acyclic}

\displaystyle~(N\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1},1\leq\forall i^{j}_{1}\leq\forall i^{j}_{n}\leq n,\forall k^{j}_{1},\forall k^{j}_{n}\in{\mathbb{Z}})\}.

Then the following holds by (
4.8
):

Proposition 7.2
.

The canonical morphism

{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal Q}^{n}_{f^{*}{\textrm{-}}{\rm acyc}}({\cal A}^{\prime}))\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})
induces an equivalence

{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal Q}^{n}_{f^{*}{\textrm{-}}{\rm acyc}}({\cal A}^{\prime}))_{({\rm F}^{n}{\rm Qis})}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})

of categories
and the left derived functor

Lf^{*}
is calculated by the following formula

Lf^{*}[(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})]=[f^{*}((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))]

((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm K}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal Q}^{n}_{f^{*}{\textrm{-}}{\rm acyc}}({\cal A}^{\prime})))
.

Next we define the
gr
-
functor
.
For a sequence
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{n})
of integers
and for
(i_{1},i_{n})

(1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{n}\leq n)
,
there exists the following functor

(7.2.1)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}\colon{\rm K}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\owns(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longmapsto

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}E^{\bullet}\in{\rm K}^{\star}({\cal A})

\displaystyle(\star=+,-,{\rm b},~{\rm nothing}),

which we call the
gr
-
functor
.

Lemma 7.3
.

If
f\colon(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(F^{\bullet},\{F^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is
an
n
-filtered quasi-isomorphism in
{\rm KF}^{n}({\cal A})
, then

{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}(f)\colon{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}E^{\bullet}\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}F^{\bullet}

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

Using (
2.8.1
) and (
2.8.2
), one obtains
(
7.3
).
∎

By (
7.3
) the gr-functor (
7.2.1
) induces
a functor

(7.3.1)

{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}\colon{\rm D}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{\star}({\cal A}).

We also call this functor the
gr
-
functor
.

Lemma 7.4
.

For a morphism
f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})
of ringed topoi,
the following diagrams are commutative
:

(7.4.1)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})@>{{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}({\cal A})\\
@V{Rf_{*}}V{}V@V{}V{Rf_{*}}V\\
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})@>{{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}}>{}>{\rm D}^{+}({\cal A}^{\prime}),\end{CD}

(7.4.2)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})@>{{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}}>{}>{\rm D}^{-}({\cal A})\\
@A{Lf^{*}}A{}A@A{}A{Lf^{*}}A\\
{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})@>{{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}}>{}>{\rm D}^{-}({\cal A}^{\prime}).\end{CD}

Proof.

First we prove that the diagram (
7.4.1
) is commutative.
Let
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be
an object of
{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

and
let
(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be
an
n
-filtered flasque resolution of

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Then

{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}I^{\bullet}

is a flasque resolution of

{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}E^{\bullet}

by (
7.3
). Hence

Rf_{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}E^{\bullet})=f_{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}I^{\bullet})
.
Because

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bigcap^{(i_{1},i_{n})}_{k_{1},k_{j}-1,k_{n}}(I^{q},\{I^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
is flasque,

R^{1}f_{*}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bigcap^{(i_{1},i_{n})}_{k_{1},k_{j}-1,k_{n}}(I^{q},\{I^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))=0\quad(\forall q\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Hence the following sequence

0\longrightarrow f_{*}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bigcap^{(i_{1},i_{n})}_{k_{1},k_{j}-1,k_{n}}(I^{q},\{I^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\longrightarrow f_{*}(\bigcap^{(i_{1},i_{n})}_{k_{1},k_{n}}(I^{q},\{I^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

\longrightarrow f_{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}I^{q})\longrightarrow 0\quad(\forall q\in{\mathbb{Z}})

is exact and

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}f_{*}((I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

\displaystyle=f_{*}(\bigcap^{(i_{1},i_{n})}_{k_{1},k_{n}}(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))/f_{*}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bigcap^{(i_{1},i_{n})}_{k_{1},k_{j}-1,k_{n}}(I^{\bullet},\{I^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

\displaystyle=f_{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}I^{\bullet}).

Hence

{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}Rf_{*}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))=f_{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}I^{\bullet})=Rf_{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}E^{\bullet}).

Next we prove that the diagram (
7.4.2
) is commutative.
Let
(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be
an object of
{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})

and
let
(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
be
an
n
-filtered flat resolution of

(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Then
{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}Q^{\bullet}

is a flat resolution of
{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}E^{\bullet}

by (
7.3
). Hence

Lf^{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}E^{\bullet})=f^{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}Q^{\bullet})

and
the following sequence

0\longrightarrow f^{*}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bigcap^{(i_{1},i_{n})}_{k_{1},k_{j}-1,k_{n}}(Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))\longrightarrow f^{*}(\bigcap^{(i_{1},i_{n})}_{k_{1},k_{n}}(Q^{q},\{Q^{q(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

\longrightarrow f^{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}Q^{q})\longrightarrow 0\quad(\forall q\in{\mathbb{Z}})

is exact.
This shows that

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}f^{*}((Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

\displaystyle=f^{*}(\bigcap^{(i_{1},i_{n})}_{k_{1},k_{n}}(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))/f^{*}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bigcap^{(i_{1},i_{n})}_{k_{1},k_{j}-1,k_{n}}(Q^{\bullet},\{Q^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))

\displaystyle=f^{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}Q^{\bullet}).

Hence

{\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}Lf^{*}((E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}))=f^{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}Q^{\bullet})=Lf^{*}({\rm gr}^{(i_{1})}_{k_{1}}{\rm gr}^{(i_{n})}_{k_{n}}E^{\bullet}).

∎

Lastly we give the definition of
the
taking the filtration functor

\pi^{(i)}_{k}

and
the
forgetting filtration functor

\pi
.

Let
k
be an integer.
The following morphisms

(7.4.3)

\pi^{(i)}_{k}\colon{\rm K}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\owns(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longmapsto E^{\bullet(i)}_{k}\in{\rm K}^{\star}({\cal A})\quad(\star=+,-,\text{ b, nothing})

and

(7.4.4)

\pi\colon{\rm K}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\owns(E^{\bullet},\{E^{\bullet(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longmapsto E^{\bullet}\in{\rm K}^{\star}({\cal A})\quad(\star=+,-,\text{ b, nothing})

induce morphisms

(7.4.5)

\pi^{(i)}_{k}\colon{\rm D}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow D^{\star}({\cal A})

and

(7.4.6)

\pi\colon{\rm D}^{\star}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})\longrightarrow D^{\star}({\cal A}),

respectively.
It is easy to check the following
diagrams are commutative:

(7.4.7)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})@>{\pi^{(i)}_{k}}>{}>D^{+}({\cal A})\\
@V{Rf_{*}}V{}V@V{}V{Rf_{*}}V\\
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})@>{\pi^{(i)}_{k}}>{}>D^{+}({\cal A}^{\prime}),\end{CD}

(7.4.8)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})@>{\pi}>{}>D^{+}({\cal A})\\
@V{Rf_{*}}V{}V@V{}V{Rf_{*}}V\\
{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})@>{\pi}>{}>D^{+}({\cal A}^{\prime}),\end{CD}

(7.4.9)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})@>{\pi^{(i)}_{k}}>{}>{\rm D}^{-}({\cal A})\\
@A{Lf^{*}}A{}A@A{}A{Lf^{*}}A\\
{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})@>{\pi^{(i)}_{k}}>{}>D^{-}({\cal A}^{\prime})\end{CD}

(7.4.10)

\begin{CD}{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})@>{\pi}>{}>D^{-}({\cal A})\\
@A{Lf^{*}}A{}A@A{}A{Lf^{*}}A\\
{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A}^{\prime})@>{\pi}>{}>D^{-}({\cal A}^{\prime}).\end{CD}

8
A remark on bifiltered derived categories

In this section we prove some
properties of complexes
of
{\cal A}
-modules with
n
-pieces of filtrations
by using the formulation of
the derived category of complexes of objects of quasi-abelian categories in
[
Schn
]
.
See also
[
SS
]
for the general theory of
the derived category of complexes of objects of
quasi-abelian categories.

Let the notations be as in §
2
.
Let
n\leq 2
be a positive integer.
Let
{\cal I}
be an additive full
subcategory of
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
satisfying
the following three conditions which are the dual part of

[
Schn
, Definition 1.3.2 (a), (b), (c)]
.

(8.0.1): For any object

(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
,
there exists an object

(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}
with a
strictly injective morphism

(E,\{E^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.

(8.0.2): In any strictly exact sequence

0\longrightarrow(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow 0

in
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})
, if
(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}

and
(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}
,
then
(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}
.

(8.0.3): If a sequence

0\longrightarrow(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow 0

in
{\rm MF}^{n}({\cal A})

is a strictly exact sequence with

(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n}),(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}
,
then, for any morphism

f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})
of ringed topoi,
the sequence

0\longrightarrow f_{*}(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow f_{*}(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow f_{*}(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\longrightarrow 0

is strictly exact.

Proposition 8.1
.

Let

{\cal I}
be
{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A})
,

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm inj}({\cal A})

or
{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A})
.
Then
{\cal I}
satisfies the
conditions
(8.0.1)
,
(8.0.2)
and
(8.0.3)
.

Proof.

By (
3.6
),
{\cal I}
satisfies the condition (8.0.1);

{\cal I}
also satisfies the condition (8.0.3).
It is easy to check that
the categories
{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A})
and

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm inj}({\cal A})

satisfy the condition (8.0.2).
Consider the exact sequence in (8.0.2) with

(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
,

(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A})
.
Then, by the definition of

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A})
,
there exists a splitting
of the strictly injective morphism

(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Hence
(J,\{J^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\simeq(I,\{I^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\oplus(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})
.
Now it is easy to see that

(K,\{K^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{n})\in{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A})
.
∎

Let
{\cal I}
be

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm flas}({\cal A})
,

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm inj}({\cal A})
or

{\cal I}^{n}_{\rm stinj}({\cal A})
.
Let
{\rm N}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I}^{n})
be a full
subcategory of
{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I})

which consists of the strictly exact sequences of

{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I})
.
We can prove the following as in the classical case
(
[
Schn
, (1.3.4)]
):

Corollary 8.2
.

The canonical functor

{\rm K}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I})/{\rm N}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal I})\longrightarrow{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{n}({\cal A})

is an equivalence of categories.

Part II.
l
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture

In the Part II of this paper we construct a fundamental bifiltered complex which gives us
the
l
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture.

9
l
-adic bifiltered El Zein-Steenbrink-Zucker complex

Let
S
be a family of log points defined in
[
Nakk5
, (1.1)]
.
That is,
S
is locally isomorphic to

(\overset{\circ}{S},{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S})
,
where the morphism

{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S}
is
given by
(n,a)\longmapsto 0^{n}a

(n\in{\mathbb{N}},a\in{\cal O}_{S}^{*})
, where
0^{n}=0\in{\cal O}_{S}

for
n\not=0
and
0^{0}:=1\in{\cal O}_{S}
.
In this paper we assume that
S
is isomorphic to

(\overset{\circ}{S},{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S})

and we fix an isomorphism
S\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\overset{\circ}{S},{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\cal O}_{S}^{*}\longrightarrow{\cal O}_{S})
.
We do not assume a condition on the characteristic of
S
.
This
S
is different from the
S
in the introduction.
For a monoid
P
and a commutative ring of
A
,
we denote by
{\rm Spec}^{\log}(A[P])
the log scheme whose underlying scheme
is
{\rm Spec}(A[P])
and whose log structure is the association of
the natural morphism

P\longrightarrow A[P]
. We have a natural morphism

S\longrightarrow{\rm Spec}^{\log}({\mathbb{Z}}[{\mathbb{N}}])
.
Let
l
be a prime number
which is invertible on
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Let
\mu_{l^{m}}

(m\in{\mathbb{N}})
be the group of

l^{m}
-th roots of unity in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Set
\mu_{l^{\infty}}:=\varinjlim_{m}\mu_{l^{m}}
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a scheme over

{\rm Spec}({\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-1},\zeta_{l^{\infty}}~|~\zeta_{l^{\infty}}\in\mu_{l^{\infty}}])
.
That is,
{\cal O}_{S}
is assumed to be a
{\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-1}][T_{m}]/(T^{l^{m}}_{m}-1)
-algebra
with
T_{m}\longmapsto T_{m+1}^{l}
for any
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Let
X
be an SNCL scheme with a relative SNCD
D
over
S

defined in
[
NY
, (6.1)]
.
(In
[
Nakk6
, §2, §3]
we have also recalled
the definition of an SNCL scheme with a relative SNCD.)
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is quasi-compact.
In this section we construct a bifiltered complex

(A_{l^{\infty}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S),P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)

producing the generalizations of the
l
-adic weight spectral sequences
(
1.1.3
) and (
1.1.4
).

Let
\Delta:=\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
and
\{\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in M}

be decompositions of
\overset{\circ}{X}
and
\overset{\circ}{D}

by smooth components, respectively (
[
Nakk5
, (1.1.9)]
,
[
NY
, §6]
).
As in
[
NS
, (9.13.1), (9.13.2)]
,
for a nonnegative integer
k
and a subset

\underline{\lambda}=\{\lambda_{0},\cdots,\lambda_{k}\}

(\lambda_{i}\not=\lambda_{j}~{\rm if}~i\not=j,\lambda_{i}\in\Lambda)
of
\Lambda
,
set

(9.0.1)

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}:=\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}}\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{2}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{k}}

and

(9.0.2)

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}=\underset{\#\underline{\lambda}=k+1}{\coprod}\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{\lambda}}.

For a positive integer
k
and a subset

\underline{\mu}=\{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{k}\}

(\mu_{i}\not=\mu_{j}~{\rm if}~i\not=j,\mu_{i}\in M)
of
M
,
set

(9.0.3)

\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{\mu}}:=\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu_{1}}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu_{2}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu_{k}}\quad(\mu_{i}\not=\mu_{j}~{\rm if}~i\not=j)

and

(9.0.4)

\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)}=\underset{\#\underline{\mu}=k}{\coprod}\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{\mu}}.

Set
\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(0)}:=\overset{\circ}{X}
.
Let
M(D)
be the log structure in
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et}

which is the pull-back of the log structure
obtained by
\overset{\circ}{D}
in
[
NY
, §6]
by
the natural morphism of topoi
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm zar}
.

Proposition 9.1
.

For a positive integer
k
,

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}

(
resp.
\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)})

is independent of the choice of the decomposition of

\overset{\circ}{X}

(
resp.
\overset{\circ}{D})
by smooth components of

\overset{\circ}{X}

(
resp.
\overset{\circ}{D})
.
In particular, the log scheme
D^{(k)}
whose underlying scheme
is
\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)}
and whose log structure is the pull-back of

X
is independent of the choice of the decomposition of

\overset{\circ}{D}
by smooth components of
\overset{\circ}{D}
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of
[
NS
, (2.2.14), (2.2.15)]
.
∎

As in
[
D2
, (3.1.4)]
, let us define the orientation
sheaves of the sets
\{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

and
\{\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in M}
.

Let
E
be a finite set with cardinality
k\geq 0
.
Set
\varpi_{E}:=\bigwedge^{k}{\mathbb{Z}}^{E}
if
k\geq 1

and
\varpi_{E}:={\mathbb{Z}}
if
k=0
.

Let
k
be a nonnegative integer.
Let
P
be a point of
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}
.
Let
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{0}},\ldots,\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda_{k}}

be different smooth components of
\overset{\circ}{X}
such that
