-modules.

11
Contravariant functoriality

In this section we give the generalization of the contravariant functoriality of
(weight) spectral sequences stated in the Introduction.
In fact, we show the contravariant functoriality of

(A^{\bullet}_{l^{\infty}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)
.

Let
v\colon S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S
be a morphism of families of log points.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a log scheme over

{\rm Spec}({\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-1},\zeta_{l^{\infty}}~|~\zeta_{l^{\infty}}\in\mu_{l^{\infty}}])
.
Consider
S^{\prime}
as a log scheme over

{\rm Spec}({\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-1},\zeta_{l^{\infty}}~|~\zeta_{l^{\infty}}\in\mu_{l^{\infty}}])

by the composite morphism

S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S\longrightarrow{\rm Spec}({\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-1},\zeta_{l^{\infty}}~|~\zeta_{l^{\infty}}\in\mu_{l^{\infty}}]).

Assume that the log structures of
S^{\prime}
and
S
are constant and that
the degree function
\deg v\colon\overset{\circ}{S}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
for
S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S

(
[
Nakk5
, (1.1.41)]
,
[
Nakk4
, (2.1.15)]
)
is not divisible by
l
.

Definition 11.1
.

Let
w\colon E\longrightarrow F

be a morphism of
({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}}
-module.
Let
k
be an integer.
The
D
-
twist
(:=degree twist) of
w
by
k
with respect to
v\colon S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S

w(-k)\colon{\cal E}(-k;v)\longrightarrow{\cal F}(-k;v)

is, by definition, the morphism

\deg(v)^{k}w\colon{\cal E}\longrightarrow{\cal F}
.
This definition is well-defined for the derived category

D^{+}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}})
.

Example 11.2
.

Let
F_{X}\colon X\longrightarrow X
be the absolute Frobenius endomorphism
over the absolute Frobenius endomorphism
F_{S}\colon S\longrightarrow S
.
Then the
D
-twist of
F_{S}
is the usual Tate twist.
We denote
(-k;F_{S})

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}})
simply by
(-k)
as usual.

By virtue of the adjunction formula of
{\rm RHom}^{\bullet}
of bifiltered complexes
((
5.2
)), we can obtain the following without difficulty
(though the proof is not difficult, the precise formulation is not easy):

Theorem 11.3

(
Contravariant Functoriality).

Let
v\colon S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S
be as above.
Assume that the morphism
v
fits into
the following commutative diagram

(11.3.1)

\begin{CD}S^{\prime}@>{v}>{}>S\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\rm Spec}({\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-1},\zeta_{l^{\infty}}])@>{}>{}>{\rm Spec}({\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-1},\zeta_{l^{\infty}}]),\end{CD}

where the lower horizontal morphism is induced by an endomorphism
\sigma

of the commutative ring
{\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-1},\zeta_{l^{\infty}}]
such
that
\sigma(\zeta_{l^{\infty}})=\zeta_{l^{\infty}}^{\deg v}

(\zeta_{l^{\infty}}\in\mu_{l^{\infty}})
.
For a commutative diagram

(11.3.2)

\begin{CD}(Y,E)@>{g}>{}>(X,D)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S^{\prime}@>{v}>{}>S\end{CD}

over

\begin{CD}(\overset{\circ}{Y},\overset{\circ}{E})@>{\overset{\circ}{g}}>{}>(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{D})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}@>{v}>{}>\overset{\circ}{S},\end{CD}

where
(Y,E)\longrightarrow(X,D)

is a morphism of SNCL schemes with relative SNCD’s over

v\colon S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S
,
there exists a morphism

(11.3.3)

g^{*}\colon(A^{\bullet}_{l^{\infty}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)\longrightarrow R\overset{\circ}{g}_{*}((A^{\bullet}_{l^{\infty}}((Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S^{\prime}),P^{E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P))

of bifiltered complexes in

{\rm D}^{+}{\rm F}^{2}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})

fitting into the following commutative diagram

(11.3.4)

\begin{CD}A^{\bullet}_{l^{\infty}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})@>{g^{*}}>{}>R\overset{\circ}{g}_{*}(A^{\bullet}_{l^{\infty}}((Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}))\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
R(\epsilon_{(X,D)}\pi_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}))})_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})@>{g^{*}}>{}>R\overset{\circ}{g}_{*}R(\epsilon_{(Y,E)}\pi_{(Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})})_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\end{CD}

in
D^{\rm b}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})
.
The morphism
g^{*}
in
(
11.3.3
)
satisfies the obvious transitive law for
the composition of
g
’s.

Proof.

(The proof is not straightforward; we have to be careful for the definition of
g^{*}
,
which has not been considered in references.)
Because we ignore the contravariant action
g^{*}
in the definition of

(A^{\bullet}_{l^{\infty}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{D},P)
in previous sections and
because we ignore the Galois action in this proof, we have to give another definition of

(A^{\bullet}_{l^{\infty}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{D},P)
.
Fix a generator
T
of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}(1)
;
T
acts on
X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}

and
Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}
naturally.
For
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
, denote

{\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1)(j+1)/\tau_{j}{\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1)(j+1)
by

{\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1)(j+1)/\tau_{j}
and
set

(11.3.5)

A^{ij}_{l^{n}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}):=({\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1)(j+1)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+1}(j+1;v)\quad(i\in{\mathbb{Z}},j\in{\mathbb{N}})

and

(11.3.6)

\displaystyle\{P_{k}A^{ij}_{l^{n}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle:=\{((\tau_{2j+k+1}+\tau_{j}){\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1)(j+1)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+1}(j+1;v)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}.

(Note that both
(j+1)
and
(j+1;v)
are necessary.)
Consider the same boundary morphisms as those in (
9.10.2
).
Set
(A^{\bullet}_{l^{\infty}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P):=s((A^{\bullet\bullet}_{l^{\infty}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P))
.
Let
I^{\bullet}_{X,l^{n}}
(resp.
I^{\bullet}_{Y,l^{n}}
)
be an injective resolution
of
{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}
in
X_{\rm ket}
(resp.
Y_{\rm ket}
).
Then we have a natural morphism

(11.3.7)

g^{*}\colon I^{\bullet}_{X,l^{n}}\longrightarrow g_{*}(I^{\bullet}_{Y,l^{n}})

of complexes.
Let
{\rm MF}^{\prime}_{l^{n}}(T-1)

be the mapping fiber of the morphism

T-1\colon I^{\bullet}_{Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},n}\longrightarrow I^{\bullet}_{Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},n}
.
The morphism (
11.3.7
) induces a filtered morphism

g^{\bullet*}\colon\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}(({\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1),\tau))\longrightarrow({\rm MF}^{\prime}_{l^{n}}(T-1),\tau).

Because the pull-back
v^{*}(T)
of the action
T
on
S_{l^{\infty}}
by

v^{*}
is equal to
\deg(v)T
on
S^{\prime}_{l^{\infty}}
,
we define the action
g^{*}
on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}(1)
as
\deg(v)T
.
For
i,j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
set

\displaystyle g^{*ij}:=(\deg v)^{-(j+1)}g^{*i+j+1}\colon

\displaystyle\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}({\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1)(j+1)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+1}(j+1;v)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow({\rm MF}^{\prime}_{l^{n}}(T-1)(j+1)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+1}(j+1;v).

Then we obtain the following commutative diagram:

(11.3.8)

\begin{CD}(\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}({\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1))(j+2)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+2}(j+2;v)@>{g^{*i,j+1}}>{}>({\rm MF}^{\prime}_{l^{n}}(T-1)(j+2)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+2}(j+2;v)\\
@A{g^{*}(\theta)}A{}A@A{}A{\theta}A\\
(\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}({\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1))(j+1)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+1}(j+1;v)@>{g^{*ij}}>{}>({\rm MF}^{\prime}_{l^{n}}(T-1)(j+1)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+1}(j+1;v).\end{CD}

It is easy to check that the following diagram

\begin{CD}S^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{n}}}@>{T}>{}>S^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{n}}}\\
@V{v}V{}V@V{}V{v}V\\
S_{\frac{1}{l^{n}}}@>{T}>{}>S_{\frac{1}{l^{n}}}\end{CD}

is commutative by the action (
9.1.5
) and
the commutative diagram (
11.3.1
).
Hence the following diagram is commutative:

(11.3.9)

\begin{CD}(\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}({\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1))(j+1)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+1}(j+1;v)@>{-d}>{}>(\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}({\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1))(j+1)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+2}(j+1;v)\\
@V{g^{*ij}}V{}V@V{}V{g^{*i+1,j}}V\\
({\rm MF}^{\prime}_{l^{n}}(T-1)(j+1)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+1}(j+1;v)@>{-d}>{}>({\rm MF}^{\prime}_{l^{n}}(T-1)(j+1)/\tau_{j})^{i+j+2}(j+1;v).\end{CD}

Taking a filtered injective resolution of

({\rm MF}^{\prime}_{l^{n}}(T-1),\tau)
(
[
NS
, (1.1.5)]
) and using
(
11.3.8
), (
11.3.9
) and
(
11.3.6
),
we have a filtered morphism

(11.3.10)

\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}((A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P))\longrightarrow(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P).

Let
I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{D},l^{n}}
(resp.
I^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{E},n}
)
be an injective resolution of
{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}

in
(\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{D})_{\rm ket}

(resp.
(\overset{\circ}{Y},\overset{\circ}{E})_{\rm ket}
).
Then we have a morphism

(11.3.11)

g^{*}(I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{D},l^{n}})\longrightarrow(I^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{E},n})

of complexes.
By (
9.2
)
(see also
[
NS
, (2.16), (2.18), (2.20)]
),
there exist bounded filtered flat resolutions of

(M_{l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S}),Q)

and

(M^{\prime}_{l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{E}/\overset{\circ}{S^{\prime}}),Q^{\prime})

of

(I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{D},l^{n}},\tau)
and

(I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{E},n},\tau)
, respectively,
fitting into the following commutative diagram:

(11.3.12)

\begin{CD}g^{*}(\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{D}*}(I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{D},l^{n}}),\tau)@>{}>{}>(\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{E}*}(I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{E},n}),\tau)\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
g^{*}((M_{l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S}),Q))@>{}>{}>(M_{l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{E}/\overset{\circ}{S^{\prime}}),Q^{\prime}).\end{CD}

The morphism (
11.3.10
) and
the upper horizontal morphism in (
11.3.12
)
induce filtered morphisms

\displaystyle L\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}((A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P)\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{D}*}(I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{D},l^{n}}),\tau)

\displaystyle=L\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P)\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}L\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}(\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{D}*}(I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{D},l^{n}}),\tau)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P)\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{E}*}(I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{E},n}),\tau)

and

\displaystyle L\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{D}*}(I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{D},l^{n}}),\tau))

\displaystyle=L\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}L\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}(\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{D}*}(I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{D},l^{n}}),\tau)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{E}*}(I^{\bullet}_{\overset{\circ}{E},n}),\tau).

In fact, these are underlying morphisms of the following morphism

L\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{*}((A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P))\longrightarrow(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(((Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P).

By the adjunction formula (
5.2
) and (
5.3.2
),
we obtain the following morphism

(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)\longrightarrow R\overset{\circ}{g}_{*}((A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P))

of bifiltered complexes.
∎

Let us recall the following:

Proposition 11.4

(
[
Nakk3
, (4.3)]
)
.

Let
g\colon Y\longrightarrow Y^{\prime}
be a morphism of
fs log schemes. Set
Z:=Y
or
Y^{\prime}
.
Assume that
M_{Z,z}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Z,z}\simeq{\mathbb{N}}^{r}

for any point
z
of
\overset{\circ}{Z}

and for some
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
depending on
z
.
Let
b^{(k)}_{Z}\colon\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)}(M_{Z})\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{Z}

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}})
be the morphism of schemes defined in

[
Nakk3
, p. 34]
.
Assume that, for each point
y\in\overset{\circ}{Y}

and for each member
m
of the minimal generators
of
M_{Y,y}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Y,y}
, there exists
a unique member
m^{\prime}
of the minimal generators of

M_{Y^{\prime},\overset{\circ}{g}(y)}/{\cal O}^{*}_{Y^{\prime},\overset{\circ}{g}(y)}

such that
g^{*}(m^{\prime})\in m^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}}
.
Then there exists a canonical morphism

\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)}(M_{Y})\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)}(M_{Y^{\prime}})
fitting
into the following commutative diagram of schemes
:

\begin{CD}\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)}(M_{Y})@>{\overset{\circ}{g}{}^{(k)}}>{}>\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)}(M_{Y^{\prime}})\\
@V{b^{(k)}_{Y}}V{}V@V{}V{b^{(k)}_{Y^{\prime}}}V\\
\overset{\circ}{Y}@>{\overset{\circ}{g}}>{}>\overset{\circ}{Y}{}^{\prime}.\end{CD}

Let

(11.4.1)

\begin{CD}Y@>{g}>{}>X\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
S^{\prime}@>{v}>{}>S\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of SNCL schemes such that
g
satisfies the assumption in
(
11.4
). In this case,
\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)}(M_{X})=\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}
and

\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)}(M_{Y})=\overset{\circ}{Y}{}^{(k)}

and the second assumption in (
11.4
) is equivalent to
the following:
For each smooth component
\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
of
\overset{\circ}{Y}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}
,
there exists a smooth component
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}
of
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}

such that

g(\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\lambda^{\prime}})\subset\overset{\circ}{X}_{\lambda}
(cf.
[
SaT
, (2.3)]
).
Let
\Lambda^{\prime}
and
\Lambda
be the sets of indices
of the
\lambda^{\prime}
’s and the
\lambda
’s, respectively.
Then we obtain a function

\phi\colon\Lambda^{\prime}\owns\lambda^{\prime}\longmapsto\lambda\in\Lambda
.
There exist positive integers
e({\lambda}^{\prime})
’s

(\lambda^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime})
such that
there exist local equations
x_{\lambda^{\prime}}=0
and

x_{\phi(\lambda^{\prime})}=0
of

\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\lambda}
and
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\phi(\lambda)}
,
respectively, such that
g^{*}(x_{\phi(\lambda^{\prime})})=x^{e({\lambda^{\prime}})}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
.
In
[
Nakk5
, (1.5.7)]
we have proved the following (the proof is easy):

Proposition 11.5
.

Let the assumptions and the notations be as above.
Set
\Lambda^{\prime}(y):=\{\lambda^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}~|~y\in\overset{\circ}{Y}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\}
.
Then
\deg(v)_{y}=e({\lambda}^{\prime})
for
\lambda^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}(y)
.
In particular,

e({\lambda}^{\prime})
’s are independent of
the choice of an element of
\Lambda^{\prime}(y)
.

The following new definition is a generalization of the usual Tate twist and
the
l
-adic analogue of the
D
-twist(degree-twist) defined in
[
Nakk5
, (1.5.10)]

and
[
Nakk4
, (5.1.5)]
:

Let
E
and
D
be horizontal SNCD’s on
Y/S^{\prime}
and
X/S
, respectively.
Assume that,
for each smooth component
\overset{\circ}{E}_{\mu^{\prime}}
of
\overset{\circ}{E}/\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}
,
there exists a smooth component
\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu}
of
\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S}

such that

\overset{\circ}{g}(\overset{\circ}{E}_{\mu^{\prime}})\subset\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu}
.
Let
M^{\prime}
and
M
be the sets of indices
of the
\mu^{\prime}
’s and the
\mu
’s, respectively.
Then we obtain a function

\psi\colon M^{\prime}\owns\mu^{\prime}\longmapsto\mu\in M
.
There exist positive integers
e({\mu}^{\prime})
’s

(\mu^{\prime}\in M^{\prime})
such that
there exist local equations
y_{\mu^{\prime}}=0
and

y^{\prime}_{\psi(\mu^{\prime})}=0
of

\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu}
and
\overset{\circ}{E}_{\psi(\mu)}
,
respectively, such that
g^{*}(y^{\prime}_{\psi(\mu)})=y^{e({\mu})}_{\mu}
.

Definition 11.6
.

(1) We call

\{e(\mu)\}_{\mu\in M}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{M}_{>0}
the
multi-degree
of

g
with respect to a decomposition

\Delta:=\{D_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in M}

and
\Delta^{\prime}:=\{E_{\mu^{\prime}}\}_{\mu^{\prime}\in\psi(M)}
of
D
and
E
, respectively.
We denote it by
{\rm deg}_{\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}}(g)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{M}_{>0}
.

(2)
Let
u\colon{\cal E}=\bigoplus_{\{\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k}\}}E_{\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k}}\longrightarrow{\cal F}=\bigoplus_{\{\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k}\}}F_{\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k}}

be a morphism of

({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k)}}
-modules, where

E_{\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k}}
and
F_{\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k}}
are

({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{D}_{\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k}}}
-modules.
Let
k
be a nonnegative integer.
The
k
-
twist

u(-k)\colon{\cal E}(-k;g;\Delta,\Delta^{\prime})\longrightarrow{\cal F}(-k;g;\Delta,\Delta^{\prime})

of
u
with respect to
v
,
\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime}

is, by definition, the direct sum of the morphisms

e_{\mu_{1}}\cdots e_{\mu_{k}}u\colon\allowbreak{\cal E}_{\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k}}\allowbreak\longrightarrow{\cal F}_{\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{k}}
’s.

As a corollary of (
11.3
) we obtain the following:

Corollary 11.7
.

The following hold
:

(1)

There exists the following spectral sequence
:

(11.7.1)

E_{1}^{-k,q+k}=R^{q-k}f_{D^{(k)}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\pi^{*}_{D^{(k)}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}\epsilon^{*}_{D^{(k)}}(\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})))(-k;g,\Delta,\Delta^{\prime})\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}).

(2)

There exists the following spectral sequence
:

(11.7.2)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus^{k}_{k^{\prime}=-\infty}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k^{\prime},0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime}),(k-k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}((\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{D})/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k^{\prime};v)(-(k-k^{\prime});g,\Delta,\Delta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}).

(3)

Then there exists the following spectral sequence for
k\in{\mathbb{N}}:

(11.7.3)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k,0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k^{\prime})}/\overset{\circ}{S}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k),(k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}((\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{D})/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k;v)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{D^{(k^{\prime})}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}).

Proof.

This corollary follows from the consideration of the action
g^{*}
on

\bigwedge^{k}(M(D)^{\rm gp}/{\cal O}_{X}^{*})
and

\bigwedge^{q}(M_{X}^{\rm gp}/{\cal O}_{X}^{*})
in
the isomorphisms (
9.2.1
) and (
9.8.3
), respectively.
∎

12
Fundamental properties of the
l
-adic weight filtrations and
the
l
-adic weight spectral sequences

In this section we prove several fundamental properties of
the
l
-adic weight filtrations and
the
l
-adic weight spectral sequences defined in the previous sections.

Let the notations be as in the previous section.
The following is the
l
-adic analogue of the bifiltered base change theorem in

[
Nakk6
, (8.2)]
:

Theorem 12.1

(
Base change theorem of
(A,P^{D},P)
).

Let
u\colon S^{\prime}\longrightarrow S
be a solid morphism of log schemes.
Set
(X^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}},D^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}}):=(X_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}})\times_{S}S^{\prime}

(m\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{\infty\})
.
Let
f^{\prime}\colon(X^{\prime},D^{\prime})\longrightarrow S^{\prime}

be the structural morphism.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}
is quasi-compact.
Assume also that
\overset{\circ}{u}{}^{*}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}_{\rm et}
is exact.
Then

(12.1.1)

\displaystyle L\overset{\circ}{u}{}^{*}R\overset{\circ}{f}_{*}((A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P))=R\overset{\circ}{f}{}^{\prime}_{*}((A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{D^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)).

Proof.

Because
R\overset{\circ}{f}_{*}((A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P))
is bounded above,
the left hand side of (
12.1.1
) is well-defined.
It is easy to see that there exists a canonical morphism from
the left hand side of (
12.1.1
) to the right hand side of (
12.1.1
)
by using the adjunction formula (
5.2
) (and (
5.3.2
))
and the contravariant functoriality
(
11.3
).
Because the filtrations
P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
and
P
are biregular,
it suffices to prove that
the canonical morphism

(12.1.2)

\displaystyle L\overset{\circ}{u}{}^{*}R\overset{\circ}{f}_{*}({\rm gr}_{k^{\prime}}^{P}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})){\longrightarrow}R\overset{\circ}{f}{}^{\prime}_{*}(({\rm gr}_{k^{\prime}}^{P}{\rm gr}_{k}^{P^{D^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})))

is an isomorphism.
This follows from (
10.3.3
) and
the smooth base change theorem
(
[
SGA 4-3
, XVI (1.2)]
) because

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}
is smooth over
\overset{\circ}{S}

because
{\cal H}^{q}(L\overset{\circ}{u}{}^{*}E^{\bullet})=\overset{\circ}{u}{}^{*}{\cal H}^{q}(E^{\bullet})
for a complex of

({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{S}}
-modules since
\overset{\circ}{u}{}^{*}
is exact.
∎

The following is a generalization of
[
Nak3
, (1.5)]
:

Proposition 12.2
.

Let the notations be as in
(
12.1
)
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{\prime}
is quasi-compact.

(
We do not assume also that
\overset{\circ}{u}{}^{*}
is exact.
)

Let
f_{(X^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}},D^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}})/S^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}\colon(X^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{m}}}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}{}^{\prime}

be the structural morphism.
Then the spectral sequences
(
11.7.1
)

and
(
11.7.2
)
for

R^{q}f_{(X^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})

are canonically isomorphic to the inverse images of

(
11.7.1
)
and
(
11.7.2
)
by
\overset{\circ}{u}
,
respectively.

Proof.

As in (
12.1
) we have morphisms
from the inverse images of
(
11.7.1
) and (
11.7.2
) by
\overset{\circ}{u}

to the spectral sequences (
11.7.1
)
and (
11.7.2
) for

R^{q}f_{(X^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})
, respectively.
As for (
11.7.2
), the smooth base change theorem
(
[
SGA 4-3
, XVI (1.2)]
) does the job because

\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}
is smooth over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
As for (
10.4.1
), we have just proved that

g^{*}R^{q-k}f_{D^{(k)}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S*_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})=R^{q-k}f_{D^{\prime}{}^{(k)}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}^{\prime}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})
.
Now the claim for (
11.7.1
) follows.
∎

The following is a generalization of the main result of
[
Nak3
]
:

Proposition 12.3
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is proper over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Then the spectral sequence
(
10.4.2
)

degenerates at
E_{2}
modulo torsion.

Proof.

We use a standard specialization argument in
[
Nak3
, (2.3)]
.
Though we can give the analogous proof to the proof in
[
Nak3
]
,
we give a quite or slightly (?) different proof from it because our strategy of
the proof below is valid in the
p
-adic case in
[
Nakk6
]
.
We do not use the spectral sequence (
10.6.3
) of

{\rm Gal}({\kappa}_{\rm sep}/\kappa)
-modules.

It suffices to prove that
(
11.7.2
) degenerates at
E_{2}
.
By (
12.2
) we may assume that

S
is a log point

s:=({\rm Spec}(\kappa),{\mathbb{N}}\oplus\kappa^{*})
.
As in
[
Nak3
, (2.3)]
we may assume that
there exist a finitely generated subring
A
over
{\mathbb{Z}}

of
\kappa
and a proper SNCL scheme
{\cal X}
with a
relative SNCD
{\cal D}

on
{\cal X}/S^{\prime}:=({\rm Spec}(A),{\mathbb{N}}\oplus A^{*}\longrightarrow A)

such that there exists the following cartesian diagram

(12.3.1)

\begin{CD}(X,D)@>{}>{}>({\cal X},{\cal D})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
s@>{}>{}>S^{\prime}.\end{CD}

By (
12.2
)
we have only to prove that
the spectral sequence (
11.7.2
)
for
({\cal X},{\cal D})/S^{\prime}
degenerates at
E_{2}
modulo torsion.
Take a closed point
\overset{\circ}{t}={\rm Spec}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
of
\overset{\circ}{S^{\prime}}
.
Let
t
be the log point whose underlying scheme is

\overset{\circ}{t}
and whose log structure is
the pull-back of the log structure of
S^{\prime}
.
Because
E^{-k,q+k}_{1}
is smooth,
we may assume that
S^{\prime}=t
.
Set
\overline{t}:=t\otimes_{{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.
Let
F\colon\overline{t}\longrightarrow\overline{t}
be the
q
-th power Frobenius endomorphism.
Then the morphism
F
fits into
the following commutative diagram

(12.3.2)

\begin{CD}\overline{t}@>{F}>{}>\overline{t}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\rm Spec}({\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-1},\zeta_{l^{\infty}}])@>{}>{}>{\rm Spec}({\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-1},\zeta_{l^{\infty}}]),\end{CD}

where the lower horizontal morphism is induced by an endomorphism
\sigma

of the commutative ring
{\mathbb{Z}}[l^{-1},\mu_{l^{\infty}}]
such
that
\sigma(\zeta_{l^{\infty}})=\zeta_{l^{\infty}}^{q}

(\zeta_{l^{\infty}}\in\mu_{l^{\infty}})
.
Hence we obtain the spectral sequence (
11.7.2
) for

({\cal X}_{\overline{t}},{\cal D}_{\overline{t}})
, which is equal to the following
in this proof:

(12.3.3)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus^{k}_{k^{\prime}=-\infty}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k^{\prime},0\}}H^{q-2j-k}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{t}}}\cap\overset{\circ}{\cal D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{t}}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime}),(k-k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}((\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{t}}},\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{t}}})/\overset{\circ}{\overline{t}}))(-j-k)\Longrightarrow H^{q}(({\cal X}_{\overline{t},\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\cal D}_{\overline{t},\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}).

The weight filtration on

H^{q}(({\cal X}_{\overline{t},\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\cal D}_{\overline{t},\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

induced by the spectral sequence (
12.3.3
)
is the same as the weight filtration defined by the pull-back of
the Frobenius endomorphism of

({\cal X}_{\overline{t},\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\cal D}_{\overline{t},\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})
by
the purity of weight of the Frobenius endomorphism
for the
l
-adic cohomology
of a proper smooth scheme (
[
D3
, (1.6)]
).
Because the edge morphism
d^{-k,q+k}_{r}

commutes with the Frobenius endomorphism,
we see that
the spectral sequence (
12.3.3
) degenerates at
E_{2}
modulo torsion because

E_{1}^{-k,q+k}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
is of pure weight
q
.
This implies that (
10.4.2
)
degenerates at
E_{2}
modulo torsion.
∎

Set

\displaystyle P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}_{q+k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

\displaystyle:={\rm Im}(R^{q}\overset{\circ}{f}{}_{*}(P^{\overset{\circ}{D}}_{k}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}))\longrightarrow R^{q}\overset{\circ}{f}_{*}(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Im}(R^{q}\overset{\circ}{f}{}_{*}(P^{\overset{\circ}{D}}_{k}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}))

and

\displaystyle P_{q+k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

\displaystyle:={\rm Im}(R^{q}\overset{\circ}{f}_{*}(P_{k}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{*}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Im}(R^{q}\overset{\circ}{f}_{*}(P_{k}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}))\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})).

Proposition 12.4
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is proper over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Then the following hold
:

(1)

The sheaf

P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}},n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
is a smooth sheaf in
\overset{\circ}{S}_{\rm et}
.

(2)
The sheaf

P_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}},n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})
is a smooth sheaf in
\overset{\circ}{S}_{\rm et}
.

Proof.

First we prove (2).
The
E_{1}
-terms of the spectral sequence (
10.4.2
)
are smooth by
[
SGA 4 1/2
, Arcata V (3.1)]
.
Especially they are constructible.
Because the constructivility is stable under the kernel and the image
of a morphism of constructible sheaves
(
[
SGA 4-2
, IX (2.6)
({\rm i}_{\rm bis})
]
),
the
E_{r}
-terms
(r\geq 1)
are also constructible.
Because the constructivility is stable under
an extension of constructible sheaves ([loc. cit., IX (2.6) (ii)]),
the sheaf

P_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
is
also constructible.
Let
\overline{S}(\overline{x}{}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\overline{S}(\overline{x})
be the specialization map
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
([loc. cit., VIII (7.2)]).
Then we have the following specialization map

\displaystyle(\ref{eqn:lledd})_{\overline{x}}\longrightarrow(\ref{eqn:lledd})_{\overline{x}{}^{\prime}}

of spectral sequences by (
11.3
).
Since the
E_{1}
-terms of the spectral sequence (
10.4.2
)
are smooth, the specialization morphism

(E_{1}^{-k,q+k})_{\overline{x}}\longrightarrow(E_{1}^{-k,q+k})_{\overline{x}{}^{\prime}}

is an isomorphism.
Hence the specialization morphism

P_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overline{x}}\longrightarrow P_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overline{x}{}^{\prime}}

is an isomorphism and consequently

P_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

is smooth by the criterion [loc. cit., IX (2.11)].

(1): By (2) we see that the
E_{1}
-terms of the spectral sequence
(
10.4.1
) are smooth. The rest of the proof is the same as that of (2).
∎

Proposition 12.5
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is proper over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Endow the
E_{1}
-terms of
(
10.4.1
)
with the weight filtration

P
by using the equality
(
10.5.2
)
for the case where the horizontal SNCD is empty.
Endow the
E_{r}
-terms of
(
10.4.1
)

(r\geq 2)
with the induced filtration by
P
.
Then the edge morphism

(12.5.1)

d^{-k,q+k}_{r}\colon E^{-k,q+k}_{r}\longrightarrow E^{-k+1,q+k}_{r}\quad(r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1})

of the spectral sequence

(\ref{eqn:lehkd})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}

is strictly compatible with the
weight filtration
P
on the
E_{r}
-terms.

Proof.

We have already seen that
E^{-k,q+k}_{1}
is smooth on
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Hence we may assume that
S^{\prime}=t
as in the proof of (
12.3
).
The rest of the proof is the same as that of
(
12.3
).
∎

Remark 12.6
.

It is obvious that
d^{-k,q+k}_{r}
in (
12.5.1
) vanishes for
r\geq 2
if
\dim\overset{\circ}{X}=1
.
We do not know whether
d^{-k,q+k}_{r}
in (
12.5.1
) vanishes for
r\geq 2
in general.
However we prove that
d^{-k,q+k}_{r}=0
for
r\geq 2
in §
14
below
if
(X,D)
is a the log special fiber of a proper strict semistable family
with a horizontal simple normal crossing divisor
over a henselian discrete valuation ring of any characteristic.

Theorem 12.7

(
Strict compatibility).

Let the notations be as in
(
11.3
)
.
Let
q
be an integer.
Set
R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l}):=R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}

and

R^{q}f_{(Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}^{\prime}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l}):=R^{q}f_{(Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S^{\prime}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
.
Then the induced morphism

(12.7.1)

g^{*}\colon v^{*}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow R^{q}f^{\prime}_{(Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}^{\prime}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\quad(h\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})

is strictly compatible with the weight filtrations
P
’s.

Proof.

This follows from the specialization argument as in
the proof of (
12.3
).
∎

13
Log
l
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture

Let the notations be as in the previous section.
In this section we give the log
l
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture
and we prove that this is true for the case where the relative dimension of
\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}

is less than or equal to
2
.

Consider the following morphism

(13.0.1)

(T-1)\otimes{\rm id}\otimes\check{T}\colon K^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})\longrightarrow K^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})(-1).

We also have the following morphism

(13.0.2)

\nu:=(T-1)\otimes{\rm id}\otimes\check{T}\colon A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})\longrightarrow A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})(-1).

The morphism
\nu
is compatible
with the filtration
P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
.

The following holds as in
[
RZ
, (1.7)]
:

Proposition 13.1
.

The morphism
\nu
is homotopic to a morphism

A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})\longrightarrow A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})(-1)
defined by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nu}:={\rm proj}.\otimes{\rm id}\colon

\displaystyle A^{ij}_{l^{n}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})\otimes M_{l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})^{k}\longrightarrow A^{i-1,j+1}_{l^{n}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})(-1)\otimes M_{l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})^{k}

\displaystyle(i\in{\mathbb{N}},j\in{\mathbb{Z}},k\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Proof.

(Though the proof is the same as that of
[
RZ
, (1.7)]
, we give the proof because the signs of
our horizontal and vertical boundary morphisms are different from theirs.)
We omit the Tate twist in this proof.
It suffices to prove (
13.1
) for the case
D=\emptyset
.
Denote
A^{ij}_{l^{n}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S)
simply by
A^{ij}
.
Let
\sigma\colon A^{i+1,j-1}\longrightarrow A^{i,j-1}
be a morphism defined by
(x,y)\longmapsto(y,0)
, where

x\in K_{l^{n}}(X/S)^{i+j+1},y\in K_{l^{n}}(X/S)^{i+j}
.
Then
\sigma
gives us a homotopy form
\nu
to
(T-1)
.
Indeed,

\displaystyle\{(-d+\theta)\sigma+\sigma(-d+\theta)\}(x,y)

\displaystyle=(-dy,-(T-1)y)+(0,y)+\sigma(-dx,-(T-1)x+dy)+\sigma(0,x)

\displaystyle=(-dy,-(T-1)y+y)+(-(T-1)x+dy+x,0)

\displaystyle=(-(T-1)x+x,y-(T-1)y)=\{\nu-(T-1)\}(x,y).

∎

Proposition 13.2
.

(1)
The morphism

(13.2.1)

\nu\colon R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})(-1)

is nilpotent.

(2)
For
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\nu(P_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D)/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}))\subset(P_{k-2}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D)/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}))(-1)
.

Proof.

Obvious.
∎

Because
(T-1)\otimes\check{T}\colon R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})(-1)

is nilpotent, the morphism

(13.2.2)

N:=\log T\otimes\check{T}\colon R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-1)

is well-defined.

Definition 13.3
.

We call
\nu
and
N
the
l
-
adic quasi-monodromy operator
on

R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

and the
l
-
adic monodromy operator

on
R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l})
, respectively.

Because the morphism
\nu
is compatible
with the filtration
P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
, the morphisms
(
13.2.1
) and (
13.2.2
) are compatible with

P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
.

Let us recall the following definition:

Definition 13.4

(
[
D5
, (1.6.13)]
,
[
SZ
, (2.5)]
).

Let
(V,Q)
be a filtered vector space.
Let
N
be a nilpotent endomorphism of
V
.
A
monodromy filtration of

N

relative to

Q

is a filtration
M
such that
N(M_{k}V)\subset M_{k-2}V

and the induced filtration
M
on
{\rm gr}_{k}^{Q}V

is the monodromy filtration of the nilpotent endomorphism

N
on
{\rm gr}_{k}^{Q}V
.
(In
[
SZ
, (2.5)]
Steenbrink and Zucker have called the
filtration
M
the weight filtration of
N
relative to
Q
.)

Imitating the relative monodromy filtration
in characteristic
p>0
in
[
D5
, (1.8.5)]
and
the relative monodromy filtration
over the complex number field in

[
SZ
]
and
[
E
]
,
we conjecture the following which we call
the
log
l
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture
:

Conjecture 13.5

(
log
l
-adic relative monodromy-weight conjecture).

Let
q
be a nonnegative integer.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is projective over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Then the filtration
P
on

R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

is the monodromy filtration of
N
relative to

P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
, especially,
the induced morphism

(13.5.1)

N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}^{P}_{q+k+e}{\rm gr}^{P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}}_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}*({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P}_{q+k-e}{\rm gr}^{P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}}_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-e)

for
e,k\in{\mathbb{N}}

is an isomorphism.

Remark 13.6
.

It is easy to check that (
13.5
) is equivalent to
the following:
the following induced morphism

(13.6.1)

\displaystyle\nu^{e}\colon

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{q+k+e}{\rm gr}^{P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}}_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{q+k-e}{\rm gr}^{P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}}_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-e)\quad(e,k\in{\mathbb{N}})

by the morphism
\nu\colon R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-1)
is an isomorphism.

We also recall the following conjecture by K. Kato
(
[
Nak3
, (2.4) (2)]
,
[
Nakk2
, (2.0.9;
l
)]
):

Conjecture 13.7

(
log
l
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture).

Let
q
be a nonnegative integer.
Let
f_{X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}\colon X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{S}
be the structural morphism.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is projective over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
Then the filtration
P
on

R^{q}f_{X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

is the monodromy filtration of
N
, especially,
the induced morphism

(13.7.1)

N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}^{P}_{q+e}R^{q}f_{X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P}_{q-e}R^{q}f_{X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-e)\quad(e\in{\mathbb{N}})

is an isomorphism.

Remark 13.8
.

(1) In
[
Kat
]
Kato has conjectured (
13.7
) in the case where

\overset{\circ}{S}
is a point.
The proposition (
12.4
) (2) tells us that, if
Kato’s original conjecture is true, then (
13.7
) is also true.

(2) In
[
Kat
]
Kato kindly suggested to me that
the weight filtration and the monodromy filtration on
the first log
l
-adic cohomology of the
degeneration of a
p
-adic analogue of the Hopf surface
(this is a proper SNCL surface) are different.
However the proof in [loc. cit.] is not complete.
A generalization of his suggestion including the
p
-adic case
has been given in
[
Nakk2
, (6.5)]
.
The proof in
[
Nakk2
, (6.5)]
is totally different from his proof.

It is evident that (
13.5
) is a generalization of
(
13.7
). Conversely (
13.7
) implies (
13.5
):

Theorem 13.9
.

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is projective over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
If
(
13.7
)
is true,
then
(
13.5
)
is true.
Consequently
there exists a monodromy filtration

M
on
R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

relative to
P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
and the relative monodromy filtration

M
is equal to
P
.

Proof.

By (
13.2
) (2),

N(P_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l}))\subset P_{k-2}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}})
.
It suffices to prove that the morphism
(
13.5.1
) is an isomorphism.
In this proof we ignore orientation sheaves.
By (
12.2
) we may assume that
S
is the log point

({\rm Spec}({\kappa}),{\mathbb{N}}\oplus{\kappa}^{*}\longrightarrow\kappa)
.
Consider the
E_{1}
-term
\{E_{1}^{-k,q+k}\}_{k,q\in{\mathbb{Z}}}

of (
10.4.1
)
\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
.
Then, by the assumption,

N
induces an isomorphism

(13.9.1)

N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}^{P}_{q+k+e}E_{1}^{-k,q+k}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}^{P}_{q+k-e}E_{1}^{-k,q+k}(-e)

(
E_{1}^{-k,q+k}=H^{q}_{\rm ket}(D^{(k)}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}))
for any
k,q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
By (
12.5
)
the edge morphism

d^{-k,q+k}_{r}\colon E_{r}^{-k,q+k}\longrightarrow E_{r}^{-k+r,q+k+r-1}\quad(r\geq 1)

is strictly compatible with
P
.
Apply the key lemma (
13.10
) below by setting
f\colon U:=d^{-k-1,q+k}_{r}\colon E_{r}^{-k-1,q+k}\longrightarrow V:=E_{r}^{-k,q+k}
and
g:=d^{-k,q+k}_{r}\colon V:=E_{r}^{-k,q+k}\longrightarrow W:=E_{r}^{-k+1,q+k}

and consider the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}E_{r}^{-k-r,q+k+r-1}@>{d^{-k-r,q++r-1k}_{r}}>{}>E_{r}^{-k,q+k}@>{d^{-k,q+k}_{r}}>{}>E_{r}^{-k+r,q+k-r+1}\\
@V{N^{e}}V{}V@V{N^{e}}V{}V@V{N^{e}}V{}V\\
E_{r}^{-k-r,q+k+r-1}(-e)@>{d^{-k-r,q+k+r-1}_{r}}>{}>E_{r}^{-k,q+k}(-e)@>{d^{-k,q+k}_{r}}>{}>E_{r}^{-k+r,q+k-r+1}(-e).\end{CD}

(Note that,
because

N\colon(A^{\bullet}_{l^{\infty}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P)\longrightarrow(A^{\bullet}_{l^{\infty}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}},P\langle-2\rangle)(-1)

is a morphism of bifiltered complexes,

N
and
d^{-k,q+k}_{r}
’s indeed commutes in the diagram above.)
By the key lemma below,
we see that the morphism

N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}^{P}_{q+k+e}E_{r}^{-k,q+k}\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P}_{q+k-e}E_{r}^{-k,q+k}(-e)

is an isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 13.10
.

Let

\begin{CD}(U,P^{U})@>{f}>{}>(V,P^{V})@>{g}>{}>(W,P^{W})\\
@V{N}V{}V@V{N}V{}V@V{N}V{}V\\
(U,P^{U}\langle-2\rangle)@>{f}>{}>(V,P^{V}\langle-2\rangle)@>{g}>{}>(W,P^{W}\langle-2\rangle)\end{CD}

be a commutative diagram of filtered objects of
an abelian category such that
g\circ f=0
.
Set
\Lambda=U,V,W
.
Assume that
P^{U}
is finite and
that
f
and
g
are strict with respect to
P^{\Lambda}
’s.
Assume that the induced morphisms

N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}^{P^{\Lambda}}_{e}\Lambda\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}^{P^{\Lambda}}_{-e}\Lambda

are isomorphisms.
Then the
induced morphism

(13.10.1)

N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}^{P^{V}}_{e}({\rm Ker}(g)/{\rm Im}(f)){\longrightarrow}{\rm gr}^{P^{V}}_{-e}({\rm Ker}(g)/{\rm Im}(f))

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

For a finitely filtered morphism
F\colon(V,P)\longrightarrow(V^{\prime},P^{\prime})
,
F
is strict if and only if
the sequence

0\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P}{\rm Ker}(F)\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P}V\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P^{\prime}}V^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P^{\prime}}{\rm Coker}(F)\longrightarrow 0

of graded objects is exact.
Hence the morphisms

(13.10.2)

\displaystyle N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}^{P}_{e}{\rm Ker}(g)\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P}_{-e}{\rm Ker}(g)

and

(13.10.3)

\displaystyle N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}^{P}_{e}{\rm Ker}(f)\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P}_{-e}{\rm Ker}(f)

are isomorphisms.
Here we denote
P^{\Lambda}
simply by
P
.
Consequently

(13.10.4)

\displaystyle N^{e}\colon{\rm gr}^{P}_{e}{\rm Im}(f)\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{P}_{-e}{\rm Im}(f)

is an isomorphism.
Because

\displaystyle{\rm gr}^{P}_{\pm e}({\rm Ker}(g)/{\rm Im}(f))

\displaystyle=(P_{\pm e}V\cap{\rm Ker}(g))/(P_{\pm e-1}V\cap{\rm Ker}(g)+P_{\pm e}V\cap{\rm Im}(f))

\displaystyle(=(P_{\pm e}V\cap{\rm Ker}(g))/(P_{\pm e-1}V\cap{\rm Ker}(g)+f(P_{\pm e}U))),

the surjectivity of the morphism (
13.10.1
) follows from that of
(
13.10.2
).
We can show the injectivity of the morphism (
13.10.1
) as follows.

By the surjectivity of the morphism (
13.10.4
) for various
e
’s,
we obtain the following inclusions

(13.10.5)

\displaystyle f(P_{-e}U)

\displaystyle\subset N^{e}(f(P_{e}U))+f(P_{-e-1}U)\subset N^{e}(f(P_{e}U))+N^{e+1}f(P_{-e-1}U)+f(P_{-e-2}U)

\displaystyle\subset\cdots\subset\sum_{k=0}^{m}N^{e+k}f(P_{e+k}U)(=\sum_{k=0}^{m}g(N^{e+k}P_{e+k}U))\subset N^{e}(f(P_{e}U))

for some
m>>0
because the filtration
P
on
U
is finite
(and hence
N\colon U\longrightarrow U
is nilpotent).
By Mitchell’s embedding theorem
we may assume that
f
and
g
are morphisms of filtered
R
-modules,
where
R
is a ring with unit element.
Assume that, for
x\in P_{e}V\cap{\rm Ker}(g)
,

N^{e}(x)\subset P_{-e-1}V\cap{\rm Ker}(g)+f(P_{-e}U).

By the inclusion (
13.10.5
)
there exists an element
y\in f(P_{e}U)
such that
N^{e}(x-y)\in P_{-e-1}V\cap{\rm Ker}(g)
.
By the injectivity of (
13.10.2
),

x-y\in P_{e-1}{\rm Ker}(g)
and hence

x\in f(P_{e}U)+P_{e-1}{\rm Ker}(g)
.
This shows that the injectivity of (
13.10.1
).
∎

Remark 13.11
.

To prove the
\infty
-adic analogue of the conjecture (
13.5
) for the case of a projective
strict semistable family over the unit disk, Steenbrink and Zucker have used the
E_{2}
-degeneration
of the
\infty
-adic analogue of (
10.4.1
) modulo torsion in
[
SZ
, p. 527]
.
We have not used the
E_{2}
-degeneration of (
10.4.1
) modulo torsion;
we do not know whether it holds in general.
Instead we use the strict compatibility of
d_{r}^{-k,q+k}
for any
r\geq 1
in the proof of
(
13.9
).

Proposition 13.12
.

If
\dim\overset{\circ}{X}\leq 2
, then
(
13.7
)
is true.

Proof.

By the main result in
[
Nak3
]
or (
12.3
),
the
l
-adic weight spectral sequence
(
10.4.2
) for the case

D=\emptyset
degenerates at
E_{2}
.
The conjecture (
13.7
) for the case
q=1
has been proved by
Kajiwara-Achinger (
[
Kaj
, (3.1)]
or
[
Ac
, Theorem 3.6]
).
Hence the conjecture for the case
q=3
is also true
by the classical Poincaré duality and the description of the edge morphisms
of
E_{1}
-terms of (
12.3
) (cf. (
15.4.1
) below for the case
D=\emptyset
)
because
the induced morphism

\nu^{k}\colon E_{1}^{-k,q+k}\longrightarrow E_{1}^{k,q-k}(-k)
by
\nu\colon R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D)/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D)/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(-1)

is identified with the tensorization of the identity morphism with
\check{T}^{\otimes k}
.
The conjecture for the case
q=2
holds by the proof of
[
M
, (6.2.1)]
.
∎

Corollary 13.13
.

If
\dim\overset{\circ}{X}\leq 2
, then
(
13.5
)
is true.

Proof.

(
13.13
) follows from (
13.12
) and (
13.9
).
∎

Problem 13.14
.

Let
{\cal V}
be a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristics

(0,p)
with perfect residue field. Let
K
be the fraction field of
{\cal V}
.
Set
B=({\rm Spf}({\cal V}),{\cal V}^{*})
.
Let
S
be a
p
-adic formal family of log points over
B

such that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is a
{\cal V}/p
-scheme.
Let
(X,D)/S
be a proper SNCL scheme with a relative SNCD.
In
[
Nakk6
]
we have constructed the following spectral sequence

(13.14.1)

E_{1}^{-k,q+k}:=\bigoplus_{k^{\prime}\leq k}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k^{\prime},0\}}R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/K}({\cal O}_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/\overset{\circ}{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime},k-k^{\prime})}((\overset{\circ}{X},\overset{\circ}{D})/\overset{\circ}{S}))(-j-k)\Longrightarrow R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\otimes_{\cal V}K\quad(q\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

Assume that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is connected.
Let
q
be a nonnegative integer.
Is the rank of
P_{k}R^{q}f_{(X,D)/S*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\otimes_{\cal V}K

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

is equal to the rank of

P_{k}R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
?
(If one knows that the rank of

R^{q}f_{(X,D)/K*}({\cal O}_{(X,D)/S})\otimes_{\cal V}K
is equal to
that of

R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
, then
one can prove that this problem is affirmatively solved by the specialization argument.)

14
Strict semistable family

Let
{\cal V}
be a henselian discrete valuation ring with residue field
\kappa
.
Let
K
be the fraction field of
{\cal V}
.
Let
\overline{\cal V}
be the integral closure of
{\cal V}

in a separable closure
\overline{K}
of
K
.
Let
\overline{\kappa}
be the residue field of
\overline{\cal V}
.
Let
\pi
be a uniformizer of
{\cal V}
.
Let
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
be
a strict semistable family over
{\cal V}
.
Endow
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
with the canonical log structure and
let
{\cal X}
be the resulting log scheme.

Definition 14.1
.

Let
\overset{\circ}{\cal D}
be a closed subscheme of

\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
.
We call
\overset{\circ}{\cal D}
a
horizontal SNCD
on

\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
if
the following three conditions are satisfied:

(14.1.1):
\overset{\circ}{\cal D}
is an effective Cartier
divisor on
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
over
{\cal V}
.

(14.1.2):
\overset{\circ}{\cal D}
is a union of smooth divisors

\{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in M}
on
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}/{\cal V}
.

(14.1.3): There exists an étale morphism

\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{\longrightarrow}{\rm Spec}({\cal V}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}]/(x_{0}\cdots x_{a-1}-\pi))

zariski locally on
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}

such that

\overset{\circ}{\cal D}
is locally the fiber product of
the following diagram
(a+b\leq d+1)
:

(14.1.4)

\begin{CD}\overset{\circ}{\cal X}\\
@V{}V{}V\\
{\rm Spec}({\cal V}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}]/(\prod_{i=0}^{a-1}x_{i}-\pi,\prod_{i=a}^{a+b-1}x_{i}))@>{\subset}>{}>{\rm Spec}({\cal V}[x_{0},\ldots,x_{d}]/(\prod_{i=0}^{a-1}x_{i}-\pi))\end{CD}

and such that

\{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in M}=\{\{x_{i}=0\}_{i=a}^{a+b-1}\}
in the
diagram (
14.1.4
).
Here the left hand
\{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\mu}\}_{\mu\in M}
of this equality
means the set of non-empty
{\cal D}_{\mu}
’s in the local situation above.

Set
{\cal U}:=\overset{\circ}{\cal X}\setminus\overset{\circ}{\cal D}
and

(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}},\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}):=(\overset{\circ}{\cal X},\overset{\circ}{\cal D})\otimes_{\overline{\cal V}}\overline{\kappa}
.
Denote
\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}/{\rm Spec}~\overline{\kappa})
,

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}/{\rm Spec}~\overline{\kappa})
and

\varpi^{(k),(k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}/{\rm Spec}~\overline{\kappa})

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
simply by

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}/\overline{\kappa})
,

\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}/\overline{\kappa})
and

\varpi^{(k),(k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}((\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}},\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}})/\overline{\kappa})
,
respectively.

Set
\eta:={\rm Spec}(K)
and

\overline{\eta}:={\rm Spec}(\overline{K})
.
Set

,

{\cal D}_{{\eta}}:={\cal D}\otimes_{\cal V}{{K}}

and

.
Set

,

.
In this section we construct two spectral sequences
converging to

(q,n\in{\mathbb{N}})
.
These spectral sequences turn out to be

(

) and (

), respectively.

Set
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}:=\overset{\circ}{\cal X}\otimes_{\cal V}\overline{\cal V}
and

\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}}:=\overset{\circ}{\cal X}\otimes_{\cal V}\overline{K}
.

As in §
9
we can define the orientation sheaves
