.

Proposition 14.2
.

The image of the complex

(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}},{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}})/\overline{\cal V}),P^{{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}},P)\in{\rm C}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{2}((\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}})_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

in
{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{2}((\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}})_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

is independent of the choice of

K^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V})
,
T
and

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\iota}}{}^{*}((M^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V}),R))

up to canonical isomorphisms.
It is an object of

{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{2}_{\rm ctf}((\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}})_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
.
The family

\{(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}},{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}})/\overline{\cal V}),P^{{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}},P)\}_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
defines an object of

{\rm D}^{\rm b}{\rm F}^{2}_{\rm ctf}((\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}})_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of (
9.19
).
∎

Lemma 14.3
.

The following hold
:

(1)
There exists an isomorphism

(14.3.1)

{\rm gr}_{k}^{P_{{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}}}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}},{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}})/\overline{\cal V})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}c^{(k)}_{*}(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal D}^{(k)}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V})(-k)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}/\overline{\kappa}))\{-k\}\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

This isomorphism is compatible with
n
’s.

(2)
There exists an isomorphism

(14.3.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{P}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}},{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}})/\overline{\cal V})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus^{k}_{k^{\prime}=-\infty}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k^{\prime},0\}}a^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1),(k-k^{\prime})}_{*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1)}\cap\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle\quad\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1),(k-k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}((\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}},\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}})/\overline{\kappa}))(-j-k)[-2j-k]\quad(k\in{\mathbb{Z}}).

This isomorphism is compatible with
n
’s.

Proof.

The proof of (1) and
(2) is the same as that of (
10.3
).
∎

Next we generalize Rapoport-Zink’s

l
-adic weight spectral sequence
(see (
14.8
) below).
For the generalization, we need some lemmas.

Lemma 14.4
.

Let
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}
be a
closed subscheme of
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}
is strictly semistable over
{\cal V}
.
Set
{\cal X}^{\prime}:=\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}\times_{\overset{\circ}{\cal X}}{\cal X}
.
Set
\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}{}^{\prime}:=\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}\otimes_{\overline{\cal V}}\overline{\kappa}
.
Let
R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

and
R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}{}^{\prime}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

be the classical nearby cycle sheaf of
{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}
on

\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}

and
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}
, respectively.
Let

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\iota}}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}},\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}{}^{\prime}}\colon\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}{}^{\prime}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}

be the closed immersion.
Then

(14.4.1)

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\iota}}{}^{*}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}},\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}{}^{\prime}}R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})=R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}{}^{\prime}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}).

Proof.

Let

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\iota}}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}},\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}

be the closed immersion.
Let
{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}
and
{\cal X}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}

be log schemes whose underlying schemes are

\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}
and

\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}
and whose log structures
are the inductive limits of canonical log structures.
Let
\epsilon_{{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}}\colon{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}

and

\epsilon_{{\cal X}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}}\colon{\cal X}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}

be the morphisms forgetting the log structures.
Let

\overline{j}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}

and

\overline{j}{}^{\prime}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}_{\overline{\eta}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}{\cal X}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}

be the open immersions of log schemes.
Then, by
[
FK
, (3.1)]
(see also
[
Il3
, (7.4), (7.5)]
),

R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})=R\epsilon_{{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
.
and
R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}{}^{\prime}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})=R\epsilon_{{\cal X}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
.
By the log proper base change theorem
(
[
Nak1
, (5.1)]
) for the following commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\cal X}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}@>{\subset}>{}>{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}\\
@V{\epsilon_{{\cal X}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}}}V{}V@V{}V{\epsilon_{{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}}}V\\
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}@>{\subset}>{}>\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}\end{CD}

we obtain

(14.4.2)

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\iota}}{}^{*}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}},\overset{\circ}{\cal X}{}^{\prime}_{\overline{\cal V}}}R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})=R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}{}^{\prime}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}).

Hence

\overset{\circ}{\overline{\iota}}{}^{*}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}},\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}{}^{\prime}}R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})=R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}{}^{\prime}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
.
∎

The following is the dual of
[
NS
, (2.7.2)]
:

Lemma 14.5
.

Let

f\colon({\cal T},{\cal A})\longrightarrow({\cal T}^{\prime},{\cal A}^{\prime})

be a morphism of ringed topoi.
Then, for an object
E^{\prime}{}^{\bullet}
in

D^{-}({\cal A}^{\prime})
,
there exists a canonical morphism

(14.5.1)

Lf^{*}((E^{\bullet},\tau))\longrightarrow(Lf^{*}(E^{\bullet}),\tau)

in
{\rm D}^{-}{\rm F}({\cal A}^{\prime}).

Proof.

By the adjunction
there exits a natural morphism

(14.5.2)

(E^{\bullet},\tau)\longrightarrow(Rf_{*}Lf^{*}(E^{\bullet}),\tau).

By
[
NS
, (2.7.2)]
we have the following natural morphism

(14.5.3)

(Rf_{*}Lf^{*}(E^{\bullet}),\tau)\longrightarrow Rf_{*}((Lf^{*}(E^{\bullet}),\tau)).

Hence we have the following composite morphism

(14.5.4)

(E^{\bullet},\tau)\longrightarrow Rf_{*}((Lf^{*}(E^{\bullet}),\tau)).

By applying the adjunction formula
[
NS
, (1.2.2)]
or (
5.2
)
for (
14.5.4
),
we obtain the morphism (
14.5.1
).
∎

Consider the following cartesian diagram of
fs log schemes:

(14.5.5)

\begin{CD}Y@>{q}>{}>Z\\
@V{p}V{}V@V{}V{\alpha}V\\
W@>{\beta}>{}>V.\end{CD}

Assume that
R\alpha_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

and
R\beta_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
are bounded above.
Then we can construct
the following “filtered Künneth morphism”

(14.5.6)

\cup\colon R\beta_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}(R\alpha_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau)\longrightarrow R\beta_{*}((Rp_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau)).

Indeed, by the adjunction formula
[
NS
, (1.2.2)]
,
we have only to construct a morphism

L\beta^{*}R\beta_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}L\beta^{*}(R\alpha_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau)\longrightarrow(Rp_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau).

The following composite morphism

\displaystyle L\beta^{*}R\beta_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}L\beta^{*}(R\alpha_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}L\beta^{*}((R\alpha_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau))

\displaystyle=L\beta^{*}((R\alpha_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau))\longrightarrow(L\beta^{*}R\alpha_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau)\longrightarrow(Rp_{*}Lq^{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau)=(Rp_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau)

is a desired morphism.
Here we have used the morphism (
14.5.1
).

Let
({\cal X},\overset{\circ}{\cal D})
be as in
(
14.1
).
Consider the following cartesian diagrams

(14.5.7)

\begin{CD}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}},\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}})@>{\subset}>{}>(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}},\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}})@<{\supset}<{}<({\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}})\\
@V{\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}}}V{}V@V{\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}}}V{}V@V{\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}}}V{}V\\
\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}@>{\overset{\circ}{\overline{\iota}}}>{}>\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}@<{\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}}<{}<{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},\end{CD}

where the vertical morphisms are morphisms forgetting log structures
obtained by
\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}
,
\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}

and
\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}
.
Then we have the following Künneth morphism

(14.5.8)

\cup\colon R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}R\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\longrightarrow R(\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}})_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}).

To prove that the morphism
(
14.5.8
) is an isomorphism,
we cannot use the log Künneth formula
(
[
Nak1
, (6.1)]
) for the right cartesian diagram in (
14.5.7
)
since
\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}
is not proper.
However we can prove the following:

Lemma 14.6
.

The filtered Künneth morphism

(
14.5.6
)

(14.6.1)

\cup\colon R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}(R\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau)\longrightarrow R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}((R\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau))

for the right cartesian diagram of
(
14.5.7
)

is an isomorphism.
In particular the Künneth morphism
(
14.5.8
)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By abuse of notation, we denote

R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
by
a representative of
R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
.
Because
R^{k}\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

(k\in{\mathbb{Z}})
is a flat
{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}
-module,
we have only to prove that the following morphism

(14.6.2)

\displaystyle{\rm gr}_{k}^{\tau}(\cup)[k]\colon R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}R^{k}\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\longrightarrow{\rm gr}_{k}^{\tau}R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}((R\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau))[k]

\displaystyle=R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\rm gr}_{k}^{\tau}(R\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}),\tau)[k])=R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}(R^{k}\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}))

is an isomorphism.
Let
d^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal D}{}^{(k)}_{\overline{\cal V}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}

and

e^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal D}{}^{(k)}_{\overline{\eta}}\longrightarrow\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})

be the natural morphisms.
Then the source of the morphism (
14.6.2
)
is

R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}d^{(k)}_{*}(\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V}))(-k)
,
while the target of the morphism (
14.6.2
)
is
R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}e^{(k)}_{*}(\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}/{\overline{K}})))(-k)
.
Fix a total order on the irreducible components of

{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}
.
Then the source and the target are isomorphic to

R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}d^{(k)}_{*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}})(-k)

and

R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}(e^{(k)}_{*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}}))(-k)
,
respectively.
Let
\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}{}^{(k)}\colon\overset{\circ}{\cal D}{}^{(k)}_{\overline{\eta}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{\cal D}{}^{(k)}_{\overline{\cal V}}

be the natural open immersion.
Then the source is equal to

d^{(k)}_{*}R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}{}^{(k)}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})(-k)

by (
14.4.2
).
Since
d^{(k)}\circ\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}{}^{(k)}=\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}\circ e^{(k)}
,
the target is
d^{(k)}_{*}R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}{}^{(k)}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})(-k)
.
Hence the source and the target of (
14.6.2
)
are the same.
∎

Lemma 14.7
.

(14.7.1)

H^{q}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et},A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}R\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}))=H^{q}({\cal U}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\quad(q,n\in{\mathbb{N}}).

Proof.

Because (
14.1.14
) is an isomorphism,
we have the following equality:

(14.7.2)

\displaystyle H^{q}(\overset{\circ}{X},A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}R\overset{\circ}{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}))=H^{q}(\overset{\circ}{X},R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}\overset{\circ}{\overline{\iota}}{}^{*}R{\epsilon}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{{\cal V}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}))

\displaystyle=H^{q}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{{\cal V}}},R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\otimes^{L}_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}R\epsilon_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{{\cal V}}}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}))=H^{q}_{\rm ket}(({\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}),{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

\displaystyle=H^{q}_{\rm et}({\cal U}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}).

Here the third equality is obtained by
the isomorphism (
14.5.8
)
and the last equality follows
from Gabber’s purity (
[
Fu2
, §8, third Consequence]
)
(cf.
[
FK
]
,
[
Il3
, (7.5)]
).
∎

The following is a generalization of
Rapoport-Zink’s
l
-adic weight spectral sequence
(see also (
14.10
) below):

Theorem 14.8
.

There exists the following spectral sequences
:

(14.8.1)

E_{1}^{-k,q+k}=H^{q-k}_{\rm et}({\cal D}^{(k)}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}/\overline{K}))(-k)\Longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm et}({\cal U}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}).

(14.8.2)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-k,q+k}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus^{k}_{k^{\prime}=-\infty}\bigoplus_{j\geq\max\{-k^{\prime},0\}}H^{q-2j-k}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1)}\cap\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1),(k-k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}((\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}},\overset{\circ}{\overline{D}})/\overline{\kappa}))(-j-k)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm et}({\cal U}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}).

Proof.

(
14.8
) immediately
follows from (
14.3
), (
14.1.13
) and (
14.7.1
).
∎

Proposition 14.9
.

There exists a natural isomorphism

(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}},\overset{\circ}{{\cal D}}_{\overline{\cal V}})/\overline{\cal V}),P^{{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}},P))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((\overline{X},\overline{D})/\overline{s}),P^{\overline{D}},P).

Proof.

(
14.9
) immediately follows from

[
Nak3
, (1.9)]
, which tells us that
there exists a natural isomorphism

(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V}),P^{{\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X_{\overline{\kappa}}/\overline{s}),P^{X_{\overline{\kappa}}}).

∎

Corollary 14.10
.

The spectral sequences

(
14.8.1
)
and
(
14.8.2
)

are canonically isomorphic to

(
10.4.1
)
and
(
10.4.1
)
,
respectively.

Proof.

By
[
Nakk2
, (5.6)]
the following diagram is commutative:

(14.10.1)

\begin{CD}R\epsilon_{\overline{X}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})@>{\sim}>{}>s((K^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(\overline{X}/\overline{s}),d)\overset{T-1}{\longrightarrow}(K^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(\overline{X}/\overline{s}),-d))\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
\overset{\circ}{\overline{i}}{}^{*}R\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})@>{\sim}>{}>s((K^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V}),d)\overset{T-1}{\longrightarrow}(K^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V}),-d)).\end{CD}

By
[
Nakk2
, (5.7)]
the induced morphism

\overset{\circ}{i}{}^{*}R^{r}\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\longrightarrow R^{r}\epsilon_{\overline{X}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
by the
left vertical isomorphism in
(
14.10.1
) fits
into the following commutative diagram:

(14.10.2)

\begin{CD}\bigwedge^{r}(M_{\overline{X}}^{\rm gp}/{\cal O}_{\overline{X}}^{*})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}(-r)@>{\underset{\sim}{(\ref{eqn:tkx})}}>{}>R^{r}\epsilon_{\overline{X}*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})\\
\Big\|@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}}{}^{(r)}}(-r)@>{\underset{\sim}{(\ref{eqn:gap})}}>{}>\overset{\circ}{\overline{i}}{}^{*}R^{r}\overset{\circ}{\overline{j}}_{*}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}).\end{CD}

(
14.10
) immediately follows from
(
14.9
),
∎

Theorem 14.11
.

Let
S
be a family of log points.
Assume that the log structure of
S
is constant and that
\overset{\circ}{S}
is connected.
Let
X/S
be a proper SNCL scheme and let
D
be a relative SNCD on
X/S
.
For a point
\overset{\circ}{s}\in\overset{\circ}{S}
, denote by

s
the log scheme whose underlying scheme is
\overset{\circ}{s}

and whose log structure is the pull-back of
the log structure of
S
.
Let
X(s)
and
\overset{\circ}{D}(\overset{\circ}{s})
be the fibers of

X
and
\overset{\circ}{D}
at
s
and
\overset{\circ}{s}
, respectively.
Assume that, for a point
\overset{\circ}{s}\in\overset{\circ}{S}
,
there exist a henselian discrete valuation ring

{\cal V}(\overset{\circ}{s})
with residue field

\kappa(\overset{\circ}{s})
and a proper strict semistable family

{\cal X}(s)
with canonical log structure and with
a horizontal relative SNCD
\overset{\circ}{\cal D}(\overset{\circ}{s})
over

{\cal V}(\overset{\circ}{s})
such that

({\cal X}(s),\overset{\circ}{\cal D}(\overset{\circ}{s}))\times_{({\rm Spec}({\cal V}(\overset{\circ}{s})),{\cal V}(\overset{\circ}{s})\setminus\{0\})}({\rm Spec}(\kappa(\overset{\circ}{s})),{\mathbb{N}}\oplus\kappa(\overset{\circ}{s})^{*})=(X(s),\overset{\circ}{D}(\overset{\circ}{s}))
.
Then the spectral sequence
(
10.4.1
)

degenerates at
E_{2}
modulo torsion.

Proof.

Because the
E_{r}
-terms
(r\geq 1)
are smooth
on
\overset{\circ}{S}
(the proof of (
12.4
)) and
because
\overset{\circ}{S}
is connected,
it suffices to prove the
E_{2}
-degeneration for
(X(s),D(s))/s
.
Hence we may assume that
S=s
and
we omit to write

s
and
\overset{\circ}{s}
in the notations
X(s)
,

\overset{\circ}{D}(\overset{\circ}{s})
,
{\cal V}(\overset{\circ}{s})
,

\kappa(\overset{\circ}{s})
,
{\cal X}(s)
and

\overset{\circ}{\cal D}(\overset{\circ}{s})
.
Thus we may assume that we
are given a proper strict semistable family
{\cal X}

with canonical log structure and with
a horizontal SNCD
\overset{\circ}{\cal D}

over a henselian discrete valuation ring
{\rm Spec}({\cal V})
with
separably closed residue field
\kappa
such that
the log special fiber obtained by
({\cal X},{\cal D})
is
(X,D)
.
Let
\eta
be the generic point of
{\rm Spec}\,{\cal V}

and
\overline{\eta}
the geometric generic point of
{\rm Spec}({\cal V})
.
Let
G
be the absolute Galois group of
K
.
Let
R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
be
the classical nearby cycle
sheaf in
D^{+}(\overset{\circ}{\overline{X}},G,{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
.
Then

K^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/\overline{s})=R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

by the latter part of the proof of
[
Nak3
, (1.9)]
.
We have the following commutative diagram

(14.11.1)

\begin{CD}A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V})@>{\sim}>{}>A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})\\
@A{\simeq}A{}A@A{}A{\simeq}A\\
R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})@>{\sim}>{}>K^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})\end{CD}

(see [loc. cit.], though the boundary morphisms of our complexes

A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V})
and

A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})

are different from those of the corresponding complexes in [loc. cit.]).
Hence we have a canonical isomorphism

A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal X}_{\overline{\cal V}}/\overline{\cal V})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}\overset{\circ}{{\overline{\iota}}}{}^{*}(M^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{\cal D}/\overset{\circ}{S}),R)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/\overline{s}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}})

in
D^{+}(\overset{\circ}{X}_{\rm et},{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})
.
Let
R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}_{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})

be the classical nearby cycle sheaf of
{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}
on

\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}
.
By (
14.4.1
)
we obtain

(14.11.2)

\displaystyle s(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}({\cal X}_{\overline{{\cal V}}}/\overline{\cal V})\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}{\rm gr}^{R}_{k}M^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{{\cal V}}}/\overline{{\cal V}}))=c^{(k)}_{*}(R\overset{\circ}{\Psi}_{\overset{\circ}{{\cal D}_{\overline{\cal V}}}}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})(-k)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{D}_{\overline{{\cal V}}}/\overline{{\cal V}}))[-k]

as in the proof of (
10.3
) (1).
Hence the
E_{1}
-term
E_{1}^{-k,q+k}
of
(
10.4.1
) is isomorphic to

H^{q-k}_{\rm et}({\cal D}^{(k)}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})(-k)
.

First consider the case where
\eta
is of characteristic
p>0
.
As in the proof of
(
12.3
), the specialization argument and
the purity of the Frobenius
show the degeneration at
E_{2}
modulo torsion.

Next consider the case where
\eta
is of characteristic
0
.
In this case, the Lefschetz principle, the comparison theorem between
the étale cohomology and the Betti cohomology and the Hodge theory
tell us the degeneration at
E_{2}
.
∎

Theorem 14.12
.

Let the notations be as in
(
14.8
)
.
Assume that
{\cal V}
is of characteristic
p>0
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{\cal X}
is proper over
{\cal V}
.
Then there exists a monodromy filtration

M
on
H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})(=H^{q}({\cal U}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}))

(q\in{\mathbb{N}})

relative to
P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
. The relative monodromy filtration

M
is equal to
P
.

Proof.

By the proof of (
13.9
) it suffices to prove that
(
13.7
) for
D^{(k)}

(k\in{\mathbb{N}})
is true ((
13.9.1
)).
This follows from
[
It1
, (6.1)]
.
∎

Corollary 14.13
.

Let the notations be as in
§
12
.
Assume that
\overset{\circ}{X}
is proper over
\overset{\circ}{S}
.
If, for each connected component
S^{\prime}
of
S
, there exists an exact closed point
s\in S^{\prime}

such that the fiber
(X_{s},D_{s})/s
of
(X,D)/S
at
s
is the log special fiber
of a proper strict semistable family over a henselian discrete valuation ring
of equal characteristic, then there exists a relative monodromy filtration on

R^{q}f_{(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}*}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
with respect to
P^{D}
and
it is equal to
P
.

Proof.

(
14.13
) follows from the proof of (
12.4
) and (
14.12
).
∎

Corollary 14.14
.

Let the notations be as in
(
11.3
)
.
Assume that
S
and
S^{\prime}
are log points.
Assume that

g^{*}\colon H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{X}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm ket}((\overline{Y}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

has a section of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
-vector spaces
which are strictly compatible with respect to

P^{\overline{D}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
and
P^{\overline{E}_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}}
, and two
P
’s on

H^{q}_{\rm ket}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

and
H^{q}_{\rm ket}((Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},E),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
.
Assume also that this section is compatible with

N
’s on
H^{q}_{\rm ket}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\longrightarrow H^{q}_{\rm ket}((Y_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},E_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
.
Furthermore, assume that
(Y,E)/T

is a special fiber of proper strict semistable family with a horizontal
SNCD over a henselian discrete valuation ring of
characteristic
p>0
.
Then there exists a monodromy filtration

M
on
H^{q}_{\rm ket}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

relative to
P^{\overset{\circ}{D}}
.
The relative monodromy filtration
M
is equal to
P
.

Proof.

(
14.14
) immediately follows from
(
14.12
) and (
11.3
).
∎

Remark 14.15
.

(1) Let the notations be as in the introduction.
In some cases in mixed characteristics,
in
[
Fu1
]
K. Fujiwara has raised a strategy
for the conjecture (
1.0.2
):
if
X
is the log special fiber of
a log proper strict semistable family
{\cal X}

over
S
in the case where
S
is
of mixed characteristics and, also,
the special fiber of a log proper strict semistable family
{\cal X}^{\prime}

over the spectrum of a henselian discrete valuation ring of
equal characteristic with canonical log structure,
then the monodromy-weight conjecture
for
H^{q}(\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}},{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
follows from (
1.0.4
).

If
(X,D)
comes from a
proper semistable family
({\cal X},{\cal D})
with a horizontal SNCD over
a henselian discrete valuation ring of
mixed characteristics and, also, a proper semistable family
with a horizontal SNCD over a henselian discrete valuation ring of
equal characteristic, then
the relative monodromy filtration on

H^{q}((\overset{\circ}{\cal X}_{\overline{\eta}}\setminus\overset{\circ}{\cal D}_{\overline{\eta}}),{\mathbb{Q}}_{l})

relative to the filtration
P^{\overset{\circ}{\cal D}}
exists by (
14.12
).

(2) In the workshop “Algebraic geometry 2000” at Azumino,
the author heard from T. Katsura that
F. Oort has raised the following problem:
for a proper smooth scheme
Z
over a perfect field,
is there an alternation
Z^{\prime}\longrightarrow Z
such
that
Z^{\prime}
is the special fiber of a proper smooth scheme
over a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristics?
One year later(=2001), N. Tsuzuki asked to me the following:
is there a surjective morphism
Z^{\prime}\longrightarrow Z
such
that
Z^{\prime}
is the special fiber of a proper smooth scheme
over a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristics?

One can ask the analogous problem
in the context of log geometry:
for a proper log smooth scheme
Z
over
the log point of a perfect field,
is there a surjective morphism
Z^{\prime}\longrightarrow Z
such
that
Z^{\prime}
is the special fiber of a proper log smooth scheme
over the spectrum of
a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristics
with canonical log structure.
Influenced by Fujiwara’s strategy in (1),
C. Nakayama kindly suggested to me in 2007 that,
replacing “mixed characteristics” by “equal characteristics”
has an application to the log
l
-adic monodromy-weight conjecture
(
13.7
).
However it seems to me that the analogous problem in the log case cannot
be solved affirmatively in general
because there exist log special fibers of rigid analytic analogues of non-Kähler
elliptic surfaces for which the monodromy filtrations on
H^{1}
’s and
the weight filtrations on them do not coincide (
[
Nakk2
, (6.5), (6.7)]
).

Appendix

15
Edge morphisms between
the
E_{1}
-terms of
l
-adic weight spectral sequences

In this section we prove that
the edge morphisms
d_{1}^{\bullet\bullet}

between the
E_{1}
-terms of
(
10.4.2
) are described by
Čech Gysin morphisms and
the induced Čech morphisms of pull-back morphisms
by closed immersions.
We also prove that
the edge morphisms
d_{1}^{\bullet\bullet}

between the
E_{1}
-terms of
(
10.4.1
) are described by Gysin morphisms.

For the time being, consider the case

\overset{\circ}{D}=\emptyset
.

Let
\overset{\circ}{X}:=\bigcup_{i\in I(\overset{\circ}{X})}\overset{\circ}{X}_{i}

be the union of smooth components of
\overset{\circ}{X}
over
\overset{\circ}{S}
,
where
I(\overset{\circ}{X})
is a set of indexes.
We fix a total order on
I(\overset{\circ}{X})
.
Let
k\geq 2
be an integer.
Set

I_{k}(\overset{\circ}{X}):=\{(i_{0},\ldots,i_{k-1})\in I(\overset{\circ}{X})^{k}~|~i_{m}<i_{m^{\prime}}~(0\leq m<m^{\prime}\leq k-1)\}

and
\underline{i}:=(i_{0},\ldots,i_{k-1})\in I_{k}(\overset{\circ}{X})
.
For an integer
0\leq m\leq k-1
,
set
\underline{i}_{m}:=(i_{0},\ldots,\hat{i}_{m},\ldots,i_{k-1})
.
Set

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}}:=\overset{\circ}{X}_{i_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{i_{k-1}}

and
\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}_{m}}:=\overset{\circ}{X}_{i_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\hat{\overset{\circ}{X}}_{i_{m}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{X}_{i_{k-1}}
.
Let
\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}_{m}}

be the natural closed immersion and let

G_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}\colon\iota_{\underline{i}*}^{\underline{i}_{m}}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}(-1))_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}}}\{-1\}\longrightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}_{m}}}[1]

be the Gysin morphism of the closed immersion

\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}
.

Set

\varpi_{\underline{i}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}):=\varpi_{i_{0}\cdots i_{k-1}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

and

\varpi_{\underline{i}_{m}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}):=\varpi_{i_{0}\cdots\widehat{i}_{m}\cdots i_{k-1}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}).

The exact closed immersion

\iota^{\underline{i}_{m}}_{\underline{i}}\colon X_{\underline{i}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}X_{\underline{i}_{m}}

induces a morphism

(15.0.1)

(-1)^{m}\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}*}\colon({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}_{m}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{i}_{m}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})\longrightarrow\iota_{\underline{i}*}^{\underline{i}_{m}}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{i}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

defined by

x\otimes(i_{0}\cdots\widehat{i}_{m}\cdots i_{k-1})\longmapsto(-1)^{m}\iota_{\underline{i}{\rm et}}^{\underline{i}_{m}*}(x)\otimes(i_{0}\cdots i_{k-1})
.
Let
a_{\underline{i}_{m}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}_{m}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{X}

be the natural closed immersion.
Set

(15.0.2)

\iota^{(k-1)*}:=\sum_{\{(i_{0},i_{1},\ldots,i_{k-1})~|~i_{m}\not=i_{m^{\prime}}~(0\leq m\not=m^{\prime}\leq k-1)\}}\sum_{m=0}^{k-1}a_{\underline{i}_{m}*}\circ((-1)^{m}\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}*})\colon

a_{*}^{(k-1)}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k-1)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\longrightarrow a_{*}^{(k)}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})).

Set

L^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X):={\rm MF}_{l^{n}}(T-1)

((
9.7.5
)).

In
[
Nakk2
]
we have proved the following two lemmas:

Lemma 15.1

(
[
Nakk2
, (5.1)]
).

Let the notations be as above.
Then the following diagram

(15.1.1)

\begin{CD}{\cal H}^{k}(L^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X))(k)@>{\theta}>{}>{\cal H}^{k+1}(L^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X))(k+1)\\
@V{{\rm(\ref{eqn:tkx})}}V{\simeq}V@V{{\rm(\ref{eqn:tkx})}}V{\simeq}V\\
a^{(k)}_{*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))@>{\iota^{(k)*}}>{}>a^{(k+1)}_{*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k+1)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k+1)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\end{CD}

is commutative.

We fix an isomorphism

(15.1.2)

\varpi_{i_{m}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{i}_{m}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\varpi_{\underline{i}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

by the following morphism

(i_{m})\otimes(i_{0}\cdots\widehat{i}_{m}\cdots i_{k-1})\longmapsto(-1)^{m}(i_{0}\cdots i_{k-1}).

In (
15.1.2
) we have omitted to
write the direct images of the closed immersions

X_{\underline{i}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}X_{\underline{i}_{m}}

and
\iota^{\underline{i}_{m}}_{\underline{i}}
.
We identify

\varpi_{i_{m}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{i}_{m}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})
with

\varpi_{\underline{i}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

by this isomorphism.
We also have the following composite morphism

(15.1.3)

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}G_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}

\displaystyle\colon\iota^{\underline{i}_{m}}_{\underline{i}*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{i}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\iota^{\underline{i}_{m}}_{\underline{i}*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{i_{m}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{i}_{m}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

\displaystyle=\iota^{\underline{i}_{m}}_{\underline{i}*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{i}_{m}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})

\displaystyle\overset{G_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}\otimes 1}{\longrightarrow}({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}_{m}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{\underline{i}_{m}{\rm et}}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S})[1]\{1\}

defined by

(15.1.4)

{\rm``}x\otimes(i_{0}\cdots i_{k-1})\longmapsto(-1)^{m}G_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}(x)\otimes(i_{0}\cdots\widehat{i}_{m}\cdots i_{k-1}){\rm"}.

Set

G^{(k)}:=\sum_{\underline{i}\in I_{k}(\overset{\circ}{X})}\sum_{m=0}^{r}(-1)^{m}G_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}\colon a^{(k)}_{*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}}(-1)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))

\longrightarrow a^{(k-1)}_{*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k-1)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))[1]\{1\}.

Lemma 15.2

(
[
Nakk2
, (5.4)]
).

Let the notations and the assumptions be as in

(
15.1
)
.
Let

d\colon{\cal H}^{k}(L^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X))\{-k\}\longrightarrow{\cal H}^{k-1}(L^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X))\{-(k-1)\}[1]

be the boundary morphism
of the following triangle

{\rm gr}^{\tau}_{k-1}L^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X)\longrightarrow(\tau_{k}/\tau_{k-2})(L^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X))\longrightarrow{\rm gr}^{\tau}_{k}L^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X)\overset{+1}{\longrightarrow}

by using the Convention
(4)
.
Then the following diagram

(15.2.1)

\begin{CD}{\cal H}^{k}(L^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X))\{-k\}@>{d}>{}>\\
@V{{\rm(\ref{eqn:tkx})}}V{\simeq}V\\
a^{(k)}_{*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k)}}(-k)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\{-k\}@>{-G^{(k)}}>{}>\\
{\cal H}^{k-1}(L^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X))\{-(k-1)\}[1]\\
@V{{\rm(\ref{eqn:tkx})}}V{\simeq}V\\
a^{(k-1)}_{*}(({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(k-1)}}(-(k-1))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-1)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))\{-(k-1)\}[1]\end{CD}

is commutative.

By (
15.1
) and (
15.2
) we obtain the following:

Corollary 15.3

(
cf.
[
Nakk2
, (5.5)]
).

The edge morphism

d^{-k,q+k}_{1}\colon E_{1,l}^{-k,q+k}\longrightarrow E_{1,l}^{-k+1,q+k}

of the spectral sequence of
(
10.4.2
)

for the case
\overset{\circ}{D}=\emptyset
is
identified with the following morphism
:

(15.3.1)

\sum_{j\geq{\rm max}\{-k,0\}}(G^{(k)}+\iota^{(k)*}).

Next consider the general case where the horizontal SNCD

\overset{\circ}{D}
is not necessarily empty.
Let
\overset{\circ}{D}=\bigcup_{i\in I(\overset{\circ}{D})}\overset{\circ}{D}_{i}

be a union of smooth divisors,
where
I(\overset{\circ}{D})
is a set of indexes.
Fix a total order on
I(\overset{\circ}{D})
.

Let
k
be a positive integer.
Set

I_{k}(\overset{\circ}{D}):=\{(i_{0},\ldots,i_{k-1})\in I(\overset{\circ}{D})^{k}~|~i_{m}<i_{m^{\prime}}~(0\leq m<m^{\prime}\leq k-1)\}

and
\underline{i}:=(i_{0},\ldots,i_{k-1})
.
For an integer
0\leq m\leq k-1
,
set
\underline{i}_{m}:=(i_{0},\ldots,\hat{i}_{m},\ldots,i_{k-1})
.
Set

\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{i}}:=\overset{\circ}{D}_{i_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{i_{k-1}}

and
\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{i}_{m}}:=\overset{\circ}{D}_{i_{0}}\cap\cdots\cap\hat{\overset{\circ}{D}}_{i_{m}}\cap\cdots\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{i_{k-1}}
.

For a nonnegative integer
e
,
let
\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(e)}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(e)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{i}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(e)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{i}_{m}}

be the restriction of
\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}
to

\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(e)}
and let

G_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}\colon({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}(-1))_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(e)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{i}}}\{-1\}\longrightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(e)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}_{\underline{i}_{m}}}[1]

be the Gysin morphism of the closed immersion

\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}|_{\overset{\circ}{X}_{\underline{i}}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(e)}}
.
For a nonnegative integer
e
,
let
\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}(\overset{\circ}{D})|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(e)}}\colon\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(e)}\cap D_{\underline{i}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(e)}\cap D_{\underline{i}_{m}}

be the natural closed immersion and let

(15.3.2)

G_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}(\overset{\circ}{D})\colon({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}(-1))_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(e)}\cap{D_{\underline{i}}}}\{-1\}\longrightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(e)}\cap D_{\underline{i}_{m}}}[1]

be the Gysin morphism of

\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}(\overset{\circ}{D})|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(e)}}
.

Let
k
and
q
be integers.
Consider the following three morphisms for

k^{\prime}\leq k
and
j\geq\max\{-k^{\prime},0\}
appearing
in the edge morphism

E^{-k,q+k}_{1}\longrightarrow E^{-(k-1),q+k}_{1}
of
the spectral sequence (
10.4.2
):

\displaystyle G^{(k),(k^{\prime})}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\underline{i}\in I_{2j+k^{\prime}}(\overset{\circ}{X})}\sum_{m=0}^{2j+k^{\prime}}(-1)^{m}G_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}\colon R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/\overset{\circ}{S}{\rm et}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle(\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}})(-j-k)\longrightarrow

\displaystyle R^{q+1-2j-(k-1)}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime}-1)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/S{\rm et}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}(\varpi^{(2j+k-1^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k-1^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle\varpi^{(k-k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime}-1)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}})(-j-(k-1)),

\displaystyle\iota^{(k),(k^{\prime})*}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\underline{i}\in I_{2j+k^{\prime}}(\overset{\circ}{X})}\sum_{m=0}^{2j+k^{\prime}}(-1)^{m}\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}*}\colon R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/S{\rm et}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle(\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}})(-j-k)\longrightarrow

\displaystyle R^{q+1-2(j+1)-(k-1)}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/S{\rm et}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle(\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1)}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime}+1)}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}})(-j-k)

and

\displaystyle G^{(k),(k^{\prime})}(\overset{\circ}{D}):=\sum_{\underline{i}\in I_{k-k^{\prime}}(\overset{\circ}{D})}\sum_{m=0}^{k-k^{\prime}}(-1)^{m}G_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}(\overset{\circ}{D})\colon R^{q-2j-k}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}/S{\rm et}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle(\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{(k-k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime})}})(-j-k)\longrightarrow

\displaystyle R^{q+1-2j-(k-1)}f_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{((k-1)-k^{\prime})}/S{\rm et}*}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle(\varpi^{(2j+k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{X}/\overset{\circ}{S}))|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime}-1)}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\varpi^{((k-1)-k^{\prime})}_{\rm et}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S})|_{\overset{\circ}{X}{}^{(2j+k^{\prime})}\cap\overset{\circ}{D}{}^{(k-k^{\prime}-1)}})(-j-(k-1)).

By noting the sign arising from the Convention (4) and the definitions
(
9.12.2
) and (
9.16.6
), we obtain the following:

Corollary 15.4
.

The edge morphism

d^{-k,q+k}_{1}\colon E_{1,l}^{-k,q+k}\longrightarrow E_{1,l}^{-k+1,q+k}

of the spectral sequence
(
10.4.2
)
is
identified with the following morphism
:

(15.4.1)

\sum_{k^{\prime}\leq k}\sum_{j\geq{\rm max}\{-k^{\prime},0\}}\{G^{(k),(k^{\prime})}+\iota^{(k),(k^{\prime})*}+(-1)^{2j+k^{\prime}+1}G^{(k),(k^{\prime})}(\overset{\circ}{D})\}.

Proof.

Because
(A^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P)

is the single complex of the triple complex

(A^{\bullet\bullet\bullet}_{l^{n}}((X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}},D_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}})/S),P)=(A^{\bullet\bullet}_{l^{n}}(X_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}/S_{\frac{1}{l^{\infty}}}),P)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}}(M^{\bullet}_{l^{n}}(\overset{\circ}{D}/\overset{\circ}{S}),Q),

we obtain (
15.4
) by (
15.3
)
as in
[
Nakk1
, (10.1)]
.
∎

Proposition 15.5
.

Let

G^{(k)}(D)_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}\colon({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n}(-1))_{D_{\underline{i}}}\{-1\}\longrightarrow({\mathbb{Z}}/l^{n})_{D_{\underline{i}_{m}}}[1]

be the Gysin morphism
of the closed immersion

\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}(D)\colon D_{\underline{i}}\overset{\subset}{\longrightarrow}D_{\underline{i}_{m}}

obtained by
\iota_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}(\overset{\circ}{D})
.
Then the edge morphism
d_{1}^{-k,q+k}\colon E_{1}^{-k,q+k}\longrightarrow E_{1}^{-k+1,q+k}
of

(
10.4.1
)
is expressed as

\sum_{\underline{i}\in I_{k}(\overset{\circ}{D})}\sum(-1)^{m}G^{(k)}(D)_{\underline{i}}^{\underline{i}_{m}}
.

Proof.

This is obvious.
∎
